Almightie God ffather sonne and holie ghost three personnes
but one divine essence by his omnipotent power did in the
begining make all thing of nothing and prepared whatsoever
was in the world for the vse benifitt and comfort of mankynd:
as Beast, Trees, Plantes, herbes, ffrutes and spices which live
vppon the earthe, and springe out of the same, stones and minerall
which are digged and drawne out of the bowell of the earthe; ffowles
of sondrye kynd in the ayre, ffishes innumerable sortes in the
waters and also made him Lord over all his creatures &
gave him free election to take the best and leave the worst.
only the forbidden ffrute of Paradice excepted; And did
alsoe give him knowledge to know and vnderstand the
motions of the superior and celestiall bodies, that is to saye
of the Seaven Plannet. and the Twelve Signes, Soe as
philosophers and wise men have studied and endevored to provide
remedies for the sicknesses and infirmities of mens bodies
according to the observation of the heavens. Learned men affirme
that the naturall course of tyme dothe changethe fower tymes
in the yeare, whervppon they devide the yeare into fowre part
To witt the Spring, Sommer, harvest, & the winter: And in
eache of these quarters they foverne them selves as the
season of the yeare requirethe & as that variethe the change
they ther manner of dyett. ffor the meate that is good & holsome
in Winter may be hurtfull in Sommer; and that which is profi-
table in Sommer may be contagious in Winter. And for to
know the alterations of the tymes the better, these learned
doctors doe consider and observe the course of the sonne by
the Twelve Signes of the Zodiack, sayeinge that everie
Quarter of the Yeare endurethe Three monethes, and that the
sonne passethe by Three Signes, namelie in Pryme tyme
by Piscis, Aries & Taurus, which reigne and governe in ffebru-
arie, Marche, and Aprill, at which tyme the earthe is clothed
wth





wth greene herbes and grasse, trees doe then florishe wth
greene leaves, soe t it is a greate beautie, wher wth
the earthe is then adorned werit pleasant & delectable
for the eyes of man to behold And this quarter of e
yeare is of the nature of sanguine complexion, hott
& moyst In sommer e Some passethe by Gemini Cancer
& Leo, wch have their dominion in the Montthes of May
June & Julye, and then corne, Grasse, and ffrutes
of the earthe doe growe and waxe ripe. and this season
tis of the nature of the colerick complexion In Autumne
or hare oft the some passethe by Virgo, Libra & Scorpio
wch have their dominion in August, September & October
in wch tyme fflowers fade leaves fall and frutes are
gathered In Winter the some passethe By Sagitarius,
Capricorne & Aquaries wth governe in the monethes
of November, December, & Fanuarie & then the Earth
waxethe bare, Trees wthout Leaves and all becometh barre
of a melancholie complexion According to wch seasons
they saye that ther are fowre ages of mans Life to wett:
Juvenilis a'tas or youthe. virilis a'tas his perfect age or
stenght, stueltas his old age & a'tas decrepita his
decrepit or decayee age. all wch have a lieknes ovre
semblance vnto the ffowre seasons of the yeare, as
youthe may be liekned to the pryme fyme, that is
hott and moyste, and as the hearbes and trees doe
florishe and growe in the spring tyme soe dothe growe and
increase in bignes, stength, and beawtie vntill hecome
to the age of pp... yeares. After this he is liekned to
sommer wch is hott and drye, and then is man in hillfull
force and stringth and endurethe till he be  plvtie years old
Old age is compared to hurvest tyme wch is cold and  drye,
and then mans strength decayethe and he wapethe weake

least he want when he comethe to stoopinge age and his pte

he still declyning, comes to stoopeinge and docrepite
old age, wch may swell be compiared to the winter season
cold and mayst by reason of abundance of humors tt an.
cold and thorought desert of naturall heate at wch tyme

tt he hathe spared and Layed vpp nothing agaynst that tyme
then he remaynethe poore and maled as the earthe and trees
doe in the tyme of winter and this tyme of mans age
endure the vntill lebelpy or more in such men as have by
nature stronge constitutions By this wch hath bene sayd
may be knownt that he Spring or pryme tyme is hott
and muyst, of he nature of the ayre, and of sanguine complexion,
wch is freshe blod wch ingendrethe in the liver .
long lieke the ayre , and reignethe in May, September

& ffebruarie when the moone is in Cancer Scorpio &

Of these fower complexions everie man is made, composod

&





V6
and man hath alsoe fowre prime qualities in him to witt
moysture and drynes wch are contrarie, heate and cold, wch
are alsoe contrarie and cannot stand togither wthout a meanes
for moystnes bynedthe on the one side and drynes on the
other. moystnes is cause of everie thin substance and
drynes is the cause of everie thick substance, moystnes
is the cause of everie sweete tast and drynes of sower
tast and unsavorie. Hottnes is cause of redd coler and
large quantitie and on the contrarie whitnes is caused
by coldnes and smale quantitie. Now these fower pryme
qualities combyne and knitt togither doe yeald fower
kynd of elements viz. The Ayre wch is Hott and moyst
The ffyer wch is hott and drye, The Earthe cold and drye,
The water cold and moyste. The Ayre and the Earthe are
two contraries and cannot ioyne togither, but that the fyre
byndethe them in on one side and the water on the other,
And liekwise the fyer and water are two contraries that cannot
ioyne togither but as the Ayre byndethe them in on one side &c
and the Earthe on the other side. And soe the fowre Elem
are combyned togither. The Ayre and the fyre are swift
subtile, sharpe, comfortable and moveable, the water and
the earthe be corpulent and dull. The water is moueable
The Earthe is vnmoueable. And like as an Egg shell covereth
the thinn skin, and the skin coverethe the white and the white the
yolke and in the midest of the yolke is a little round hole,
even soe the fyer overgoethe the ayre ten times more then th
ayre overgoeth the Earthe In the hart of the earthe is the Center
of the world tht is to saye the middle poynte and in t myddle
poynt of the Center is a hole and directlie aboue that in
the ffirmamt is the ffyer of the starres, and aboue that is the
Christall heaven and waters of all Blisse devided amongst
the nyne ordres of Angell. Then is Heaven in the hiest pte
of all, and is most bright, and hathe most rome for it compasseth
about all the Element.





17
And as ther are fowre Element soe ther are fower complexions
accordinge and agreeing in all manner of qualities to the said
Elements viz. Sanguine, Colerique, Malencolique, flegmatique

The sanguine man hath his nature of the ayre and is hott and moyst
and hathe freshe blood growinge in his liver and he is of statare large
loving, ientill, hardie, pleasinge, seemelie redd of colure,abundant
in nature and may performe the workes of nature plenteouslie.

The Colerique man hathe his nature of the fyer, that is hott and drye
for Coler ingendrethe in the gall, and naturally he is leane slender
Covetus, irefull, hastie, backbitinge, brawling deceytfull, Subtill
of Venus but little he is able to pforme.

The fflgmatique man hath his nature of the water whch is cold &
moyst and fleame  engendrethe in the lunges, and he is naturally
disposed to be heavie, sloathefull, lumberinge, sleepeinge & ever
spittinge, dull and hard to conceyve pale visaged and hathe
little desire to Venerie, but it able to pforme muche.

The Melancholie man hath his nature of the earthe whch is cold and
drye and he is disposed to be lumpishe and heavie. malencholie ingen-
drethe in the mylte lieke to the dregg of blood. He is by nature
Covetous, malitious, envious, dreadfull froward & fastholdinge
he is of claye Color. And he delighteth not soe little in fleshlye
lust but as little he may performe he is soe drye and cold.

After the deluge and greate vniversall fflood of water whch drowned all the
world save Noe and his sonnes whch by the devine providence and power
of God were preserved in the arke whch he had made. Noe seatinge
him self vppon the land planted vynes for his comfort whch would
in noe wise bare any frute vntill he had taken the blood of ffowre
beastes and powred it at the rootes of the vynes whch made them to bear
plenteouslye: and that was wth the blood of an Ape, of a Lyon, of a
sheepe, and of a Swyne, and vnto this daye the operation of the
wyne accordethe to the nature of these beast, in men of  severall natur..

The





The sanguine man in takinge of his wyne resemblethe the Ape
for the more he drinckethe the merryer he is amongst companie
and he delightethe to weare highe colored clothe.

The Colericke man in takeinge his wyne resemblethe the Lyon
for when he is droncke then will he reddilie fight brawle and
chyde and he lovethe to weare black cloathe russett and graye.

The fflegmatique man takes his wyne of the Sheepe for when
he hathe druncke hard then he thinkes him self the wisest in
all the companie and he loves to weare greene Coler.

The Melancholie man in takeinge his wyne is swinishe for
when he is well whittled then he desirethe to sleepe, and he
delightes in black colers in his garment.

A Question wh the answere thervnto. viz.

Whether a man shall contynue all his lyfe in the same
complexion wherin he is borne or noe. Or whether
a mans complexion may be altered by misfortunes
& sicknes &tc.

ffor the disssolvinge of this Question it is to be vnderstood that a
man hathe two complexions a Naturall and an accidentall.

1The Naturall never changethe from the tyme of a mans
byrthe vntill he dye. and his complexion comethe of humors
that be in a man, that is to saye, of his bones, Senewes and of
his other members.

2The Accidentall complexion may be changed for it comethe of
the fower humors before specyfied & they may be changed in two
manners, Eyther naturallye or vnnaturallye.

1 Complexion may change naturallie fowre tymes in a mans lyfe
for when he is borne he is of sanguine Complexion hott and
moyst, of the nature of the Ayre, and disposition of ver. the
springe or pryme tyme, wch is in februarie, Marche &
Aprill the first quarter of the yeare. In wch complexion
he endurethe vntill he xxv. yeares old wthout alteration.

The





The second tyme complexion alterethe is when a man entrethe into the
Colericke Complexion whch is hott and drye of the nature of ffyre
and of the disposition of the Sommer whch is May June & July whch
is the second qter of the yeare and endurethe vntill a man
be xl years old before he change his complexion.

The third tyme Compexion alterethe is when a man entrethe into
melancholie that is cold and drye of the nature of Earthe and
disposition of Harvest viz. August September & October the third
Quarter of the yeare and this endurethe vntill a man be of the
age of Lvj. before he change Complexion.

The ffowerthe tyme that Complexion dothe alter in a man is when
he entrethe in to the flegmatique Complexion whch is a constitution
cold and moyst of the nature of water and dispostion of Winter.
whch is November, December & Jannuary whch is the ffowrethe
and last quarter of the yeare, and contynuethe all the reside
of a mans lyfe. And thus these Accidentall complexions doe
change naturallie wthout fallinge into sicknesses.

2  A mans complexion may vnnaturallie be changed and then
it causethe him to fall into a fevor or other sickness. And it
may be changed diverse and sondrye wayes. As in changing
of dyett in meate and drinke. or in changinge the place wher
in a man hathe bene brought vpp. alsoe in takinge meate
or drinke eyther to much or to little, eyther in muche sleepe,
or watchinge, studdie, Travell, rest, Sorrowe, ioye, anger, heate, cold,
Thirst, dronkennes, any of all these may alter and change a mans
complexion, for the excesse or superfluitie of any thinge maybe
hurtfull and contagious vnto man Wherfore moderation
is one of the best preservitves that can be for the healthe of
mans bodie.

That meates and drinckes will change the complexion of a mans
bodie into such nature and qualitie as they be of themselves is most
evident: ffor hott meates and hott drinkes drawe a man from a
cold Constitution to a hott, and on the contrarie cold meates and
drinke bringe a man from a hott temper to a cold, Soe liekewise
moyst meates drawe a man from a drye, and drye meates from a

moyst






20 a moyst complexion. Wherfore to observe a meane and a moderation
in these thing that a man shall eate or drinke is the best
rule that can be given in naturall Phisique for a man to
keepe his bodie in good healthe and temper.

Now as Almightie God hath given vnto man the knowledge
of the motion of the superior and celestiall bodyes as of
the vij Plannetes and the xij signes of t Zodiack &c.
Soe it is especyallie requisite and expedient for all practi-
tioners in Physique and Chyrurgerie in their administra-
tion of Physque and application of medycynes, in giveinge
of purgations and in Phlebotomie and lettinge of blood &c.
to have the perfect knowledge of the true aspectes of the sonne
& Moone. the coniuctions, oppositions, Motions, revolutions,
Period, Quartils, stations retrogradation, risinge & setting
of the Plannett, and to know the operations, nature and
qualitie of the xij Constellations of starrs or signes in th
Zodiack. to vnderstand in what signe, in what degree of th
signe the sonne or moone settethe everye daye in any of the
xij signes. and comparinge Cosmus the greate world wth
Microcosmus the little world man, to observe and consider the
twelue parts of mans bodye (a thinge soe observed and affir-
med by learned and wise men) wch are ruled and gouerned
by the said xij signes. as it vsually appeares in the Anatomie
of mans bodies in t begining of euerie Almanake:
ffor sure ther is nothinge more profitable, nothing more sure &
certayne to guide and direct them in their practise, then
the true and perfect vnderstandinge of these thinges (yf
soe be the witt of man can attayne to the perfect know-
ledge of them) for is most certayne and apparant, that t
sonne, Moone and starrs and God instruments by secondarye
causes by wch he workethe according as best seemethe to his
divine wisdome and power. /





21

Now as the sonne passethe thoroughe all the xij signes in the space
of a yeare, abyding in a signe thirtie dayes, Soe dothe the moone
passe thoroughe all the xij signes in xxxtie dayes, abyding in a
signe .. howres. And as the moone amongst all the Plannet
is nearest vnto the Earthe soe she hathe greatest affect in working
in Earthlie thing after the disposition and nature of the signes tht
she hath hir course in. And therfore it is requisite and necessarie
to know trulie the state nature and course of the moone for
therby a man may know when it is good to begin thing &
when to ende. And alsoe what weather is lieke to fall after
the nature of the signe whether it be drye or moyst, hott or cold,
It followethe accordinge to the nature of the signe that she is in,
In diverse coniuctions of plannet that she is ioyned wth and
therfore in ordre naturallie I purpose I purpose to declare th
nature and the state of everie signe.

Of the twelue Signes of the Zodiaque.

Aries is the Signe wch governeth in the head of man and
woman, therfore if the moone be in Aries beware of cuttinge
in the head face or any veyne belonging vnto them. Also
it is dangerous in the the begining of Aries to take any head-
ache, or hurt in the head for it is a token of longe contynu-

ance of the same or of deathe. It is not soe dangerous
in the ende as in the begininge, for in the ende of
Aries a man may soone recover and be made hole yf he
be of good govermt. This signe is Orientall, hott &
drye, Colerique and of masculyne kynd, and is attractive
and raigneth from the middest of Marche, to the midest of
Aprill, He that is borne in this signe shalle of good witt
and shall not be to riche nor to poore. and he shall have
damage by his neyghbours. And he shall have power of
dead mens good. He shall be soone angrie and soone pleased
he shall have diverse ffortunes and discord. He shall love
eloquent people, and shall be excellent in many degrees.

He







22

He shalbe vnstedfast of his tong and Carriage and will
take vengeance on his enemyes, And shalbe better disposed
in all thing in his youthe then in his age, He shalbe wed-
ded at xxvtie years of age, if he be not he shalbe a forni-
cator, He shalbe a mediator for soome of his freind He,
shall have damage, a signe in his head in his shoulder
& in his bodie, and shalbe inriched by dead mens good
His first soone shall not live longe. He shalbe in danger
of ffowre footed beast. He shall have great sicknes
at xxiijtie years of age. And yf he fortune then to escape
he is lickelie then to live Lxxxv yeares after the course
of nature, yf he governe well him self.

The woman that is borne in Aries shalbe yrefull and suffer greate
wrong from daye to daye, she wilbe verye prone to lyeinge
She shall loose hir first husband, and shortlie after marrye a better
She shalbe in dread of deathe at xxvtie yeares of age & at xliij yeares
and shall have greate payne in hir head. The dayes of Sol and
of Mars shall be good for them but the dayes of Iupiter shalbe
evell dayes for them as well for the man as for the woman. And
they may be liekned to the sheepe that everie yeare loosethe his
fleese of wooll and in short space recoverethe agayne. The 15,
16,19 dayes of marche be dayes of perill to take sicknes or
to surfett in.

Taurus is the signe that raigneth in the neck and throate. It is
dangerous to be ventosed or cutt in that place whiles the signe
is in Taurus or to take any disease in the throate, and it is more
meridionall perilous in the ende then in the begininge. This signe is meridio-
nall that is of the southe pte whch is cold and drye malencholie
of the complexion of the earthe retentive and of the ffeminine kynd.
note In his signe it is good to begin to builde.

The man that is borne in the signe of Taurus from the mydest of Aprill
tilll the mydest of May shalbe stronge hardie and full of streghthe deli-
cious and shall have good  given him by other men. What he would have
done shall have a speedie dispatche in that wch he will enforce himself
to finishe , in his youthe he will despice everie man. He shall be irefull,

and goe on pilgrymage. and shall leave his friendes and live amongst

He shalbe mache greeved wth sicknes and wth venim. at xxijtie

and shall ryse to greate dignitie, At xxxtie yeares of his age
he shalbe in pill of water. He shall live lxxxv. yeares and three

The woman that is borne vnder the signe of Taurus shalbe effectually
laboringe she shalbe a greate lyer and shall suffer greate harme,
she shall ioye and reioyce at the prosperitie and good of hir friend she
shall have many husband and many Children, she shalbe in hir best
estate att xvj yeares of age she shall have a signe in the midest of hir
bodye. She shalbe verie sicklie, and if she doe escape she shall live by
the course of nature lxxvi yeares She ought to beare ryng and pretious
stones about hir. The dayes of Jupiter and Luna are good bothe for e
man and woman, and the dayes of Mars are contrarie. They maybe
liekened vnto the Bull t laborethe the land and helpethe to sowe the seede
and when the corne comethe into the barne, he hath nothinge but the
straw only for his part and the Corne cleerelie taken from him. Soe
shall they t are borne vnder this signe keepe well their owne and
it shall not profitt them nor any other, and they shalbe reputed vnkind
The 10. & 21. of Aprill are contagious and dangerous dayes for
them to take any sicknes.

Gemeni raignethe in e hand of a man and is dubble bodyed
Occidentall t is of the West parte of sanguine Complexion that
is hott and moyst of the disposition of the Ayre, Digestuie when
the moone is in Gemini beware of Cuttinge in the Shoulders, Armes
or Handes, for whosoever takethe sicknes or hurt att that tyme it is
greate mervayle yf he escape, and much more pill in the ende then
in the begininge for then it is a verie greate token of deathe
It is not good then to shave yo beard or pare yo nayles.

The man.





24

The man that borne vnder Gemini from mydd May to the
midest of Iune shall have many wound He shalbe gentle
and mercyfull, He shall leade an open free and reasonable
lyfe, He shall receyve much money, He shall goe in knowne places
and goe many Pillgramages. He shall love to prayse him
self, he will not abyde wher he was borne, He shalbe wise
but negligent in his workes. He shall come to riches at
xxvjtie, yeares of his age, his first wyfe shall not live long wth
him, and he shall marrye a contentious woman, he shalbe
in yeares before he marrye, he shalbe bitten wth a dogg and
shall have a marke of yron or of fyer, he shalbe troubled in
Water, and shall passe the sea and shall live a hundred
yeares and Ten Monethes after the course of Nature.

The woman t is borne vnder Gemini shall labor verye hard
& paynefullie and shall come to greate honor, and be advanced
by other mens good and be accased of a false cryme. She shalbe
marryed at xiiij yeares of age and then she shalbe chast &
eschue all pill. And by the course of nature she shall live
lxx yeares and love and honor God. The dayes of Sol &
Mercurie be to them verie good and the dayes of Luna & Venus
are to them contrarious, And as well the man as the woman
shall heape vpp and gather to gyther greate store of good and
riches for their successors but will skantlye have the vse or
benifitt of them their owne selves they shalbe soe covetouslye
mynded. The 7. ♋︎15. &20. dayes of May be dayes to take
sicknesse or harme in.

♋︎  Cancer is the signe whch raigneth in the brest e sides and the Sto-
make, e mylt, e hart, e liver & the gall yf any man fall sicke
when the Signe is in Cancer it is mervayle yf he dye not for
it is perilous bothe in the begining & in the ende. The Signe
is of the Northe pte Septentryall cold and moyste of the nature of
water fflegmatique of Complexion the howse of the moone,
Expussative. When this signe rulethe it is good to take a
iourney toward the Northe pt When his Signe governethe
it is good alsoe to take laxatives & receyve potions, and indifferent
for bleedinge wher in those places wher the signe governethe:

The man that is borne in this signe of Cancer that is from the midest of Iune
to the mydest of Iuly shalbe very Covetous and of equall ffortune he
wilbe merrye and love well weomen t be courteous, wise and well favored
he shall have damage by envie. he shall have other mens good in
governement. ther shalbe discord and stryfe betwixt him and his
neyghbours. He wilbe revenged vppon his enemyes, many shall
mocke him & deryde him by his pryde and arogancye. He shalbe
in danger of water often tymes. He wilbe merrye wthin him
self, he shall feele greate griefes and paynes in his bodye,
He shall fynd money that is hidden, and he shall have trouble
about his wyfe. He shall see his perrill in some certayine yeare of
his lyfe but whch is best knowne vnto God. At the xxxiijtie yeare
of his age he shall passe the seas, He shall live accordinge to e
course of nature Lxx yeares, And shall have ffortune, fovorable
vnto him.

The woman that is borne vnder the signe of Cancer shalbe furious, inconti-
nent, angry and soone pecefied, she shalbe wise, serviceable, and
ioyfull,and shall suffer many pilles, she shall paye trulye that

of Venus Iupiter & Luna are good bothe for the man
& woman. and the dayes of Mars are exceedinge evill.
But they shall have good ffortune & victorie over their
enemyes. The 9. & 7. dayes of Iune are dangerous

A Treatice of medicynes good agaynst he pestilence,
devided into Three part. Wherof the first declarethe
how a man should ordre and keep him self in the tyme of
the Pestilence t he fall not into the said sicknes. The second
expressethe how this sicknes comethe. And the third part sheweth
what medycynes are good and fitt to be vsed agaynst this conta-
gious disease.

Caput.1.

It often fallethe out t thoroughe default of observinge good ordre and govermt
in dyet, eateinge and drinckinge they care not how nor what men
doe fall many tyme into the danger of his sicknes. Therfore when
the pestilence dothe raigne it behovethe everie man to observe a
good dyett and keepe him selfe from excesse of meate and drincke,
To vse noe bathes, nor sweate to much, for all these open the powers
of the bodye & cause infectious ayre to enter. and they destroye the
livelie spirit in a man and doe weaken it verie muche. And
princypallie to eschue and avoyde the often vse of Venerie, for that
enfeoblethe a man and wastethe nature and soe openethe the powers of
his bodie that evill ayre may enter in and soe weaken and decaye a
mans spirit. Alsoe in the tyme of Infection vse to eate noe frutes
but such as be tarte. Alsoe abstayne from Garlicke, Oynions, Leekes
or any meates that bringe a man into any vnkynd heates. drincke
measurablye to slake thie thirst or heate. The best drinke were cold
water mixed wth vinegre, or Tysen for that drincke is verie good for
them that are of Cholerique Complexion, for they be hott and drye and
leane of bodye

Caput .2.

In a man are three princypall members. That is to saye the hart, the
Liver and the Brayne. and everie of these have their clensinge places.
wher they may put out their superfluities and puryfie them selves.
The heart hath his clensinge place vnder the arme holes. The liver
hathe his clensinge place in the flanckes of man or woman. And
the Braynes have their clensinge place vnder the eares and vnder
the thoate. Then by reason his sicknes comethe when the powers
be open, and that the ayre may speedilie enter into the bodie , and
mixe it selfe wth a mans blood and soe it runnethe to a mans harte
wch is the roote and ground of lyfe and soe it destroyethe man kynd &
killethe him in short tyme. Soe t the harte being thus infected, infec-
teth and destroyethe what soever is obvious vnto it, strivinge to

expell





32

expell his contagion at his clensing place, wch yf it fynd soe stopped
t it may not passe furthe, then it remaynethe still wth in the bodye
and soe dothe infect t principall part wch is next vnto it.
wch is the liver and destroyethe it. the liver haveinge recey-
ved infection from the hart indevourethe to send the same
to his clensinge place, wch yf it fynd the passage soe stopped
that it may not ysshue forthe, then the contagion passethe
to the third principall part, the Brayne and t send it to
his purginge place viz. to the eares or the throat and yf it
may ^not goe out ther it is greate mervayle yf it rest stedfastlie
in any place. And yf his contagious venim passe not out
wthin twelue howres after it is receyved wth bleedinge
then it abydethe in some place or other and makethe a botche in
some one of the three clensinge places aboue said.

Caput .3.

Whosoever feelethe him self infected wth the plague or
pestilence he shall know it by the shootinge and prickinge
in his bodye, wch is a manifest token of the Sicknes wherfore
he should bleed as speedilie as he can and yf he bleed not wthin
xii howers, then lett him abstayne from meate and drinck vntill
he doe bleede but defer not bleedinge aboue xii howers for
lettinge blood in tyme (as shalbe declared hereafter) is a specyall
meanes of cure. And the matter is then hardned and gathered
togither when it will not passe out of the vayne thoughe it
were cutt wth a sword. neverthelesse yf a man doe bleede
it cannot hurt him, neyther is he sure t it will helpe him after
the contagious matter is hardned and collected togither. And
if the matter have gathered to a head vnder the armehole
it comethe of the veyne that is called Tocall and then it is ex-
pedient to bleed in the same side tht the sicknes appearethe
otherwise it bringethe a double harme: One is that yf you bleede on
the hole side, the good blood on that side is not corrupt and ven-
nomed and then the evill blood will corrupt that place fro
whence the good blood went and soe the bodye becomethe the feobler
for want of good blood to comfort natuer: the second harme is
greater for then the corrupted blood will passe over the hearte
and venome it, and soe bringe deathe vnto the partie soe infected
And yf the sicknes doe appeare betweene the thyghe and e belly
then it comethe of the liver, and then yf the matter appeare in
the inner side by the privie members, then lett the ptie blood

The water of dytanie , Pimpernell, Tormentill, and scabious,

But whiles a man is in this sicknes he ought to be carefullie and measurably

old ale and thinn And yf the patient desire to drinke redd wyne
give him in steede therof vinegre mingled wth fayre water, but
Reinshe wyne is much better then redd wyne yf the patient drinke
measurablie therof

And it good to lake the poulder of these herbes followinge wch is
verie comfortable agaynst infection and Venom:

Take Dittanu, Pimpernell, Tormentill, Scabeous, Bollarmo-
niack and Terra Sigillata: Braye all these herbes seulye by
them selves and the Bolar and Terra sigil. liekewise by them
selves and put it in Wyne or ale and drinke it and it will
drive the venome out wher it did enter. To conclude keepe
him from those thing recyted in the first chapter wch are thing
that Doe further the sicknes. And lett him that is infected
bleed betymes as is expressed in the iij. Chapter. And yf he ^be ruled
and ordred accordinge to this pscription by God grace he shall
be made hole and delivered of his sicknes for ever.

A Medecyne for Plague wch king Henrye
the Eyght did vse in e first yeare of his raigne
wch healed aboue eyght Thousand persons
the same yeare.

Inprimis Take a handfull of herbe grace, of feverfewe, mary-
gold, Sorrell the lieke quantitie, of Burnett a quantie,
of the topp or roote of Dragons a quantitie, washe them
cleane and seethe them softlye in a pottle of runninge
water from a pottle to a quart of liquor Then let it
stand from the fyre till it be almost cold. stayne it wth
a fyne cloathe and drinke it, and yf it be to bitter put
therto a quantitie of Suger Candie And yf it be dronken
before any purples or flowers doe arise ther is noe perill in
the patient by God grace ffor his medycyne is well pved.

Another sovoraigne medycyne for the plague

Take of Sage of Vertue, of herbe Grace, of Elder leaves
of Bramble leaves, of wormewoode of eache of these a handfull
Stampe all these togither and straine them thoroughe a cleane
clothe, wth a quart of, whyte wyne, and put therto a quantitie
of white ginger and drinke therof everie daye a spoonefull
Nine dayes togither, and after the first Spoonefull you shalbe
safe from the disease all e yeare after yf you be not infec-
ted before you drinke it. Neverthelesse yf a man be inifected
before he drinke this medycyne, Then lett him take
water of Scabious, water of dragons, water of Byttenie
of eache a spoonefull and a good quantitie of Treacle
of Geene and mixe them togither and drinke it:
And it will cause e venome of the plague to growe to a
sore. And when the sore dothe appeare.

Take Bramble leaves, Elder leaves, muster seede and
stampe them altogither and make a playster therof and
laye it to the sore, and it will by God grace in short tyme
drawe out the venome and heale the Sore. yf the sicke
man be well governed and close kept for this hath
bene well pved.

Another one of the most holsome preservatives agaynst
the Plague.

Take Carduus Benedictus and hange it in a howse xiiij dayes
or more till it be drye, then lay it on a fayer clothe in the sonne
and when it is thoroughe drye braye it and make poulder therof
And yf one feele any grudinge or prickinge of the plague, then
take of the same poulder wthin xij howres after the pricking begin
or sooner the better, the quantitie of a wallnutt or more and as
much Treakle, a spoonfull of water of dragons and mixe m
togyther and take it vpp wth a knyfes poynte and eate it wthin
xij howres after, and it will cause the sicknes to breake out
in diverse places. And this Medycyne is as pretious
for poyson, Keepe this poulder for it is worthe gold and proved

To make a salue for Botches

Take Perosine white Insence and sheepes Tallowe of eache a lieke
quantitie well melted puryfied and mixed togither and stirr them
well, Then strayne them into a cleane vessell Then put therto
the iuyce of Walworte as much by wayte as it is of one of the other
and myngle them altogither over a softe fyer till it be well then
lett it coole and make it vpp.

A good water for the Plague

Take ffemitorie, Scabious, Rodes, Pimpernell, Pettemorrell
Hearbe Grace, Tormentill, Millefollie, Maythe of everie
one of them one handfull, Two handfull of Columbynes and
ffennell wth the croppes wch be seeded, gather them betwixt viij
& ix. of the clocke in the morninge, and washe them not but
make water of them by distillation altogither wthout any other
thinge.

A good drinke for the Plague.

Take Aloes a penny weyght and put it in half a pynt of
Malmesey and put therto halfe a spoonefull of Aqua Vitae
and drinke therof Two spoonfull in Marche Three spoonefulles
in Aprill fowre in Maye when you be fastinge and that
yeare you shall not be infected.

Another good drinke for the Plague.

When you perceyve on infected wth the sicknes,
Take ffive spoonefulles of the water of dragons, Two

water, and the quantitie of an hasell nutt of fyne
treacle, mixe all these well togither put therto a quantitie

thinke the patient may beare. Make altogither luke warme
and give the patient to drinke and it will expell the venome
and by god grace save his life. Bene Probatum.

Another Tretice of the plague or Pestilence

The preface.

Considering yt is requisite & necessarie for all practitioners
in Phisique and Chirugerie to have a tender care and conside-
ration of the common good and healthe of the kyng liege people
how they may be healed comforted and cured whch are infected or apt
to take infection of the Plague or pestilence: A worthie, noble &
learned Doctur of Divinite, that was prof0undlie and substantially
learned and seene in all the seven Scyences, devised by his learning
his Tretice followinge. moved and induced thervnto thoroughe
a greate desire he had to doe good to everie Christian man & woman
thinkeinge him self & all other Christians bound by the bond of
Christian Charitie, to helpe others in tyme of necessitie and danger.
And as the purpose and intention of the author of this worke was
verie good to witt the prayse and glorie of God and the helpe
and comfort of his Christian bretheren in tymes of their
great pill and danger: Soe noe doubt exceedinge much
good may be gotten herbye for every man may learne to
be his owne Physition in tyme of neede agaynst the vengeance
and deadlie corruptopn of the pestilence when it raignethe.
Now this worke of the pestilence is devided into three parts The 1:
declarethe diverse reasons how and by what meanes e pestilence
may be ingendred.

The 2. shewethe how a man may be preserved from it

The 3: Demonstratethe e remedie helpe and  medicyne agaynst
this contagious disease.

Cap:2

The ground and occasions of ingendringe the Pestilence

It is found in the writing of ffowre Principall doctors of the Churche
of Christ and Chiefe pillers and vpholders of the Catholique faythe
namelie St Augustine, St Gregorie, St Jerome, & St Ambrose, in such
places wher they speake of his contagious disease. That Sinne
is the Chiefe originall cause therof when it raignethe amongst e
people, especyallye when the governers of the Churche of
God, and the expounders of the word, and Law of God doe
transgresse and swarve from ther dutie, therby causing not only
plague and pestilence but many other sicknesses infirmities
and most grievous miseries to be (as tokens of God manifest
wrathe and indignation) inflicted vppon people.







38

It is written in the second booke of King and 24. chapter That
for king David sinne God sent his Prophett Gad and gave
him choyce of three thing viz. Famine for the space of
seaven yeares, or to pursued of his enemies for Three monethes
or els to give the plague of pestilence to raigne vppon
him and his people for the space of three dayes and three
nights: Whervppon Kyng David toke such inward grefe &
remorse of conscyence for his sinne and did soe repent
him for his abhomynal wickednes. That he wth exceeding
greate devotion and wth an humble and Contryte harte
cryed Peccavi, and in greate sorrow and anguishe of mynde
powred forthe his prayers before Almightie God, desie-
ringe pardon and remission of his sinnes att his
handes. And of these three chastisements he maide choyce
to endure the pestilence: Considering t yf hunger shold
be amongst that himself was then the liekeliest to be
pserved and in battle he should be rested and saved yf
it were possible but as for the pestilence it was as
lieklye to infect him as any other. But in his greate
strait that David was put vnto, his chiefe ground and motive
in his choyce was the mercye and goodnes of God whereon he relied
and therfore did chuse to fall into his handes. Soe the lord
sent the pestilence in Isarell from the morninge even to
the tyme appoynted; and ther dyed of the people from Daniven
to Bersheba seventie thousand men. And when king
David did perceyve that God did take soe sharpe vengeance
on his people for his sinnes , he repented, prayed to God
for grace and favour, and he obtayned his request and
the plague ceased. And therfore let everie one endevour
to leave and forsake his sinnes and strive to live and lead
his life agreable to the comandemts of Almightie God
for t is the most Specyall medycyne and best preser-
vative in the world against the plague, and the Chiefe
meane to put and turne awaye the vengeance of God
& the plague and pestilence.






39
Some men hold an opinion that the infection of the plague ingendreth
and proceedethe from the corrupted & infected ayre and vapors of e
Earthe: It may well soe be for accordinge as the people here on earthe
are disposed, soe are the heavenlie bodies aboue inclyned, wherby
you may by good reason pceyve, that of good and vertuous livinge
comethe grace and goodnes, and contrariewise of a sinfull and
wicked life comethe shame, Sorrowe Sicknes and Vengeance;
As appeared at the deluge of Noes flod wher all the world
was drowned for Sinne except eyght persons What man
liveinge can remember t ever sinne was vsed in soe greate
abyomination, soe supported clearelye wthout correction as
now, insoemuch as it is verie offensive and destable to all
good people that feare & love God. And therfore noe mer -
vayle yf the wrathe of God be powred downe from heaven
vppon men, and t he doe visit them wth the infection
of the plague and many other infirmities and miseries since
that men by their transgression doe daylie pvoke him
to anger It is written in Libro Interpretationis, that
all the fowre Elements and all other inferior thing that
are coumpounded of them are ingendred by the course and mo -
tion of the Plannet wch are aboue and that all the inferior
bodies haveinge life, have their beinge, their health, their
sicknes by the moveinge and operation of the superior
bodies.

The second Chapter wherin is declared how a man
may preserve and keepe him self  frome infectio of the
Plague. And how a man may know from what pte of e
bodie the infection dothe pceede.

The firste and princypall preservative agaynst the plague
Is wiselye and carefullie to governe himself. to eschue such
meates and drinkes as may cause him to surfett, and may
ingendre in him grosse and evill humors, wherby a man may fall
into a fever. Viz. Goose pigeons, Mallard Venison, Lamprey
Scate, Eales & such other slymie meates, alsoe Oyneons,
Garlicke, Leekes frutes wch be contagious, and doe ingender
vnnaturall and vnkynd heates, and bringe a man by vnkind
heate vnto an Ague. Alsoe Bathes, Stoves and much sweating
open the powers of mens bodyes, and makethe venemous ayre

to enter






40
to enter and to infect the same and to corrupt the spirituall members
and destroye all the vitall spirits in a man.  It alsoe requisite and
behovefull  for a man in the plague tyme to eschue the voluptuous
lust of the fleshe for they doe not onlye open the powers of the bodye
but alsoe destroye the naturall strengthe and vigor in a man &
infeeblethe the spirituall memberes and the vitall spirits.
In tyme of pestilence also it is good for everie man to abstayne from
vnmeasurable drinkinge and drunckennes, but to drinke moderately
to allaye thirst and noe more. And the vse of these drinkes and
waters viz. Tysen mixed wth vinegre, water of  Burrage, of Endife,
of Lettuce, of violett, of Roses, Scabioius, Tormentill, dictanum
myngled by even portions or druncke by them selves are verie good
and profitable agaynst the pestilence in Sommer tyme, especyallie
for them that are of a Colericke complexion, for they are hotter and
dryer then others are. It is verie contagious alsoe to come into
corrupt ayres in the morninge, before one take e ayre of the fyre.
Stinckinge gutteres, uncleane standinge waters, draught and other
evill savours may be an ocasion of the plague, wherfore it is
good for one in tyme of infection to keep his Chamber daye and
knight fast closed agaynst the East and Southe, and to have a pann
of Coales in his chamber and to laye theron baye leaves, and rose -
marie dryed and frankencense, and to keepe a good fyre in
the Chamber night and daye is verie good, with the vse of good fumo -
sities of ffrankencense rosemarie and other such good thing as
hath bene said for corrupted ayre hathe an attractive power
in it drawinge from the livelie spirits and spirituall members
their vigor and bringethe infection and venom overthwhart e
veynes of the hart, wch infectinge it, hastnethe a mans deathe.

See in the 2 Chapter of e
former treatise of e plague.

How to know from what part of the bodye the infection dothe come and
pceede viz. from wch of the princypall members of mans bodye.
ffor as hathe bene said in a former treatise ther are iij principall
Members of the bodye the Hearte the liver and the brayne wch
have their severall clensinge places wherin they expell and send
forthe their venome and infection yf they have receyved any:

The hearte hathe his clensinge place vnder the arme holes,
and therfore yf any tumor or botche be ther it is a signe that the hearte
hath sent his contagion thither

The clensinge place of the liver is the flankes and therfore yf
ther appeare any matter in the share besides the privities then it appeareth
that the infection is sent thither from the liver

The braynes have their place of clensinge vnder the Eares and vnder the
throate, and therfore yf any swellinge or botche doe ther appeare it is evident
the infection comethe from the Brayne. Now of the Cure vide Cap: sequenti






46
The third chapter wherin is declared what remedie and medycynes
are best to be vsed to avoyde the danger of this most contagious sicknes
of the plague or pestilence. /

How a man may know in tyme of the plague or pestilence that he hath
receyved the infection hath bene declared in a former treatise
viz. by the extraordinarie shootinge and prickinge that he shall feele
in his fleshe. Now for the cure as it is ingendred by some naturall
causes and pceedethe from thing (wch vsed by God as secondarie causes
and instrument for e punishmt of sinne as hath bene declared) have
their source and beginninge from nature. Soe noe doubt Almightie
God of his greate mercy and goodnes, takeinge commiseration
of mans infirmitie hath appoynted and given vnto man naturall
helpes and remedies for the same, as well as for other sicknesses
and diseases. Soe that Avecine in his commune Treatise De Cura
Febrium saythe verye well: That ther is noe sicknes that fallethe
to man pceedinge from naturall causes but ther is a naturall
remedie and medycyne pvided for the same. /

The first meanes therfore that hathe bene thought the best and fittest
meanes of remedye after the infection receyved, by learned men
in the secreet of nature and in physique is lettinge of blood
and t wth as much speede as may be, wthin twelue houres
andt the furthest and that in fittinge and convenient places
accordinge as the infection and corruption shall appeare
and manifest it self for (as hath alreadie bene shewed yf that
any corrupt matter appeare about the share and privie members
then it is an argument that the Liver is infected and then the
partie soe infected must be lett blood on the same side in the veyne
that is betwixt the greate Too and the next vnto it, and in noe
wise lett him bleed in the arme, for that would drawe vpp the
infection agayne vnto the liver and soe to the harte and
kill the man. But yf the botche be more outward toward
the side and further from the privities then lett blood on the
Veyne betweene the Ancle and the foote and e heele or
els in the veyne that is vnder the Ancle called Sophia
or els be ventosed speedilye beneathe the botche, wch thoughe it be

To make a Pommander to carrye in one hand
in tyme of infections of the Plague to take awaye
the evill ayre.

Take Laddanum puryfyed one Ounce Storax Calamint disolved, Roses
Cloves, mace of eache three halfe pennie weyght, and a Penny weight
of chosen Amber and Muske make poulder of them that will make poulder,
Then take a spoonefull of Oyle of Bayes, an ounce of Virgin waxe
and disolve them, and when they be disolved mingle them altogether
vppon the fyre, and when it is cold make Ball therof the quantitie
of a Wallnutt. And this is the true makeinge of a Pommander
the sent wherof dothe comfort the livelie spirit of a man

A good medycyne for the plague.

Take a handfull of Rewe & of Marigold Scabious ffetherfewe,
Sorrell and Burnett of eache a handfull, a quantitie of dragons
eyther the rootes or toppes, Then washe them cleane and put to them
a pottle of cleane runninge water, and lett them seethe togither
to a quarte and when it is almost cold straine and drinke it
everie morninge and eveninge fastinge. This, medycyne is e
better yf you put to it a little Sugar Candie and if it be
dronke before the purples appeare it will helpe God willinge.

An other Medycyne for the Plague.

Take nyne Sponefull of runninge water agaynst the streame
and a C. Sponefull of Red vinegre ii Sponefull of Trecle of
Iene, Seethe all these togither and skim it cleane and yf the
patient be aged let him drinck it blood warme, a Child as hott
as he can suffer it, and lett him fast longe after it.

An other Medycyne for the Sicknes and to breake the Sore

Take fetherfewe, wormewoode, Sorrell, Muggworte, Marygold Rewe Scabious,
and dragons the Cropes or the rootes of eache of them a handfull, and stampe
them and take the uiyce of them, and a pynte of Ale or Malmesie, and lett them
boyle togither. Then take ij sponefulles of treacle of Iene & put to it & lett
it seethe togither till it be well sodden. Then lett the patient drinke it
and keepe him warme, And yf  he take it in tyme it will drive the cor-
ruption out; And yf the sore doe appeare Take ij greate oynions and rost
them in the fyre, and a handfull of night shade and braye it Small and take
the iuyce, and put them to a penyworthe of Aqua vitae and put it all togither
and all cold put it in wher the sore is & it will breake it. You must beate
them altogither in a Morter, and then it wilbe like a plaister

A Treatise or worke called the Regiment of Healthe
devided inte Eyght parts or Chapteres wherof the first
entreatethe of the commoditie and profitt that comethe by
keepeinge a good ordre and governement. The second
what is be done everie morninge t a man may preserve
his healthe, The Third showethe the good and benifitt that
of honest and vertuous excersices of the Bodye, The
fowrethe declarethe the diverse kyndof bodelie exercyse
wch are to be vsed accordinge to the diversitie of complexions
and Constitutions of men. The ffyfte teachethe how a
man should ordre and dyett him self in eateinge and
drinkinge. The sixt what meate may be best for e
sustentation and comfort of mans bodye. the seventhe

Galen that profound Clarke and excellent learned  physition sayethe t
it is verie expedient for a man to learne good and holsome rules
instructions for the preservation of his healthe wch beinge well obser-
ved aman may preserve his bodye in good state free from sicknes
vntill he come vnto decrepit and impotent age. He sayethe of
him self that he never had any sicknes vnlesse it came eyther by
over violent travelinge, or labor, or by liveinge in unpure and cor-
rupt Ayre, or by visitinge of Sicke and diseased folkes, wherfore
he affirmethe that noe man can observe a good dyett or keepe a
holsome Regiment of his healthe, That is constrayned to travell
night and daye to gett his liveinge, and may not make choyce
of the place and ayre wher he would live: for that man that will
live and keepe the rules and ordres that tend to the preservation
of healthe must have so plentifull an estate as that he may live
wthout labor, and have all such meanes and helpes as will
mayntayne and preserve a man in good healthe.

Ad gubernationem sanitatis to holsome governement of a mans
healthe ther are Eyght necessarie things required.

1 The first a discreate choyce of such thing as shalbe eaten or dronken

2 The Second bodylie exercyse before meate, wch must be ad ruborem
non ad sudorem till a man begin to sweate or the wynd begin
to waxe short.

3 The Third. That the meate you eate be well chewed.

4. The ffowrthe To eate noe meate till you have a good Appetite.

5. The ffyft That a man sleepe in the morninge till he wake of
his owne accorde.

The Sixt





46

6 The Sixt, to eate yo meate wth myrthe and gladnes, for that
makethe good digestion.

7  The Seventhe, Keepe yo self from Cold in the winter and in
the Springe.

8 The Eyght, Vse to eate Saffron sometyme in yo meates
for it helpeth digestion and comforteth the Stomake, and
gladethe the harte, and keepethe the humors from rottinge.

It is reported in Storye That ther was a certayne kinge
wch demanded of Three Physitions of his wch were of
three sondreye Nations This Question, viz. What were
the best thing to be vsed for the bodelye healthe of man.

1 The first wch was a Grecian sayd, Take everye daye in
the morninge yo mouthe full of warme water, and you shall
never neede other medycyne.

2 The second wch was a Mede answered, Eate Grommell
seedes everie daye fastinge.

3 The Third and last wch was an Indian, said Sleepe in the
morninge till you fynd noe heavines in yo bodye of
the meate you eate over night.

But Aristotle saythe Eate everye daye one and twentie
sweete Raysons, for they helpe flegmatique diseases, and the
fevor quarten, they refreshe a mans mynd and his under-
standinge And that daye that a man eatethe figg wth
the Croppes of hearbe Grace, ther shall noe poyson hurt
him, wch is soone tryed.

Gallion & Constantyne affirme That measurable Abstinency
is the most pfitable medycyne to eschue Cares and pen-
sivenes, hevines and dulnes, wrathe and anger, wch doe
muche trouble and annoye cholerique psons. Vse noe
late suppers, nor vnder meales or beavers. Walke after
meate, vse noe stronge drinke, hold not yo water, nor
force or constraine yo self to  s.ege, keepe yo hart merrye
and yo mynd ioyfull. Travell in measure, Take noe
excesse in meate or drinke, In Winter eate moderatlie
of holsome meate, In the Springe tyme drinke not to much
for then Children are best in healthe. In Sommer wett yo

meat.







47

meates well for then old men are best at ease.  In winter young
men are best at ease. in August vse to eate not frutes.

Allexander sayethe That he wch dothe well observe this excellent
ordre of good goverment of his healthe Shall neede neyther
Physition nor Chyrurgion, vnlesse it be by accydent, or by
vrgent causes of Stripes and woundes receyved in ffrayes
or Battell.

The Second Chapter shewinge what is best to be done in the morninge
after that a man dothe awake from his sleepe.

First after that a man hath performed his dutie in his devotions toward
Almightie God, it is good for a man to Clothe him self in sweete clothes, To
Combe his head, washe his handes, open the powers of his bodie, that the vapors
may assend vpward from the Stomack to the head, ingendred in the Sleepe
And then assaye to put out e Superfluitie of the bodye, by Cougheinge, Spettinge
washinge yo handes, makeinge of water and emptieinge the entralles downe-
ward. In Sommer tyme washe yo handes and face wth cold water, and in
Winter, wth warme water.  Then walke a whyle in some holsome ayre, and
stretche out yo limbes. And servinge God contynuallye in his feare be
honestlye merrye. Flye Anger and Idlenes, ffor myrthe and quiet of mynd
wth moderate exercyse doe much comfort and glad the hearte, and waste and
consume evill and grosse humors in the bodye of man.

The Third Chapter of the good and comoditie of temperate exercyse.

He that will use exercyse that his holsome and good for the healthe of the bodye
must doe it moderatlie, and soe in his voluntarie labors he must avoyde excesse
Before meate to be walkinge or rydinge in Cleane places, and highe, and in
pure ayres, and not to come amongst Muckhillor Marishes, and soe to
exercyse the bodye till it begin to sweate or waxe warme, and then rest, for yf you
should make any greater labour of it, it would be paynefull and bringe fayntnes
and soe yo labour would be immoderate and troublesome lieke the laboures
of Marchant, Artificers, Ploughemen Carters and such liek, for neede
constraynith them that they cannot leave, when they would. And therfore
Plinie saythe that moderate labor may much benifitt a man in the
preservation of his bodye in healthe, for it increasethe naturall heate
wch is the cause of strengthe. wherby alsoe the facultes attrac-
tive, retentive, digestive and expulsive, togither wth
the five bodelie sences of hearinge, seeinge, Smellinge, tastinge
and feelinge are holpen and Cherished. And in like sort also
the Three interior Sences properlie belonginge to man, namelye

fantsie,







48

Phantasia seu Imaginatio the Phantasie or immagination
Judicium suie sensus communis the iudginge or descerninge
facultie, & Memoria suie Recordatis the memorative
facultie, is holpen by moderate Labor, Soe that men
did not erre in their Labors and exercyses in their extreemes
but could a due measure and moderation in them, ther
should never neede any other medycyne for a preserva -
tive of the healthe of man.

Now ther are two thinges required in moderate and discreete
Labor. Viz. That ther be not when a man dothe labor or
exercyse to greate repletion or to much emptined of bodye
for bothe of these doe exceedinglye weaken and infeeble the
bodye. The second is mode exercyse wch Supplyethe the
defects and want of bleedinge, Bathinge, and such other
Wherfore Labor is a medycyne that is not bytter, nor
dreadfull, nor chardgable in causinge great expence but
is a verie good recreation fot the bodye yf it be done in
Sweete and drye places and holsome ayres, wher a man
may take pleasure and delight to see goodlye trees and
Plantes pleasant Countryes, delectable field medowes
and pastures, delightfull streames of running waters,
and especyally in such places wher he may take the Sweete
sent and odour of most flagrant & odoriferous flowers;
and most holsome herbes in well tilled, dressed and manured
land and by the viwe hereof and by the delectation and
Sweete contentment he shall receyve herby, his mynd
may contynually be stirred vpp to give laud and prayse
to our good and gracious lord God and Saviour who hath vouche-
safed to bestowe these excellent benefitts and blessing
vppon vs. Fulgeus the phylosopher saythe that moderate
exercyse in any good Scyence, facultie or honest busines & good
labour is the most perfect preservative of bodely health and
wastethe the Superfluities of Choler and grosse humors, it increaseth

t Labour






49
t Labour dothe heate and drye the bodye, therfore it must needes be
necessarye for flegmatique persons to vse, to drye vpp their moyst
humors and to heate ther bodyes wch by rest and idlenes are
cooled and moystned but a Colerique bodye is made more moyst
by labour. wherfore wher labour and exercyse is discretely vsed
ther needethe noe other Physique as hathe bene alreadie declared
To conclude therfore let vs gather togither the benifitts and pfitts
of bodelye labour and exercyse discreetlye and moderatelye
vsed. viz.

It stirreth kyndlye and naturall heate, it openethe the powers
of the bodye, It loosenethe Cold, thick and toughe humors and
expellethe them, Makethe all the ioyntes nimble and lythe,
It comfortethe all the members and part of the bodye;
And as moderate labor is good soe measurable rest is good for e
bodye and to much therof hurtfull and evill, for therby evill
and Corrupt humors are ingendred, And even as water
yf it rest and stand still it will putryfie and stincke, Iron
yf it lye still and be not vsed rust; soe liekwise man or woman
when they have ever much rest doe decaye in healthe and strenght
of bodie, and fall in diverse infirmities, sicknesses and diseases,

The fowrthe Chapter of the diversitie of exercyses to be
vsed accordinge to the sondrye conditions of men & difference
in constitution of bodie and complexion and alteration of the
temper of the ayre.

Now as men doe differ in condition and Compexion soe are diverse
and sondrye exercyses to be vsed of them: according to the constitution
and condition of everye man: For we see t some be stronge and lustie
others weeke and feeble, Some are riche others are poore, Some have
greate libertie, other are restrayned therof, Sometyme the wether
is fayre, somtyme otherwise. And soe these diverse dispositions requir
diverse exercyses. The first and the best is to walke before meate
in Sweete ayres drye and cleane places free from evill savours and
from filthie morishe waters as is before rehearsed. And those that
cannot goe to ryde yf they have horses. An other yf a man can neyther
ryde nor goe abroad by reason of the fowlenes of the weather, Then
lett him walke in the howse, and beare some weyght from place
to place till he be wearie or take a staffe in his hand and hold it
till some take it from him by force, or hold a penny in his hand &
lett one take it out yf he can. Or els hold and pene yo wynd as
longe as you can for that is good for them t shall Runn, Wrastle, cast
the stone or such lieke, But of all exercyses flee vnmeasurable

exercyse of





50
exercyse of venerie, for when a man dothe wast and cast out
good humore to muche, he is more weakenned and infeebled
therby, then yf he should lett out fortie tymes as much blood
out of his bodye. And the overmuch vse therof  ingendrethe
the Palseye and many other diseases.

The fyfte Chapter declarethe how a man should ordre
him self in his dyett.

Now as ther are some that have all thing at their will and pleasure,
meate and drinke, and what els they can desire, Soe other ther
are wch have not this plentie, yea have scarce ynoughe many tymes
to suffice nature, and these are not bound to observe the dyett prescribed.

These Rules therfore serve for such as may make choyce in their dyett
and live in plentie, Lett them therfore eate good levened bread,
drincke holsome wyne or Ale, feede on light meat of easie digestion,
Talke merrylie att the Table, Eate not wthout an Appetite,
Chew well ther meate Sleepe not with a full stomake, nor Eate
till their Stomake be emtye, wch you shall know by the desire you
have to eate and by the Whitenes of yor Spettle, And alsoe yf
the desilre of yor Appetite be kindled, you shall feele a good ayre
come from e Stomake. And yf you eate wthout an appetite yor
Stomake may not digest yor meate by reason of coldnese therof,
And to walke after meate is verie comfortable for the Stomake.

The sixt Chapter declarethe the qualitie of meates, and that
a man should make choyse of such as are most agreable to his constitutio,
and for the healthe of his bodye.

ffor some thing are hott and burnee bodye as Pepper, Garlicke
Onions, Savage, Myntes, Cresses, and such lieke, And
some are Cold and Chill the bodye, As Lettuce, Purslen,
Gourdes and such lieke. And yf you feede on waterye
meates they are apt to Corrupt the blood and rott it as
Cucombers Mellons, and yf a man vse to feede much on drye
meates they drye the bodye. Yf yor meate be fatt they
hindre digestion, yf bitter they are not norishinge, And
yf they be Salt they bren and annoye the Stomake, And
if they be sower they hasten old age, and, wher any of these
meates are to muche vsed they are Contagious for e
bodye, And nothinge more vnholsome then those meates
wch are meanelye temperate, voyd of excesse in everye
qualitie, and participateth som what of everie one.

And






51
And therfore by naturall reason and iudgement of Physique these be
good and holsome meates. Viz. Lambe, kidd, Hennes Capons
Chickins ffeasants, Partridge and Plovers, and all Land byrd
But noe Sea fowle. Skaled fishe of runninge waters, reere Egg
Borage, Langdebeofe, good  Cernes bread well baked, Alsoe yf
one be cloyed about the stomake wth toughe fleame and Slyme
matter then it is good to eate Sharpe short meates and sower
as Cheese, Salt meates and bytter the tast wherof may be amended
wth sweete apples, and honny. Drinke good old wyne, eate Cherries
grapes damesens and Almond before meate, Peares Quinces,
Nutt and Wardens before meate. Give to Sanguine men
distempred, Malancholie meates, and vnto melancolique men
distempred give Sanguine meates. Eate not to much of the
milke of beast and drincke not much milke and wyne
togither for it ingendreth leprosie and white Scabbes. As concerninge
the quantie of meate to be taken or of drincke, noe certayne
Rule or precept can be given, only this observe what quantitie dothe
you most good and dothe best agree wth you and that vse you. Vse not
+ to eate presentlie after Sleep labor or bathinge. And feede on
of those meates wch your appetite desireth whatsoever they be whether
light and easie of digestion or grosse meates, for Such as the
Stomake earnestleye affectethe and desirethe it hastethe and eagerly
katchethe it longe holdethe it, and well and perfectly digesteth it

The seventhe Chapter teachethe certayne rules to be observed
concerninge meate and drinke and about Sleepinge good for
the preservation of the healthe of man.

It is not good at one tyme to eate diverse meates, for one turnethe
to grosse and heavie humours, the other to light and Subtill humors
and soe yf light meates be first eaten, they wilbe first digested
and drawe wth them the grosse meates that be not digested, and yf
the grosse meates be first eaten, then the light meates wax corrupt
in the stomacke, for they cannot passe because of the grosse meates.
afterward. Eate noe rawe meate, ffeede measurably, leave
wth an appetite, and yf you overcharge yo Stomacke wth meate
it provoketh you to vomitt and in that case eyther sleepe or walke
softlye, and eate noe meate in xxiiijtie howres after, And
then eate a little and drincke a draught of good wyne
therto, and have care to ^keepe a better dyett afterward.
s ss s s s
Two.





52
Two thinges are Causes of diverse sicknesses: Wherof one is
diversitie of Meates and drinkes, and of them be ingendred
diverse humors some good and some evill/
The second is longe sittinge at meate, for the meate that
is first taken is concocted and digested before any digestion of
the last beginn, Soe the meate t is digested draweth that wch
is not digested vnto the Liver. Alsoe drincke not to muche
at yo meate lest it cause the meate to goe over soone out of yo
stomake, or make yo meate to Swime in yo stomake/ Alsoe
abstayne form drinke presentlye after Sleepe or greate Labor /
till you have walked or rested a whyle/ Nor drinke by night
for that ingendrethe the dropsy. And after dynner or after
supper vse to stand or walke softlye vp and downe, till yo meate
be well settled in your stomake, And sleepe a little yf you be vsed
therto, and bothe daye and night vse to sleepe on the right side
for t makest best digestion, for then the liver lyethe vnder the
stomake as the fyre lyeth vnder the pott, And after the first
sleepe turne you on the left side till you be disposed to turne
you agayne, at lye at yo ease, soe you lye not on the backe, for
that causethe the superfluities to drawe vnto the hearte.
And observe this rule that the more you eate, the more you must
sleepe. And hathe bene said vse not to drinke much after
yo meate, for lieke as cold water cast in to a boylinge pott
lettethe the boyling therof, even soe much drincke powred
into the stomake after meate beinge ther begun to concoct
dothe hinder the digestion of the stomacke; And as before
hathe bene said it is verie good to stand or walke after
meate supper or dinner for it helpethe to emptie the
Stomacke and further the good digestion//

The Eyght Chapter declarethe what meates and
drinkes are best to be vsed for the keepinge of a holsom dyett
and also treatethe of the discommoditie of evill dyett./

Your bread shalbe made of fyne whete flowre well levenned and
well baked, yo drinke shalbe good holsome wyne, or well brued
Ale or beare freshe and stall with sage leaves and Rosemarie
yo fleshe of fowre footed beast shalbe beefe, mutton, veale,
venison, faune, kidd, hart, Cunye, Lambe, Porke, Pigge
and in ward of beast, Brawne, trypes souce and suchlieke,

Plover, Rayle, Quayle, Godwitt, Pigeons, Lark , Thrushes, mavis
Starlinge and all other smale byrd that are not fedd on the water.
Your Water fowle are these, Swann Storke Curlew, Bitter, Goose

White meate is somewhat Contagious, except Butter, Egg

De Mala gubernatione sanitatis

Many ther be that doe not onlye evill governemt in meates and drinkes
but alsoe doe defend and mayntayne their disordre wth great earnestnes
and many contentious word: affirminge that they be are best in healthe
& live most ease when they are most disorderlie in their dyett and have
lest care to governe them selves by rules and precepts of physitions
and soe they skorne the Physitions and other wise men that have a
care to keepe good dyetts. And not regardinge the state of their owne
bodyes, their owne healthe and wellfare, spend their dayes in Gurman-
dizinge in ryottinge and surfettinge perswadinge them selves that

their





The discommodi-
ties of surfetinge
& evill dyett.
their contynuall Custome of surfeting dothe them noe harme
because they are so used thereunto But noe doubt they are
greatelie deceyved, for it hurthethe them verie muche,
for it alterethe their Complexion, It debilitatethe and
infeoblethe their vitall & Spirit, and fillethe them full of grosse
humors, Alsoe as Gallion saythe yf one of those disordred
psons fall into any sicknes it is a harder matter to recover
them then twentie others that are of good governement.,
Wherby it is ^to be seene wither Custome dothe accord wth Nature
yea or noe. Yf it dothe accord and agree wth nature then it
may be vsed and kept, yf not to be vtterlye refused, and by little
and little to be left and putt cleane awaye.

The Commondatio
of Moderation
in dyett & e

Evell of ill dyett
excesse in any
thinge

Constantyne saythe that moderation in keepeinge a
meane betwixt extreemes is one of the most soveraigne
and best thing that canbe for the healthe of a mans
bodye, for the excesse in any thinge is hurtfull. To muche
or to little of meate or drinke, to muche watchinge or
to muche sleepeinge, over muche Labor and to muche
rest, the extreemes eyther of heat or cold, doe spoyle a
mans complexion, waste nature, destroye his livelie
spirits; Whereas vsinge a measare in keepinge the
bodye in good governement free from excesse in anythinge
Comfortethe the bodye in all part therof, helpethe a mans
witt and understandinge and dothe preserve him in good
vigor and disposition bothe of mynd and bodye makeinge
him more fitt to serve God and to mannage worldlye
affayrers. And soe to conclud lett us evermore give
laud and thankes vnto Almightie God or gratious
Lord, wch hath given to man remedyes for all sicnesses
& diseases.





The Preface 55

Now that I have written generallie of the Fowre Complexions
of man and the Alteration of them, Of the Twelve Signes
wth their severall properties And alsoe of the seven planet
how they move and are disposed: And have alsoe sett downe &
declared certayne rules and observations wth other thing
pertayninge vnto the Regiment of healthe: Now will I
shewe you what medycynes are agreable and fittinge for the Cure
and rememdie of diverse sicknesses, and alsoe wch contagious
hurtfull and not agreable wth the same Sicknesses.

First ther are two manner of Medycynes that be requisite &
needfull to relieve man in tyme of Sicknes: One is called
a Simple Medycyne the other is called a compound medycyne.
A Simple medycyne is made but of one thinge onlye, As of some
Oyle, herbes, Rootes, waters distilled of hearbes, wyne, honey, seed,
&c. Compound Medycynes are made of two simple or more.
And therbe many Medycynes of the same kynd As Treties
Emplasters, Oyntments, Potions that be called drinkes
Electuaries, Syrrops, and waters eyther to be outwardlye applyed
or taken inwardlye by eatinge or drinkinge them for the
Comfort of mans bodye and for the destroyinge and takinge
awaye disease and Sicknes.

Alsoe it is to be observed that Sicknesses and maledyes are Cured
by their Contraries, as hott diseases are cured wth cold medycynes,
& cold wth hott, drye wth moyst medycynes et & converso. And
those sicknesses that have bene caused thoroughe Anger, Sorrow
and griefe of mynd, are to be cured wth ioye and gladnes.
Nevertheles sometymes diseases and maladies have their Cure
not by Contrarie medycynes but wth such as doe accord &
agree wth them. As scaldinge and Brenninge be healed wth hott
medycynes that draw out vnkyndlye heate, wheras Cold medycynes
doe in close the heate and put the patient to more payne. In ffevors
alsoe t be hott yf one drinke cold water and milke it is verie Contagious &
perilous for the Sicknes. And cold medycynes applyed to hott member
increase the heate and much more augment the payne. ffor example yf
a mans head be hott inordinatelie putt it in cold water or Snow and
it will make it much more hotter then it was before, for the role Stopethe the powers
and enclosethe the heate in a lesse space than it was in before. And a thinge t
is inclosed in a lilttle space, hathe more power and force then, when it is at large,
And soe sometymes hott medycynes, and sometymes cold heale cold Sicknesses.
But Temperate medycynes are most profitable for all manner of disease
be they hott or cold.





56
Of the diverse hurtes that often tymes come by
Chance vnto the bodye of a man in diverse part therof

Of the hurtes in mens bodyes some are old and some are newe,
And lieke as ther are diverse hurtes soe their are diverse medycynes
as hereafter followethe.

If a mand heade be broken wthout breakeinge of e skinn know firste
whether the Skull be broken yea or noe, And whether any of
the Two skinnes that goe about he Brayne be perished, or not,
The wch Skinnes are called Dura mater & Pia Mater. Of
the breakeinge of the skinn these are signes and tokens. A man
loosethe his appetite to his meate, and cannot digest his meate,
he sleepethe heavelie and hathe little rest, he makethe water wth greate
payne and goethe to the privie wth greevance, he fallethe into
some fever. It will greeve and payne him to eate hard crust,
or to Crack nuttes, and these are tokens of a mans skull being broken.
And yf the skull be broken it is to be examined whether any of the
two skinnes that goe about the brayne be broken: Yf Dura Mater
be broken or hurt (wch is the outer skin) you shall know it by these
signes: The head akethe, the Cheekes looke redd the eyes are impaye-
red bothe in color and in sight, the tonge waxethe blacke.

But yf ut happen that Pia Mater wch is the skin next the Brayne
be hurt, then a man shall lacke sleepe, his speeche will waxe
smale, his eyes will waxe dull and dym, and blood will yssue out
of his nostrill and eares, and he will be costive and may not
avoyd or expell any thinge downeward, And twise or thrise in
a daye he will fall into fevors and agues, wch is a most dan-
gerous most liekelie of deathe. And somtyme the skin is
bowed inward wth a stroke and the patient shall sleepe but
little, and when he sleepethe he shall sleepe verie vnquietlye
wth much trouble in his sleepe, adn he farethe as yf he were
franticke and would rise vpp and take weapons to fight.

And sometyme the head is swollen wth a stroke and the skull
not perished, and you shall know it by these signes. he may
eate well and drincke and sleepe alsoe, neyther fallethe
he into a fever for all his hurt. And these be the same tokens
to shew you whether the skull, the skins Dura Mater or Pia
Mater be perished or not.

for the heade

And yf e head be swollen and the skull hole then it shalbe healed wth
medycynes t shall dosplve e swellinge as thus, Take mugworte,
hearbe Grace, Absinthiu or wormewood and Cummen, heate them altogither
& seathe them in meate oyle, make a playster of it and laye it to the
Sore, and change it often till the Swelling be abated and then it
is liekelye the matter will turne into Twitter, and then laye a playster
therto t may ripen it made in this manner followinge.

Take e iuyce of Marche, herbe Grace, flower honey, Swynes greace

therto. /

Alsoe a man may be wounded in the skull two manner of wayes
The head may be cutt wthout perishinge the skull And the skull
may be Cutt as farr as e outward skinn. And Dura Mater or
Pia Mater be perished wthout hurtinge of the Brayne/
But yf the brayne be hurt and the wound be eyther on
the fore part or hynder part of the head t comethe to the
brayne then is it deadlie/ But a wound that comethe
from aboue downeward wthout hurtinge the brayne, thoughe
it passe thoroughe the eare or nostrill it is not mortall soe
longe as the Brayne is not perished . And it may be
healed in this wise/ Take e Glare or whites of Egg and
wett them in a soft lynnen Clothe/ and laye it in the
Wound soe t everie place be closed therwth and then
laye playster theron to make it quitter./.

out the corruption of the wound & makes them cleane,
And thus you make the yellow Oyntment. /

The yellow
oyntment
how it is made

Take a li of meate Oyle, one li of Sheepes Tallowe,
halfe a li of pitche iij Ounces of Colophyne, an ounce
of waxe, and yf it be in winter take of Oliban mastik
Galbanum, Armoniack Seraphine and Terpentyne,
of eyther halfe an ounce, and melt them that will melt
wth the oyle & make poulder of the residue & mingle
them togither over the fyre, and when it is sodden
ynough put yr Turpentyne therto, then close it
vp and keepe it, And yf you will know when it is
well sodden put a dropp therof uppon a marble stone,
or an other cold stone & touche wth yr finger when it
is cold and yf it cleave to yr finger & relent not then
it is well sodd. /

Vnguentam Popilion how it is made (& wherfore it is good

Take iiij li of Poppler flowers, iij li of hearbe Water,
one li of Henbane, of Pettie Morrell, of orpen, of Sengreene
the lieke quantitie, of Endive, waybread, violet water
Cresses of each halfe a li washe them cleane, Stampe them
and put therto ij li of well fryed bores grease, And
when they be well medled togither, put it into a pott
well closed & lett it stand nyne dayes, then take it out
of the pott and put it into a Pann, and sett it over the fyre
& frye it well & stirr it wel wth a Slice yt it stick not to e
Pann, and when it is hott ynoughe, take it from the fyre
and straine it into a vessell, and sett it over e fyre agayne
& put therto a quarter of a li of wax in smale peeces & as
muche fyne poulder of frankencense and stir it well till
it be relented, then take it from the fyre and straine it
& lett it Coole, and put the water from it. Then turne it
& take awaye all the filthe from the under side, and sett
it over the fyre agayne & melt it, and wth a feather skume
it & put it in boxes; And this the true makeinge therof
It is a soveraigne medycyne for aches.

How to make vnguentum Sanatiuum.

Take Bitten vervayne, Bugell, Sancle Pimpnell
Strawberrie hearbe Robert, hearbe water, Tansie Orpine,
Sungreene, Solficle, Egremonie, greate Consounde mydle
Consounde, pettie Consounde, Osmonde, Cropes of Hempe
Cropes of Bryers, Cropes of Nettles, & of madder of eyther
of a lieke quantitie, and of Auence as much as all the other
herbes are in waight, Stampe them altogether and put to them
a quart of oyle Oliffe & straine altogeiher thorough a cleane
clothe, Then put therto a quarterne of waxe and as much
Swynes grease, an ounce of Incense and halfe an ounce of Mastick
Boyle them altogither and put it in boxes for it is a pretious
Oyntment to heale all wound. Probatum.

An other waye to make this Sanative Oyntment.

Take Saven, herbe Grace and Tansey of eyther of them a
handfull and stampe them altogither and put therto Oyle, waxe,
Swynes grease, Incense and Masticke in lieke quantities as
is afore said, and soe make it vp for it is pved.





How to make Oyle of Henbane 1

At mydsommer take an newe earthen pott of a pottle or a
gallon, and make many smale holes in the bottom wth an Awle
and fill e pott full wth the Cropes of Henbane & cover it close
Then sett the bottome of the pott over the mouthe of an other pott
fast closed and sett them bothe in e earthe by the space of a yeare
an then take them vpp, and you shall fynd a cleare oyle in e
nether pott, That Chirurgions call oleum.

Vnguentum Pretiosum good to drive out cold taken in the Shoul-
ders Armes Thyghes or knees, how it is made./

Take hearbe of Grace Bittony, Sage, Gowldes wallwort
the tenders of Ivie and of Sawrell leaves, of eyther a lieke
quantitie and Camamell as much as half all the residue
Shredd them smale, then take dogg grease, Catt grease
Brockes grease and duckes grease, of eyther a lieke quan-
titie, shredd the hearbes smale and seathe them well togither
in a pott, and put therto a quantitie of meate oyle &
straine it. This Oyntement is very good for the gout
for Aches and vncams.

An other Oyntment.

Take Ribworte Planten Cinqfole. dayses, Millefolly,
Compharie, Wallworte, Parietorie, Scabious, Bugle, Sanicle,
a handfull of eyther of them, Stampe them togither wth Swynes

then Seethe it, and put thero a quantitie of waxe and
Incense and straine it, and cast therin a little quicksilver,
and then it is good agaynst Scabbes.

Vanguentum viride how it is made /

Take the Rootes of Sellendyne, of Coltsfoote, The leaves of
Scabious of Alledillula & of Flos Campi, of eyther a handfull
and washe them Cleane, stampe them and put them into Oyle
Olyffe mixe them well togither in a morter and put therto
a lj of Sheepes Tallowe, and put them into an Earthen pott
well closed t noe ayre come out of it, And sett it vp in a place
& soe lett it stand ix dayes. And then take it out of the pott
and put it in a fayre pann and sett it on a Charcole fyre,
Boyle it well & stirr it wth a slice, and whiles it is hott presse
and sett it agayne on the fyre, and put half a quarter of
meede waxe in smale peeces, and when it is moulten put
therto one Ounce of ffrankencense, of Masticke and verde-
grese of eache halfe an ounce all three made in smale
pouldre, and stirr them well togither untill they be dis-
solved, then sett them downe and put therto half an Ounce
of Aloeium Epaticum well pouldered and resolved in warme
Oyle. This Oyntment is good for all woundes and
will put awaye dead fleshe and increase newe fleshe,
and make them whole and sound. /
