How to make Vnguentum called Vade mecum. /

Take the iuyce of Sellendyne Redd Dock, ffumetorie,
Scabious and Borrage of eyther of them a quarterne.
Lytherge, Ceruse, Evuscum, of eyther halfe an ounce, mixe
them wth the Lyes of Vinegre & bores grease in lieke quantitie,
and sett them over the fyre, and when they doe seethe put
yo foresaid poulder therto, and stirr them well over the fyre
wth a Slyce. Then take it from the fyre and put it in
boxes, it is good for Scabes and Vncums & suche lieke.

Flos vnguentoru.

The manner of makeinge flos Unguentorum

Take a pound of ffrankencense and Rosen togither, of Virgin
waxe of posine halfe an ounce, of Masticke, of deere
Suett, & two drames of Campher, melt what wilbe
moulten and make poulder of the residue, Boyle them
well togither over a cleane fyre, strayne it thoroughe a strong

Clothe





Clothe into a pottle of white wyne, and boyle altogither, then 2
Coole it a whyle, Then put therto    of Turpentyne & stirr it
altogither and lett it stand till it be cold, Then part the Gomm
from the wyne, and seare yo clothe wth it, and make Playsters
And it will heale old wound and newe wound, ffor of all entreit
this is the most clensinge, Souldringe, and will gather and heale
the fleshe more in a night then an other Treatie dothe in a
Monethe, for it will suffer noe corruption in a wound, nor noe
dead fleshe to ingender therin, And it will drawe out yron
Slivers. Thornes or any other evill thinge that is in a wounde,
It helpethe the Prickinge of a Thorne needle, pinn, or any other
thinge that hurtethe the fleshe. It healethe the bytyinge of
venemous beast and wormes, It rottethe and rypenethe all
botches and byles, and healethe all festers Cankers and noli metan-
geres, It drawethe out all ache from the Liver, It helpethe the
Swellinge of the genitories, and all the members of a man
It helpethe the Emerode and other Paynes and diseases
This hathe bene proved and approved. One Mr Arden
wch was an excellent Chirurgion, in his tyme, did many
good Cures by the same.

Oyntmt

Vnguentum Apostolicon wch is good to Soulder Bones
togither, and to make the Skinn of woundes smothe and
even, And is good alsoe for the swellinge of the Spleene
for grievances of the brest wch come of hurt, brusing
or falling. Is made after this manner followinge.

Take half a lj of Pitche, halfe a li of Colofony, Of Galbanum
Serapyne, Armoniack, Apopenac of eyther half an Ounce yf
it be in Sommer put therto iij ounces of waxe, and in Winter
ij ounces and make it in this wise. Take    of Vinegre and sett it
over the fyre, put therto yo pitche, Galbanu, Serapyne and
Armoniack, and melt them altogither, then put one dropp therof
into cold water, and yf it wepe of redd Swart colour, then put in
yo Colophony, smale pouldred or halfe an ounce of Masticke, halfe
an ounce of Olibanum, and mingle all these togither over a fyre,
& mixe therwth an Ounce of Terpentyne, and clense it thoroughe
a Clothe into cold water. Then Annoynt your handes wth Oyle,
and wringe out all the water, and soe keepe it. j li valet 30







An other Oyntment good to heale a cutt
and good for broken bones soe t the said oyntmt.
may come wher the sore is, for it will Suffer noe
wound to heale till it be surelie healde at the ground,
and it will have awaye the brused fleshe, & the blood,
And it is made in this manner.

Take Smallage, Ribbworte, Planten, watersuc leaves of eyther
in lieke quantitie, and a quantitie of daysie leaves and of
hearbe of Grace, Stampe them altogither wringe out the
iuyce and put it in a pann. And put therin fyne wheate
flower & stirr them altogither uppon the fyre, then put therto
a quantitie of may butter and stirr them well togither
And that will make a verye good Oyntmt. Laye it in
a wound & laye thevppon a Playster of some good Treate;
And vse this for it hath bene well approved. /

Vnguentum Appiu /

Oyntmt.
Take the iuyce of Smallage of Planten & of Morsus diaboli
halfe a pynte of all of them, of wheate flower iij ounces
of honey a quarter of a li. boyle all these togither till
they be thicke, then put therto a quantitie of Terpentyne,

Vnguentum Mundificatum

Take Perosine, waxe Oyle oliffe of eyther of them an
ounce, Boyle altogither till it be well then put therin an
ounce of Terpentyne. /

Vnguentum ad earnem regenerandum

Take two Ounces of Clarified honey, an ounce of Terpen-
tyne, a dragme of Aloes and Myrrha & a scruple & halfe
of Verdegrease, & of mill flower dust almost half soe
muche to thick it wthall and a little Bolearmoriacke,
to culler it. This Oyntmt is good to clense wounde &
to make fleshe to growe.





Vnguentum pro Dolore in Carne & in ossibus. &c. 3

Take Sage Hearbe Grace, Lawrell leaves and Absinthium Stampe
them altogither wth May butter and put them into a pott &
lett it stand ther nyne dayes, Then sett it over a fyre &
put frankencense therto, and stirr it well, and put in in boxes
and keepe it for a Soveraigne Oyntment for Aches.

An other good Oyntment.

Take an old Gander that is fatt and pull of all his feathers
and pull out all his Entrall reddye to be rosted. Then take
a fatt Catt, meade waxe Salt and Honey of eyther of them in
lieke quantitie, then Chopp them Small togither and therwth
fearse the Gander, and sow him fast agayne. Then putt
him vppon a Spitt and rost him till he be tender. Save the
droppinge therof for it is a pretious Oyntment for all Owt
and Cankers.

Vnguentum Geneste

Take the flowers of Broome and Stampe them wth May butter
Oyntmt and lett it stand all night and on the morrow boyle them
well togither and straine it thoroughe a Canvas Clothe
And keepe it for a Soveraigne Oyntmt for the Raynes of
the backe, and for Aches in other places

Vnguentum album

Take meate Oyle as much as you will and put it in a basen
and Sprinkle theron vinegre, and stirr it wth a slice till the oyle be
fayre and cleare, the fourthe part of as muche vinegre as of oyle,
Then take lythurge of leade and stampe it Smale and put it thorough
a clothe vnto the oyle a litttle quantitie therof and stirr it togyther
Then take white lead a ... to a Gill of oyle and stirr it well togither
And take as muche Seruse fyne searsed and put therto and
stirr it well. Then take a Cattle bone such as goldsmithes doe
vse and put therto and stirr it altogither in the sonne, till it be
white for the longer it is stirred the better it is, and wilbe a good oyntmt
But yf you will have it fyne put therto Campher lythurge of gold

and of silver, oyle olyffe, oyle of Lynseede, oyle of Roses, This
Oyntment is good agaynst all hott sores, Scabes, Tetters
Cankers, and all manner of woundes. /

An oyntment called Nerval riall

Take a handfull of Sage of Vertue that beareth noe flower,
Rosemarie Sage Thyme, Cammomyle, Garden Mynts Arthi-
mesia, hearbe Grace, Nepp, Costmarie, Saven, Lawrell leaves,
Red Nettle, Mallowes, Smale mallowes, Hollyhock, wallworts
Cliver Bittyne, Water Cresses, and Lavender of ether of
these a handfull stampe them altogither and put therto
iiiijli of may butter Broke grease and Bores grease,
of eyther jli and a pynte of oyle oliffe, and when the hearb
be well stamped then put therto yo butter and grease. Then
putt it in a pann & sett it over a fyre, till it be well melted
and mixed togither, Then put therto e oyle oliffe and
boyle them altogither well, And then take it from the fyre,
& presse out the iuyce into an other Pann, & put therto a
pynte of white wyne, and seethe them togither till they
be greene. Then Coole it and Clense it thoroughe a Canvas
Clothe and lett it stand ix dayes. Then take ij ounces of
mastick, ij Ounces of ffrankencense and ij ounces of meade
waxe cutt in Smale peeces & put therto. Then sett it
agayne over e fyre & boyle it agayne till it be well
mixed. Then straine it agayne and putt it in boxes
well stopped And this is the best makeinge of Nerve
oyve. Whiche is a Soveraigne Oyntment for Ache
Brouses broken bones, or for Shronken Synnews pbatu

An other Soveraigne Oyntment for Ache

Take Sage, hearbe Grace, wormewoode and Lawrell leaves and stampe
them altogither wth May butter, And putt all into a pott, and lett it
stand nyne dayes fast closed, Then sett it over a fyre & stirr it well
togither, And put therto fyne poulder of ffrankencense and boyle
them well togither. Then Coole it and straine it & keepe it in
boxes for a Soveraigne medycyne for ache, well pved. /





An other good Oyntement, for all manner of Bruses / 4

Take Planten, Ribbworte dayses, wth the Rootes a good quantitie of
all stampe them and seathe them in claryfied May butter. Then straine
it and keepe it for it will heale any brouse t is curable. /

An oyntement for Ache

Take flowres of brome, Penyriall, Thyme, Mounten, and Mallowes
by even portions Stampe them wth May butter, and put them into
an earthen pott, and lett it stand Twentie dayes in a dunghill
fast stopped, then take it out and frye it, and putt therto deare suett
and stirr them well togither and straine it, for it is good for the Ache
and for e Goute, proved.

An Oyntemente for all greife in e Veynes and in broken
bones. /

Take fowre sponefull of Aqua Vitae iiij sponefull of the gall
of an Oxe, Seathe these togither in a pann till they beginn
to ryse, Then sett it from the fyre and when it is cold annoynte
the sore therwth. Then strike a plaister of Oxecrocion vppon leather
and laye it therto. /

Oyntmts An Oyntement good for the frenche Pockes and for him
that us burnt wth a Queane, and for Pyles.

Take Verdegrece one pennyworthe, Oyle of Bayes sixe
penyworthe, quicke Silver, an Ounce, and beate them together
till they looke blewe. Then putt it in boxes and keepe it close
and annoynte the grieved place, therwithe, eveninge and
Morninge, ffor a burninge spread the oyntmt vppon a browne
paper and laye it to the sore and it will heale it, pbatum
And for the Pyles annoynte them therwth and it will heale pbatum.

Vnguentum Imperiale made as followethe:

Take jli of Perofin ij. oz. of Galbanum and iiijli of oyle oliffe, boyle
all of them togither till they be well mingled then lett it Coole and
put it into boxes, and it heales all manner of wound newe and old.





Vnguentum Sanatiuum

Take Burnett, dauke, Tormentill, Croyser, Pigle, Sanicle, hearbe
John, hearbe Robert, herbe Water, Comfarie, white Bottle dayseyes,
the Cropes of hempe, & nettle Red Bryer, Red Cole, mader
Spurge Maior et Minor, Violett, Sowethistell, growndeswell
Virga Pastoris, wilde Tasell, medowsote, Crowesope. flos
Campi, Brome flowres, Bitterne Tansie Sauge, Sutherwode
Yarrowe, Strawberrie whyte Pimpernell, Philago, weyhore,
and Verveyne of all these in lieke quantitie Then take as
muche Avence as is of all the other herbes, and all these herbes
must be gathered in the monethe of may or els before Mydsomer,
Stampe all togither and put therto newe made May
Butter, well mixed togither, and sett all in a Cleane Vessell
Nyne dayes, or more till they be hore, Then frye it in a
pan and strayne it thoroughe a Canvese Clothe, and lett
it Coole, and when it is cold, open it wth some thinge and lett
out the water that is in the bottom. Then Rechafe it agaynst
the fyre and coole it and put it in Boystes, hereof a wounded man
must drinke first and last as much as a pease and t will heale a
wound wthout any plaster, Albeit laye a Red Cole leafe to the
wound or a redd Bryer leafe and change it allwaye when
you give him drinke, and it will heale it shortlye. /

Vnguentum Dialtea is a hott oyntmt Called Dewtie it molyfiethe
neshethe & clensenthe all hard cold Sores & mostethe them that be
drye namelie in the Brest as Impostumes in the brest or in the longes.

Take e rootes of white mallowes, ijli of fenecreeke, jli of Linseede, di:li of Marshe
mallowes dij lj of oyle oliffe, iiijli of waxe, j.li of Terpentyne, Galbanum,
gumm of ere of eyther of them ijli.  di oz j of Colophium, pyz rosenum,
Rosenum of any of them joz. Beate all these togither and put them into a li
of fayre water viij dayes, and on the ixth daye boyle them on the fyre till
it wexe thick. Then put them into a Pockett and put thero a little hott water
to doe awaye the inglumore, and presse the pockett betwene two stones,
and take to it jli of Slate Coole and boyle it wth iiijli of oyle of oliffe
till the third part be wasted, and after that put in the wexe Turpentyne Gum
de Ere, Galbanu, the poulder of Colophinu & of Rosen and when all these
thinges be entyred togither sett it on the fyre till a droppe therof
will stand vppon yr nayle, for then it is well & when it is cold put it in
Boxes.

An other makinge of Dewtie./

Take Brock ffoxe, Capons, hennes, dogg and Bores grease and the
marrowe of a fore legg of a horse, the Suet of a deere, and Sheepes
Tallowe of eache in lieke quantitie, Ratt, Catt & Hares grease
Then take the iuyce of Viz. Mallowe, Rubarbe, Morrell, Comferie,
daysey, herbe of Grace, Maithe, Planten, Haros, Mattfellon, dragance,
wyld nepe, and frye altogither in a pann wth all e said grease, and
when it is well sodden wringe it thoroughe a stronge clothe, & put it in pott
of stone, It is hott and moyst, of e nature of fyre, & is verie Softe.






How to make Oyle of Roses. / 5

Gather a li of Roses t be full blowen earlie in the morninge when
the dewe is vppon them and Clipp of all the white of them and
put them in a glasse wth oyle of the greenest oliffe that you
can fynde well medled togither and soe stoped that noe ayre
can gett into it. Then sett it in the sonne Twentie dayes
and stirr it everie daye, then strayne it thoroughe a peece of
Canvas from the growndes of the Roses, and put it into a glasse
and soe keepe it in the sonne other xxtie dayes and then
it is well made

An other waye to make oyle of Roses. /

Take yo Roses and e Oyle by equall portions of waighte, yo Roses
must be Smale Shred, putt them togither in a glasse wth water vpp
to the neck, and lett stand soe close Stopped duringe the space
of two Monethes, and everie daye stire it and stopp it sure agayne
and at the two monethes ende straine it thoroughe a Canvas
and put awaye all the reffuse of the Roses. Then keepe it well
in a glasse, for some men saye that this is the best makeinge
of Oyle of Roses. /

A waye how to washe Oyle Oliffe, to make it more Cold and
Savorie. /

Take a vessell wth a smale Mouthe and put therin oyle and water
a lieke quantitie, then stopp the mouthe close and shake them well
togither a good while, and sett the mouthe downeward, and
soe lett it stand and rest an howre & more, then vnstopp it beneath
and lett the water runn out, and when the Oyle comethe then
stopp the mouthe and turne the vessell and put in more water
and doe therwth as you did before, and doe soe iij or iiij tymes
as neede requirethe. /

Gather a li of Cowslop flowres when they be in their season and putt
them in a glasse and putt to them oyle Oliffe t is cleane and neate oyle
as muche as the flowres may be hidden in the oyle. and lett them
lye therin till these herbes followinge may be gathered, viz Calamint
hearbe John, Sage, Egremonie, Suthernewoode, wormewoode, Pulial
riall, Lavender, Peritorie, Rosemarie, Cammomell, heyroff Peletorie
of spayne, Lawrell leaves, e flowres of Lyllies, of eache one a handfull

wch must





wch must be gathered in the monethe of June, Grynd
them smale in a stone morter, then take the flowres of
the Cowslopes out of the oyle, and wringe them softlie wth
cleane handes and put them into e morter wth the other
hearbes, and grynd them well togither, then laye them a
steepe in whyte wyne a daye and a night, then take them
easelye out of the wyne, and boyle them on a soft fyre,
wth oyle oliffe of the quantitie in sight of the same hearb
and lett them boyle till the water and wyne be wasted,
and then it shalbe be knowne when it is well. Take
a Sponefull of the liquor t is in the pott bottom, and
if you see noe water in the bottom of the Spone then
it is boyled ynoughe, Then take it from the fyre &
let it stand till it be nighe cold, soe that you may handle
it, Then stronglie straine it thoroughe a Canvas bagg
and put this oyle in pewter or glasse vessell & and it
will keepe well three yeares. This oyle is good
agaynst Cowtes, Bruses, all maladies that art in
the Sinewes pceedinge of cold gowte or Palsey, for
fractions of bones Ache in the ioynt. It must be
made in the ende of May or Mydest of June. /
If you put therto these herbes over and besides it is the
better and will last twentie yeares. Baulme,
herbe Robert, Origanu, Englishe Madder, Thyme
hillworte, Red mynt Horse Mynt, Isope, Horhownde
Neptryall of eyther of them a handfull, & two handfull
of Ambrose and put them a mongst the other hearbes,
and dresse them as aforesaid. In sommer tyme you
shall annoynt yo griefe agaynst the sonne, and in
winter agaynst the fyre, and after the annoyntinge
e sore shalbe Covered wth wooll t was never vsed,
and warme it well agaynst the fyre for this
Oyntment is of greate vertue as hathe bene well pvd.





An Oyle for the head. / 6

Take Margarum, Sauge, Lavender, hearbe of Grace, the tender
leaves of the baye tree and Camomell half a handfull of eyther
of them. boyle them in white wyne till they be tender. strayne
them from the wyne & braye them smale. Then boyle them in oyle
Oliffe halfe a quarter of an howre, and stirr it well for burneinge
and lett it stand a ripeninge viij dayes. Then make it mylke
warme and straine it thoroughe a strayner, and therof you shall
have a fayre oyle. Take a little therof at once and make it
mylke warme, and therwth annoynte ye sore head eveninge and
morneinge. And it will helpe that and other diseases. /

An Oyle to heale a wounde, and for broken bones soe that
the Oyntment may come vnto the sore. It will suffer noe
wound to heale but first by the ground, and it will avoyde dead fleshe
and evill blood, and keepe wound cleane.

Take smalleche, Ribworte, Planten, and water Suckles, daysie leaves
in lieke quantitie, and a little hearbe of Grace, Stampe them and wring
out the iuyce and put the iuyce in a pann, and put therto fyne wheete
flower, and sett it on the fyre and lett it be well stirred and put
therto a quantite of May butter and stirr it well, and it wilbe
a Suddye Oyntemente. Then take Lynte and the Oyntmente
togither, and laye it to the wound, and laye a Playster of Trete
thervnto, and soe lett it lye till neede be to remove it and soe conty-
nue wth the same till it be whole.

How to make an oyle for a Sore

Take a pynte of white wyne, & halfe a pynte of oyle Oliffe, and put
it togither then take Rose Campen, Grownd Ivie, Burr leaves, orpen,
Bittenye, Stampe them in a morter, and take the iuyce of them
& iij Spoonefull of Turpentyne, & honey and put it in to e
wyne and lett it seethe togither halfe an howre till it be well myn-
gled, Then take it of & put it in a glasse, and anoynte e Sore
or take Lynt & wett it in the Oyle, & laye it to the Sore, with
a Plaster, And yf you will have yo Salve a Sore drawer
take this oyle and the Salve            and melt them to-
gither vppon a Chafinge dishe till they be thicke, and put lynt
into it, and when it is cold chafe yo salve in a dishe wth a knife & stirr
it vp & downe a good while, And it wilbe a pure Salve and drawe merve-
lous well, but take half soe much of the yo oyle as of the Salve,
for yo Salve must be more then yo oyle when you melt them.





To make an Oyle for any Sore that is in greate
heate Ache or Swellinge.

Take Nightshade, Planten, Elder, Howselieke Rosemarie or e flowres or
e leaves of eache of these herbes two handfull, but let the moste part
part be of Planten greate & small, and seethe them in Swynes grease
or May Butter, butt stampe the herbes first before you put them in.
Then let them seethe till e Oyle be greene and take awaye e Skum from
it as it ryseth and when it is well sodden strayne it and put it
vpp. Then put vnto it one ounce of puer oyle of Roses yf it be for e
Ache onlye If it Swell put to e Oyle a handfull of hollyhoke leaves
& Mallowe leaves.

ffor to make an oyle to heale a Cutt alone

Take Planten Small & greate, Bitteny, Sellendyne, valerian, orpen, howselieke,
nightshade, Grownsell, woodbyne leaves, motherworte Mallowes, Sickle-
worte & Tatsane. Seethe all thes wth swynes grease but let them not seethe
drye, and when they be well sodden straine them, and take the iuyce, and
put theryo honey Turpentyne, Mastick white ffrankencense & oyle of Roses a
little Rosen & virgins Waxe. If you make much oyle, after the quantitie of
yr oyle temper yo stuffe viz. If you make of the herbes a pynte of oyle greene
then take of Mastick & white ffrankencense in poulder ij: ounces, waxe and
Rosen ij ounces, & put these into e oyle and Skum it cleane, when you melt it,
and when you have Skummed it, put to it of honey ij. ounces oyle of Roses
ij ounces, & of Turpentyne iij. ounces, and let them seethe agayne & straine it,
And if you make it pure it wilbe pure and greene. And wth a little
lynte laye it on e Sore, and it will heale it.





To make Tretis for all manner of Sores, bothe white,
redd and greene Tret

Take j li of Wax, a li of Rosen, and a Penyworthe of Oyle Oliffe,
relent them on the fyre, and put therto the Oyle and boyle them
altogither, and take it from the fyre and then it is white Treate
And yf you will have the other collored treat, then devide your white
Treate into three part, and to the second part put a penny-
worthe of redd lead and temper them well togither, and that
will make the Redd treate wch is colder than any of the other
by reason of the redd lead. And yf you will have it greene
put therto a part of this greene Oyntment that hereafter
followethe, for e Coloringe therof is more holsome then verdigreece
for a sore, for this Oyntement is good to laye on a Playster next
to the sore for it will drawe out broken bones, and is soveraignly
good for many other thing and is made as followethe:

Take redd Wort greate daysies, Osmund, Polepodie, Bugell
of eache a lieke quantitie, and as muche of Avence as of
all the other, and put therto pigle Sanicle and selfhearle
a lieke quantitie, and stampe them altogither Smale in a
morter. Then put therto halfe soe much freshe grease as all e
hearbes doe waye, and stampe altogither till they be mixed,
Then frye them in a Pann and sett it in a close Vessell fowre
dayes and then frye them agayne till they be thoroughe hott,
and then straine it thoroughe a clothe, and lett it stand till
it be cold. And lett out the water & frye the thicke agayne and
put it in boxes. This is a pretious Oyntement for all Sores
and it may be longe kept and wth this Treate you may color an
other Treate in winter and make it greene. And this Treate
should be made in Sommer, and put therto a quantitie of deere
suett, for it wilbe soe much the better. /

To make Apostolicon, wch is a verie good Treate. /

Take wormewoode, Waybread, Smallege, Centorie, e Roote of Osmund
in lieke quantitie, and as muche of Egremonie as of all the other,
washe them Cleane, stampe them, and putt them in a newe earthen Pott.
& put therto a pottle of good vinegre, and seathe them to a Quarte,
straine it, and putt e Liquor into e pott, and sett it over the fyre agayne,

Putt therto an Ounce of mead waxe, that is melted in womans milke,
An Ounce of Allam Smale Pounded, an Ounce of blacke Pitche
an Ounce of Masticke smale pouldred, an Ounce of Galbanum vn-
pouldered, an ounce of ffrankencense Smale pouldred, halfe a qur
of Terpentyne a quarterne of prosin, but not the Terpentyne
in till the rest be boyled and relented, and when you take it from
the fyre then put in the Terpentyne, and stirr them altogither
till they be well mixed and when the Terpentyne is melted
then straine it into a cleane vessell, and put therto as
much of the Roote of wallworte by weyght as is of the Smallage
and mixe all well togither.

To make Terpentyne for Treaties.

Take three quarters of cleane Rosen, a quarterne of Perosin,
halfe a li of Oyle Oliffe that is greene, make Poulder of e
Rosen and prosin, and putt the oyle into a cleane pann and
put therto the Rosen and profin, and sett them on a Charcole
fyre, and boyle them softlye, and not hastelie, and as soone as
the Rosen and prosin are relented, and well medled with
the Oyle then sett it from the fyre & lett it Coole till the
greate heate be past, then straine it and keepe it. /

The princypall of all others called Gratia Dei,

Take Bittanie Verven, hearbe Robert, hearbe Walter
wayebread Smallage, Osmund, Orpin Egremonie,
flos Campi Bugle Robworte, Chresworte a lieke quantitie
of eache viz an handfull, Rootes, Stalkes, Leaves flowres
as they growe. Washe them cleane and grynd them smale
in a Morter, and after put them into a pott, and
putt therto a pottle of good white wyne, and cover
well the pott, seethe it softlie on the fyre, till halfe
be wasted. Then take it from the fyre and lett it stand
xxiiij tie howres, and sett the pott over the fyre agayne till
it be reddie to seethe, then take it from the fyre and
straine it soe as you may have all the substance of the
hearbes, then washe cleane the pott wth white wyne &
sett it over e fyre agayne, wth the same liquor and lett it

Seethe.





seethe agayne; Then take half a li of mead waxe cutt into smale
peeces and cast into the pott, and stirr it well wth a slice, and 8
stirr it softlye almost a quarter of an howre, and then sett it
from the fyre and put therto an ounce of Mastick, halfe a
li of Rosen, three ounces of Perosin, halfe a lj of franken-
cense well beaten to pouldre, and take a li of Terpentyne,
halfe a li of Spanishe Cod, and putt it all into the pott, &
stirr it well till it be cold; Then take a pynte of vinegre
and a pynte of the iuyce of the said hearbes, and of
womans milke, and of the iuyce of Selendyne, of eyther
an ounce, and put it in a glasse till next morninge.
Then take out all the moysture that is in the pott and take
the vinegre, e womans milke, and the iuyce of the hearbes
that is in the glasse, and mixe them altogither and
putt them into the pott, Sett it agayne over the fyre,
and lett it seathe softlye a whyle, Then take it from the
fyre and lett it Coole till you may suffer yo hand therin,
Then take out the Treate, worke it in yo hand agaynst
the fyre till it cleave to yo handes, for e more it is labored
the better it wilbe. And this foresaid Gratia Dei
is the most pretious Treate, for it will heale all
manner of wound that may be healed wth any manner
of Treate. And yf any wound be to soone healed and
rankle wthin. Laye this Treate to the place it it will
open the wound agayne, and heale it agayne, yf the
Plaster be removed twice a daye in winter and thrise
in Sommer, and at everye tyme you must washe the wound
wth white wormewood wyne, and yf you have noe white wyne
then take Red wyne and at everie tyme you remove the
playster wype it, for it will serve twise layeinge to. And
this Treate is verie good for Swellinge and ranklinge
of wound, for the Canker and for the fester, for it is a
Treate, Attractative, Mittigative, Consolidative Sanative
Mundificative, and may be accepted as well as the best
Treate that can be made, wth mans handes, And therfore
it is called Gratia Dei, t it to saye the Grace of God helpethe
all thing.





how to make Oxicrocion wch is a yellow Treate,

Take deares Suett and melt it in a Pann of Earthe,
Them take a portion of Rosen a greater quantitie of Mastick
of Olibanum, Lythurge of Gold, and of Silver, and of Virgin
waxe a lieke quantitie and a little oyle Oliffe. Seathe them
well togither, and then take e vessell from the fyre, and Coole
it a little, and put therto the poulder of Saffron and the
yolkes of Egg and stirr it till it be cold. And this a good
Treate and best healpinge and drawinge, and it is good for
broken bones, and for all manner of Aches in everie Lymm,
It loosenethe hard Impostumes, And therfore old Chirurgions
of Salernum vsed noe other plaister but this only in all manner
of Causes.

An other good Treate. /

Take good fatt Bacon melt it in a Pann and lett it stand, and
when e Fatte us fallen into e bottom powre out the greace into a
Vessell and lett it Coole, Then take Three li of Rosen a li of
waxe, a li of Bacon grease, a li of Sheepes Tallowe, a qte
of Gumm Galbanum, and lett e gumm lye in a little Luke
warme water xxiiijtie howres vntill it be relented. Then
breake yo Rosen Smale into a pann, breake the waxe unto
the Rosen and put therto the Tallowe, grease, and e water
the gumm was in, and sett it over the fyre. Put therto a qrn
of good frankencense, halfe a qrt eof Masticke, a qrter
of pofsn fyne pouldred & boyle them till they be relented
& well mixed togither. Then take vij pennye wayghte
of Verdegrease, ground Small on a morter stone, Cast
it into the Pann and stirr them well togither halfe a qurt
of an howre. Then take it from e fyre and put it in a
bagg of Canvase, and put a little water in a bason,
& stirr it in the bottom, and hold over the bagg wth the Treate
& lett it runn into the bason and presse out the treate wth
a payre of Tong and when it is cleane thoroughe Take
a fether and put a waye the frothe aboue, and soe lett it stand
all one night, and on the next morninge take out the treate &
dresse it accordinge to you liekeinge, for it is as kyndly a made
Treate as yf it should be on warrantise.

A good Treate to Close a Wound and heale it /

Take e whites of Egg Rawe honney, and Rye meale a lieke quantitie
and take as much of more of the Iuyce of Marche as is of the other
and putt therto the iuyce of Comferie & of Violetts, and seethe
them togither and make it vpp. /

An other Treate good to open a wound, to clense it, to
take awaye the Ache, and heale it, and alsoe to take awaye
the dead fleshe t is cankred and corrupted. /

Take the iuyce of wallworte, Absinthium, the Croppes of redd
nettles and of Marche ana by even portions and weight, And
of the whites of Egges somewhat lesse, and putt therto rye meale,
& seethe it till it be thicke. And yf e wound be deepe put yo
Treate on a Clout and laye it on the sore, and yf it waxe drye
renewe it wth the iuyce of Marche

To make a good Plaster to heale all wound wthout Tent.

Take white waxe, Rosen, Turpentyne and oyle of Roses of eache iij ounces. Then
melt them & take of e juyce of Valerian, Orpen, Planten, Wintergreene
Woodbyne leaves and Bitteny of eache of them ij ounces, or els stampe m & seethe
them wth Swynes grease or Sheepes Suett till it be greene, Then straine it &
put to e waxe. Then put vnto it ffrankencense and Mastick made all in fyne
poulder and lett them seethe togither a Walme or two, & lett it be cold. And to
make yo Salve fayre and greene take e Sape of Nightshade & put to it

ffor to heale hurts and wound .

Take Mallowes Planten and Bitteny and seethe them well and when they
be well sodden stampe them, then take old Barrowes grease and mixe wth the
iuyce, & then take a little Virgin waxe, & poulder of white ffrankencense
and melt wth the iuyce and grease & Skum it cleane. Then straine it.
Then put Barlie meale to it. Yf it be full of Corruption then
put the Bitteny vnto it, else take it not, but e Mallowes & the Planten
And this is a reddye healer pved. / .





How to sett broken
bones & cure them:

For Broken bones hae they may be sett and Cured /.

Take e Lymm t is broken & laye it on an eaven borde, and washe it
wth warme water, then sett the bones togither. Then take the
glare of Egges & fyne wheate flowre made in a plaister on a
cleane Clothe, and bynd it to e Lymm tt is broken. and lett
it lye ther Nyne dayes and ix night before you remove it.
And give e Patient to drinke pettie Consound, Osmund
Comfarie, & Auence firt and last wth wyne and Stale Ale,
And at the Nyne dayes ende, take the same hearbes, stampe
them and frye them in freshe grease, And laye it in a plaister
to the hurt as hott as the patient can Suffer it. And lett
him drincke Egremonie and dittanum three tymes and he
shalbe hole. This is pved. /

A Redd Poulder that will Stanche blood, Soulder
bones broken & the fleshe and the Skinn.

Take an Ounce of Comfarie dryed in poulder, an ounce
of Bole Armoniacke Three Ounces of Corall, as much
of Colophonie, ij ounces of Sandragon, ij ounces of
Mumma, make poulder of all & keepe it as pretious,

An other Poulder that wch in two dayes will soulder
a wound togither thoughe it be never soe brade

Take Olibanum Sarcacole, ana, halfe an ounce of Aloes
ij ounces make poulder of them togither & strowe it vppon a
wound, & it will close it. Alsoe the Poulder of a Hart horne
burnte, Closethe a wound, and puttethe awaye evill humors,
and dryethe the wound.

An other Powder

Take Verveyne and drye it, and make a poulder therof fyne
Sersed, and laye it to a wound that is newlie hurte, & bleedethe
and it will stanche the blood, and heale the wound on warrantise,
It hathe been often proved. /

An other Powlder to stanche blood. /

Take Jasper Corall and Roche Allume, make powlder of
them and put a quantitie therof into a wound that bleedethe
and it will stanche it presentlye. /





An other thinge to stanche blood. 10

Take a quantitie of Bole Armoniack as much of Sanguis draconis
make fyne poulder of them and mixe them wth the glare of
Egges, laye it to the wound and soe lett it lye Two dayes &
it will cleave faste vnto the sore. Then to loosen it that it bleed
not when it shalbe taken of. Take Oyle of Roses and annoynte
the Plaister therwth and yt will supple it that you may easelie
take it awaye. /

An other for stanchinge of blood, souldringe of bones
and veynes and healinge of Sores. /

Take Sanguis draconis, Bole, Myrra Frankencense
Masticke, Spruce, Litthurge, poulder of Comfarie, poulder of a
post of an old house yt is called Venult, ana, Make poulder of
them all & cast it on e wound or on the sore, and it will stanche
the blood. /

To make a Poulder to heale a wound and to stopp blood. /

Take Lyturgium Bolarmoniake or Sanguis Draconis or Aloe pateru
Mastick, white ffrankencense or Olibanum and make them all
in fyne powlder and strowe it on fyne Lynte, or wett the lynt
in white wyne, and laye the poulder on it. pved.

An other good Poulder for the same.

Take Bolarmoniack or dragons blood, white ffrankencense or Olibanum
Mastick Lyturgium and the poulder of Cokellshell burned & made all
into poulder.

To make a poulder to heale to clense to stopp blood to keepe
the wound from rottinge to comfor and to Soulder - /

Take Mastick white ffrankecense, dragagante, Bolarmoniacke of each halfe
an ounce, Myrra & Boras of m bothe halfe an ounce, and put vnto it a little
quantitie of e poulder of Arabeke, or else lyturgium, for the Myrre
if you will you may put Gumm Elempanie, Then put to it a little Cam-
pherni poulder, And this will heale a wound verye well. Laye it on
wth Lynte. /





A good drinke for man or woman tt is wounded. /

Take Bugell strawberie leaves, daysie, Comfarie, the Cropes
of redd Cole, Cropes of Redd bryre, Cropes of Osmund,
Cropes of Hempe, Cropes of redd Nettle, Sanicke, Orpyne,
Tansie, Pimpernell, Mouseare, and Violett ana, of eyther
of them one handfull, Of the Cropes and Rootes of Auence
five handfull of the Cropes & rootes of hearbe Robert three
handfull. And the weight of the third part of all these
hearbes in Madder. Washe all these verye cleane and stamp
them in a Morter, and put them in an Earthen pott
and put therto a gallone of good white wyne, and boyle
them altogither till halfe be wasted. Then take it from
the fyre and coole it a little. Then straine it into the cleane
Earthen pann. Then take a pottle of lyfe honey, such as will
runn out of the Combes wthout pressinge, Clarifie it cleane
over the fyre, and put it to the said liquor, soe that you have
of them in lieke quantitie. Then boyle them togither till
the quantitie of the hearbes be wasted. Then straine it into a
glasse, And make this before Mydsommer And let the sicke
drinke therof first and last wth a spoonefull of fayre spring
water that hathe bene sodden, and lett him drink it warme.

To make good Balles for wound, to knitt Sinewes
and Veynes that be brused, and to knitt broken bones
on warrantise. /

Take daysies, Comfarie, & Osemund, the Cropes of Hempe,
the Cropes of redd Cole, the Cropes of redd nettles, the Cropes
of redd Bryre, Sutherwoode Tanseye, Pimpernell, Bitteny
Bugell Sanicle Rodis lig Gold and Crocos, Auence,
and Madder a lieke quantitie by weight of all except of the
madder wherof take as muche as of all the rest. Washe
them cleane and grynd them smale in a morter, and when
they be smale ground and well medled togither, make Ball
therof, the bignes of smale nutt kernell, Couche them on
a fayre bord, and sett e bord wth e ball in some convenient place

wher neyther sonne, wynd nor ayre may come to them, for dryeinge
to fast, and everie daye turne them and rowle them wth cleane
handes, and put awaye the wormes yf any be in them, And
thus preserve them till they be drye and better balles be
not vnder the Skye to knitt and for to heale. /

A drinke for a wound that driveth out of it all Corruption
and healethe it,

Take Sothernewoode, Tansey, Red Coles, Parsley Seede Strawberye leaves
Planten greate & small & hempe seede, seethe all these togither in white
wyne & a little honey & washe the wound wth it and drinck it or els this
drincke. viz Pimpernell, Water Roote or Sotherwoode, Egrimony, Isope,
Tansey, Planten, Strawberrye leaves, Bitteny and redd madderworte
and seethe them in wyne, vinegre and honey, and this drincke Cureth
or healeth a wound by it self wthout other helpe. pved. /

To make a good drincke for all manner of wound
& for an Aposteme.

Take Bitteny Violet leaves, Avence, Scabious, Tormentill, Pimpnell,
Egremony, Buglosse Planten Strawberrye leaves & ffemetorie and still
them all wth white wyne, and put in it a little Sugar when the patient
dothe drincke it, or else chope these herbes and seethe them wth wyne, and
washe the Sore wth this water and laye your salve vnto it.

How Potions made of Hearbes are to be Clarified and
made fitt to be dronken .

Potions for the Sicke are made of the iuyce of such herbes as are
healeinge, and of wyne or Ale sodden therwth, and clarified on e
fyre, and then clensed till it be cleare. And if the iuyce of
the herbes be bitter put therto a little honey. And althoughe
e iuyce be never soe thick, you may make it as cleare as
wyne or water in this wise, viz. Take the whites of Egg
snd the Shelles small broken, and meddle it wth the iuyce
of herbes, And sett it altogither over a softe fyre in a shallow
Pann, but let it not seethe or cast vpwarde, and then the
dregges of the herbes wth the white of the Egg shelles
will hover aboue, and the iuyce underneathe will be as
cleare as wyne or water.





Of woundes in sondrie partes of mans
bodie how they may be cured.

Yf a man be wounded wth a speare of wth an Arrowe if the
wound be deepe how it shalbe cured. And that two manner
of wayes:

The first waye to heale a deepe wound is: To make a Tent
of Linnen Clothe and lett it be smale, that the poynte may
come to the bottome of the wound, and Annoynte it wth the
yellow Oyntement, or wth some other good Oyntment & putt
it into e wound, and laye a playster of Treate theron. Butt
lett yo Tent be soe bigg aboue t it may hold the wound open,
otherwise the wound may heale aboue but not wthin, and that
is verie dangerous, for when such wounds are not perfectlie
healed, but breake out agayne after, Then are they worse to heale
than they were when they were first hurt. And therfore wound must
be healed at the bottom first and then aboue.

The second waye to heale such wounds as are deepe in any place
except the head is as followethe: Take Lard of a Swyne &
make a Tent therof t will fill the wound, annoynte it wth
the yellow Oyntmt. and laye an Emplaister therto made in this
wise: Take wyne, Oyle, honney, Meale, hearbe Grace, Sauge
and vse them in manner of an Emplaister vppon the wound,
and Change it twise a daye dureinge xiiij dayes togither and
ever as the wound healethe soe make the Tent of Lard lesse and
lesse, and soe you may heale all suche deepe wound. /

How to heale a broad wound.

Yf a man have a broad wound in his head, or any other place
of his bodie. Sowe the lippes of the wound togither in iij or
iiij places wth a quarrell needle, & a Silke thrid, & leave a hole
on the nether part of the wound, And make a plaister
of Wyne, Oyle and wheate bread that is not well baked
and boyle them over the fyre, and therof haveing made a plaister
laye it to the wound And after heale it vpp wth the Yellow
Oyntmt. or some other good thinge. And yf the Lippes of
the wound be greate and the place about it be Softe and have
smale pimples, and the wound wax white it is a good token.
Butt





12
But yf the lippes be thinn and he place about it wexethe hard
and black, it be token the Cankeringe. And yf the wound
wexe blacke wthin: Then take a fyne Clothe and wett it in the iuyce
of Sengreene, Mercurie, Penyworte and Henbane, meddle ther-
wth the gleare of an Egg, Oyle of Roses or of Violetts & Sanders,
vse this to the wound vntill it be in good temper.

And yf wormes breed in a wound. Take the iuyce of an
Oxes donge newe made and put it into the wound And
make a plaister of the Oxes donge and laye it ther vppon &
wthin three dayes the wound wilbe Red, and then heale it vpp.

Yf the scull of the head be broken, of yf that a
Peece therof be cutt awaye, how to know whether
the man may be cured yea or noe. /

Wounds of
e head.

Take Pimpernell stampe it and temper it wth water , let the sicke
drinke it, and yf he cast it agayne then he shall dye. /
And in lieke manner the iuyce of Mouseare and the iuyce
of Columbyne and Auense given him to drinke yf it
come out of the wounded he will dye in Short tyme after or els
yf it come not out he shall live. Then yf you fynd him curable
yf the weaknes and feoblenes of the patient may endure it
Take out the broken bones yf any be in the wound and
clense it. Then take a fyne peece of Linnen Clothe that is
smale, cleane and softe, and laye it on the brayne to keepe
the filthe from it. Then laye an other peece in the break-
inge of the Skull, but put the endes of the peeces in such
manner that they may get vnder the skull to keepe the
filthe from the brayne. And the best thinge for this pur-
pose weare a Sponge of the sea, well washed and dryed.
But for want therof, take (as hathe bene said) a fyne linne
Clothe softe and cleane, and when you have stopped well
bothe vnder the broken Scull and in the breache therof, Then
take a fyne Linnen Clothe, and wett it in the glare of Egg and lay
it on e wound and bynd vpp the head. And lett the man wch is
hurt sitt or lye in sych manner that e matter may come out
of the wound. And change it twise in a Winters daye and thrise
in a Sommers. and soe contynue workinge till it be whole. And yf
any corrupt fleshe growe vppon the brayne, Take a gobbett of

Sponge





Sponge of the sea that is first washed and dryed and laye
it theron for it will frett it awaye. And yf ther growe
any evill fleshe on the scull frett it awaye wth Ermadactolo
And when the Skull is well covered heale vpp the wound
wth the Oyntmt Apostolicon. And this manner all such
wound are healed, wher the skull is perished. /

But yf the skull be verie much broken and a smale wound
wthout. Then put in your finger softlie and searche how farr
the skull is wounded, and when you know that. Take a
Razor and cutt the Skinn a Crosse wise, and wth an other
Instrumt part the fowre quarters from the skull, and hold
eache of them togither in manner of a rowle, and yf the
blood be noe impediment yf any bone lye loose take it out,
or els tarrye till the third daye but in the meane tyme
put in everie use the fowre quarters a peece of Linnen clothe
wett in the glaire of Egg And yf the quarters swell &
increase it is a good signe. And the third daye when you
have broken out the bones, Then heale it upp as I sayd
before, And on the Second daye after a man his hurt you
shall laye on the brayne a cleane linnen clothe that is not
wett in the glaire of e Egg.

And if Dura Mater be hurt that clothe that shalbe layd vppo
the brayne, shalbe wett in fayre warme water, for hurtinge
of the brayne, But peradventure the skull is soe broken
as you cannot perceive it for the smalnes of the breache,
and in this case you shall cause the man to hold and stopp his
mouthe and his nose, and to pene his wynd to blowe and
yf the skull be broken then will the wynd come out of the
breache. And yf e wound be soe narrow as you cannot see the breache
then make it bigger wth a Razor as I told you before and
then at bothe endes of the breache in the skull make two holes as
you soe neede, accordinge to the largenes of the breache. Then
cutt awaye as much as is betwixt the two holes and then heale
it up wth Vnguentum Apostolicon as hath bene said, And yf it
chance t a peece of the skull be cutt awaye and hange by the Skinn
& that the Skull be not cutt quite thoroughe to the brayne, Then
part the bone from e skinn and stiche vpp the skinn agayne
and lett therbe the space of an ynche betweene everie stiche,
and beneathe a voyd place must be ordeyned for e matter to
avoyd from e, and Strew theron redd Poulder for that will
Soulder them togither agayne. Then heale them vpp wth some
of e Oyntment afore said. But laye not the poulder therto aboue
nyne dayes or twelve at the most. /





13
And yf soe be the Skull be cutt thoroughe and the peece hange besides on
e Skinn Then slitt the Skinn and take awaye the bone from it, and
sowe the two peeces of the Skinn to the Skinn of the head, and lett
the middle part be open betweene them bothe and laye a soft lynnen
clothe, vppon the brayne Then heale vpp the skull as hath bene
before declared and soulder the sides of the two skinnes to the head
wth the redd poulder.

And yf the Skull be bowed inward wth a stroke Soe much as is Apayred
must be cutt awaye, and then healed as is aforesaid, And when you
will Cutt it awaye make fowre holes a crosse as is before said, and
then Cutt from hole to hole and soe you shall best Save the brayne and
the residue of the Skull. /

And yf the Skull be Shott thoroughe bothe sides wth an Arrowe
and the Arrow cleavethe on the one side. Then take a Razor and
on that side Cutt the Skinn on crosse, and part it from the Skull,
And in the Skull make iiij holes a crosse about the arrow head,
that it may easelie be drawne out, and then drawe out the arrow
head, but drawe out the shafte on the other side first. And then
heale vpp bothe wounds as is before said.

And yf the Arrowe pearce on the one side and not on the other
Then Cutt the skinn a crosse from the Arrow, and part it
from the skull, And make the hole large ynoughe for then
you shall see the skull the better. Then drawe out the
Arrowe and heale vp agayne as is before declared.
And thus verie sufficient and sure good remedies have bene
shewed you for all hurt and wound in the head





If a veyne be cutt in any part of a man, how the blood may
be stainched. Or how Sinowes or Veynes, may be souldred
agayne togither that be cutt in sonder.

Ther are many thinges that will stenche blood of Veynes or wound
Viz. Take the Cropes of redd Nettles but not to much and laye
it on the wound and it will stenche the blood.
The hearbe called Bursa Pastoris will doe the same.
The donge of a Mare or of an Asse, made in poulder layed on a wound.
The warme donge of a Swyne.
The Webb of Ater Coppis.
The Soote of a Chimneye.
Alsoe the Poulder of Vervin fyne sersed, and layed to a wound will
stenche the blood and heale it.
Alsoe the poulder of Sanders bound to a veyne that bleedethe will
presentlie stenche the blood, but it is paynefull for yf have not the better
hart it will make the partie to Sowne.

And yf a veyne be cutt a sonder, Then sowe e endes agayne togither bothe
above and beneathe, wth a silke thrid. But be ware you perishe not the veyne
wth sewinge, and drawe the endes softlie togither wth a thrid for breakinge
the Skinn, soe that the blood may yssue out bothe above and beneathe And
then wett a fyne Lynnen Clothe in the glare of Egg and laye it to the
wound measurablye. Then laye an Emplaister about. If it be in winter
as hathe bene declared in the wound of the head. And yf it be in Sommer
Laye therto e yolke of an Egg till the wound quitter. and then wth the
yellow Oyntmt and other thing t engender fleshe, heale vp the wound.
But yf an Arrow or a Thorne, Silver Pinn or any such thinge sticke in
a Veyne, first pull out the Arrow or whatsoever it be, and sow the veyne,
as before hath bene shewed. And then cast on the red powlder or some
other thinge as is a foresaid. Or els take two ounces of Encense one ounce
of Aloes Epatic One ounce of hares heres smale Cutt, and half soe much
of the glare of Egg as is of all the other, and medle all this togither, and
laye therof a good quantitie on the veynes that are cutt. And lett it lye soe,
till it fall of by it self. And in what place soever of the bodye you laye a
medycyne to restrayne and stenche the bleedinge, Lett it lye ther three
dayes before you remove it. And yf a Veyne be cutt even longe put e
the two sides therof softlye togither wth yo fingers, and then laye e
medycyne to it as is aforesaid. But sowed it not unlesse ther be verie
greate neede, but bringe the brinkes togither, And then make this
medycyne. Take Sarcacole pouldred, Sandragon and e white of
Egg and laye theron. And yf the medycyne wexe drye and hare, &
will not well of, annoynte it wth oyle of Roses or some other thinge that will
relent it.

Yf a synew be Cutt how to sowlder it

Take Angyll watches wch be greate wormes that lye knotted togither
in Sommer morning bruese them and heate them a little over the
fyre in Oyle oliffe, and laye them to the broken synewes and that
will soulder them agayne togither.

Alsoe in this manner you may soulder Synewes togither
Sowe the vttermost skinn of the Synewes togither, then Sprinkle
theron the Redd powlder: and that will soulder them perfectlie agayne.
And yf the wound Swell then make the medycyne I told you before
for swellinge of wound in the head. and laye it therto till the
swellinge be asswaged and abated. but yf the Synew be sticked or prikd
lay therto Emplaister made of Galbanum Armoniacke Serapia, Apopo-
nack alsoe of Sporge Bene flowres and Holyhock. Then Annoynte
the necke and armeholes wth oyle &c.

A Soveraigne Emplaster good and profitable for Synnowes
that be Cutt, hurt or broken.

Take two ounces and a halfe of Colofium two ounces and a halfe of
Rosen, iij ounces of oyle, one ounce of pitche. And of Olibanum, Orpiment,
Allom a lieke quantitie. ij Ounces, and halfe a pynte of wyne
Make powlder of that wilbe made into powlder, and melt the other
and boyle altogither and make a plaster therof and laye it therto,

How to keepe
synnewes
fro e Crampe.

And yf you cannot keepe e Synnew from the Crampe Then Cutt the
Synnew all a sonder, and then it will not ake, and yf it be but somewhat
Cutt or pricked, then it will ake. And yf ther stick in the Synnew

How to draw out

bone t stick in
a Synnew. /

a thorne, Iron, or bone Laye therto the iuyce of Beet or the seede
of Clarie, or of Ditander And yf it be yron laye therto an
Adamant stone, And yf it be a Thorne, Stampe filberd Carnell

The waye how to heale an Arme or a legg yf it be hurt or broken.

Yf the bone be broken and the Skinn be hole on the out side it may be
healed on this wise. Viz. ffirst heale the broken Lymm, and ioyne
the bones togither. Then ordeyne you iij peeces of fyne Linnen
Clothe, and wett them in a glare of Egg, and laye them on every
side of the Sore, Then take a Clothe of iiij fyngers broad &
wett it alsoe in the glare, And bynd the Arme or the legge
therwth about the other, And looke that every Clothe be layed even,

that





that they make noe pitt or dent in the Arme or the legge. Then
ordeyne them Splitters and laye about the Sore, and bynd them
fast wth a stringe, to keepe the lymm even, that wexe not
croked. and soe lett it be bound three dayes, till that the bones
be souldred agayne togither. And yf it swell wth the byndinge,
then washe the legg or the Arme wth warme water, t Hockes,
or Beet or Planten hath been sodden in. And when it is well
washed, annoynte it wth Morcation or wth Deutie, and soe contynue
yo workinge untill it be hole. And in the meane tyme lett the pati-
ent drinke the iuyce of Dasies, Comferie and Columbyne seed
for these things will helpe sowlder the bone togither.

And yf the Arme, or the legg be wounded in the bone or in the Synew
cutt asonder, and yf any Sliver of the bone be left, Take it away
and then sett the bones even togither, and sowe the overmost skyn
of e endes of the Synnew, and then wett a clothe in the glare
of Egg & laye it to the wound and cast some of the Redd powlder
theron to soulder the bones and Synewes togither, Then wett
lynnen Clothes in glare and bynd about the wound, Then rowle
it fayre and wett a clout in glare and byn about it. Then Splint
it and stirr it not till iij dayes be past and then remove it &
renue it, and annoynte the fleshe wth the yellowe Oyntment.
for it reingendrethe fleshe & cast the redd powlder on the Synnew,
& laye it about the broken bone, & soe contynew yo workinge,
And looke therto everie three dayes, And give him such
drincke every daye as will Soulder bones. /

And yf it swell or ake, or if the Synewes shrinke, Then
annoynte it wth dutie all about e sore, or else make an Emplaster
of Hokkes, Beet, ffenegreke & Lyn seede powldered and beaten togither
and layed to the sore. But in any wise soe t it be well bound
or els the bones will not soulder well togither.

But you shall vnderstand that some wound of Armes and Legg
are not curable but deadlie. As yf the brawne of the Arme or his
Synnewes be cutt overtwharte, or a slent, or if the Thighe be cutt
three ynches aboue the knee, it is verie dangerous. And yf the
other part of the Arme be Swollen or wexe blacke and akethe,
That wound is deadlye, meddle not therwth, yf they have not
these Signes they may be cured by God helpe.





15
But in the hurtinge of the Legg lett not e medycyne be to drawinge
vnlesse it drawe over much matter to the sore. And if therbe a longe
wound in the Arme or in any place of the bodie, wthout breakeinge of
bones of Synnewes, or cuttinge of the Synnewes; Then sowe the brink
of the wound togither as before hath bene shewed you, And wth the
Redd powlder and the yellow oyntment you may heale it vpp. /

And yf one be wounded vppon the Liver e Longes, the Stomacke, the
Mydriffe, the Reynes of the backe, or the Throtebole, I counsell you
not to meddle wth them. Nor wth the creepinge veynes in the hamm
yf they be Cutt. ffor all these be incurable wthout the greate goodnes
of God. /

Yf the Ribbes be Cutt or broken they may be sowldred agayne, and the greate
Ropes or gutt yf they be perished or hurt may be healed better then the
smale ones, for it is dangerous healinge of the Smale Ropes and gutt.
And the lights may be Cutt and suffer medycynes for he that is soe hurt
feelethe noe greate payne, but of the receyveinge of the hurt. It is dan-
gerous medlinge wth any of these, for they be mortall. Yf the light be
cutt and come out of the wound, That wch is brused you may Cutt awaye
and heale it and putt it in, and then heale vpp the wound wthout, But
yf the Ropes be cutt and come out of the wound, Yf they be cold: Then take
a Whelpe and slitt him in two, and laye the cold ropes in his bellie
till they have gotten a heate, And then make an elder pype, and lett
it be smale and soe longe that it passe the lengthe of the breache by
an ynche, and put the pype into the rope, and on it sowe the endes
togither at ij stitches or iij and soe leave a greate Space betweene your
stiches t you take out the pype when the endes are sowed togither, And
sowe it in that manner that the superfluitie t is in the rope, may passe
thoroughe the pype wthout lettinge, And clense the intrall of the fylthe
that is in them, and then softlie putt them in thoroughe the wound, and
cast the Redd powlder over e soweinge everie daye till it be souldred togither
and then take out the pype of Elder. And then soulder vpp the remnant
wth the powlder and take out the thrides and heale vpp the wound forthwth.
This workinge is good yf the Ropes be cutt aslent, and not even over-
twhart; Mamnia is good therfore, or els you must slitt a mans wombe wch
is grievous and paynefull for any man to endure, And therfor Chyrur-
gions have by their learninge devised to make potions to drincke that
will bringe a man a sleepe whyles he is a dressinge t he shall feele
noe payne .

And such potions





And such Potions be made in this manner

To cast one in
a sleepe. /
Take the iuyce of Henbane, the iuyce of black Popie
or els the seede of them, the iuyce of Mandragon or
of his fructe, or els the iuyce of Trevy the iuyce of Mulberry
The seede of Letuce and Opiu, Theobaytiu of eyther of them
ana a lieke quantitie ozj beate them togyther in a brasen
morter and sett them in the Sonne betweene Mydsomer and the
Asumption of our Ladie. Then take a Sponge and lett it
drincke vp all the liquor, and laye the Sponge in the Sonne
and lett it drye. And when you will make a man to sleepe,
laye the Sponge on his Mouthe and on his nose till he fall
a Sleepe, And then you may cutt him at your pleasure, And
when you will awake him: Rubb his Teethe and nostrill with
vinegre.

An other potion to bring a man a Sleepe. /

Take the iuyce of White Popie and of black Popie, and
of letuce seede ana: oz j of Opium Theobaytiu oz j make
poulder of them and give it him to drinke, And when you have
done yo Cure, then wake him wth vinegre as aforesaid,
And give him some Confortative to drinke to Cheere him vp.

How to Agomye, good on warrantise to laye to any man
that is hurt or bruised.

Take a quarter of mede waxe, halfe a quarter of Terpentyne,
halfe a quarter of perofsn halfe a qr of Bore grease,
halfe a qr of Bayes Lawrell, halfe a qr of Comyn,
And of oyle of Baye of oyle Oliffe, or Dewtie and of Frank-
encense of eache of them an ounce, putt all these togither
save the poulder of Comyn and Bayes, and put all the
other thing in a cleane vessell and sett it on a Charcole
fyre. Stirr it well wth a Slice till it be melted Then neyther
Streine it nor skome it, but sett it from the fyre and stir
it well till e greate heate be done, Then put in the
powlder and strow it in featlie & stirr it as you would still
papp till all the poulder be in & the matter neare cold,
Soe that you may Spread it on a white leather, And when
it well spread, Take a Lynnen Clothe and laye it aboue.

A Medycyne eyther to be eaten or dronken, good for Festers
Wound and for all open Sores. / and it is made in this wise.

Gather the Seven Grasses wch are good for Festers, of eyther of
them ana: m.f: a handfull of those grasses t lead to the wound,
of everie one a handfull of everie one of those that drawe to the wound
ana: a handfull and liekwise a Nipsenfull of Bittenie, for as
Salt dothe Savore all meates soe dothe Bittenie Savor all oyntment
and Treat. Then stampe all these grasses in a Morter Small
and make balles of them and laye them in a pott the space
of ix dayes till it be well moysted, then putt them in a fayre
pann and putt therto Cleane butter, and boyle them togither,
and putt therto white wyne, And boyle them fast togither & stirr
them well; Then drawe it thoroughe a Canvase into a Cleane
vessell, and stirr it well, Then putt therto cleane wex moulten
or els it would be to weake for to handle, and putt therto a little
honny, And when you will vse this medycyne eate therof as
much as a beane, or drincke it in warme Ale, and laye a bryer
leafe on the Sore.

Of the Grasses. /

These are the five Grasses that are called the five Lances. That lett
into the wound, viz. Ditten, Peluette, Confond media, Pimpnell,
Auence.
These are the five that drawe to the wound, viz. Oculus i Mather,
Bugell, Redd Cole, Orvall.
And these be the seven grasses proper for the Fester viz. Ache,
hearbe Robert, Bugell, Sanicle, Gaveni, Molleyn, Crowsope
obser:  And observe this for a rule that everie man wth open sore, hath noe
hott ffester. But wher the Fester is the fleshe is hard and shining
proud and Chafed. And ther ar two Manners of ffesters.
One is hott an a nother is Cold.

That wch is hott hath a large hole And the cold a narrow,
And these two Festers are devided into three.

1. Wherof one dothe frett the fleshe, and out of that cometh
quitter.
2. The second devidethe the fleshe,
3. And the Third Frettethe e fleshe and Synnewes and the
Joynts and out of that cometh a black quitter, wch is uncurable. /






An Antidotorie of Chyrurgerie wch teachethe
to make Medycynes for all sores of grevances
that belong to chyrurgerie & are to be healed
by that art, and the thinges herein expressed
have innumerable tymes bene well proved
to be true. The whole worke is compiled
into Sixe Chapters. Wherof:

1 The first teachethe Repercussiue Medycynes
for to repell any tumor swelling wexing &c
and they are of two sorts {Symple, made of any one thinge
{by it selfe.
{ Compound, of ij: iij:iiij. or more
thing medled togither.

2 The second is of Resolutives & Maturatives wch are
Medycynes t are to resolue & loosen matters gathe-
red togither & to rypen them & to quitter them
yf neede require.

3 The third is of Mundificatiues wch are clensing medycyns
when the sore hathe well matured, & cast out the
Quitter to make all cleane.

4 The fowrthe is of conglutinatiues, Regeneratiues
or Consolidatiues. That are Medycynes knitting
& fastning the lippes of woundes togither
& to make e fleshe to growe wher neede is
& to close & heale woundes & sores in seasinable tyme.

5 The fift of molificatiues wch are Medycynes t
doe mollyfie & make soft e places t be hard,

6 The sixt is of Cautarizing Medycynes t be
to be burnt, in diverse places when neede requires,

Caput. i.
Of Repercusiue Defensiue Medycynes

Note. It is to be noted that as oft as you intend to make a Repercussion
it is good first to purge the bodie as the humors doe require,
Yff the bodie be stronge and hott then purge it by blood lettinge.
Yf it be feoble, Then purge it wth garsing and wth this medycyne
Take x plumes of Violett v. ounces (but the violett shalbe putt
in at the ende of the boyleinge). Boyled in a li of water, till
halfe be wasted, and putt therto half an Ounce of Tamaridus
as much of Arana, as much of the pithe of Cassia fistula,
Then straine it stronglye thoroughe a Clothe, and put therto
the rynd of Mirabolus Citrinus grownd into fyne poulder
halfe an ounce, And this should be made in the eveninge,
and earlye in the morninge. Then straine it agayne,
Clense it, and give it the patient. /

The Repercussiues or defensiues of the member, may be
put vppon any member of the wch you shall suspect any
corse of Melencolique matter should come, yf it chance soe
that the matter be drawne togither and confirmed to any
manner of Impostume, or els the place be Apostumed
and broken, Then put noe repercussive medycynes
vppon the Sore but about near vnto the beginninge of the
greavance of the Sore, As thus for example if the
payne and griefe be neare vnto the ioynte of the fatte
then laye the medycyne to the legg ioynte.

A Cold Matter properlie shall not be repercussed, but
some tyme it shalbe defended in e beginninge with
purgatious of medycynes in comfortinge e member soe
t ther shalbe noe gatheringe togither of the matter For e
wch Trocisti helpe much wch is made by the Apothercarie in

Medycynes that Comfor a member soe that it shall not receyve
cold matter. viz -

Wormewood, Squenutiu, Sutherwood, Spiknurd, Mastick
Roses and oyle of Roses, Take of these foresaid thing as many
as you will, and put them in an vrynall and put therto Oyle
and boyle them in a Caldron of water, and therwth annoynt
the Sore, Or else boyle them in wyne and make a playster
or make a poulder therof and temper them wth wyne, &
plaster the Sore, These foresaid medycynes serve both
for repercussives and defensives.

The  Second Chapter of Resolutiues & Mataratiues

wch have greate affinitie and agrement in their operation
and workinge, for they be of a temperate heate, and yet
they have a difference: for why Resolutiues that be
looseninge have their Substance and heate tempered wth
softness, for they open the powers and tempately they
drawe out the matter, and Comfort the heate and make
the matter fitt to be drawne forthe and to avoyd out, And
thus yf they fynd, much matter and greate soe that the
poores Suffise not to cast out the matter, then they rypen
Somtymes.

Maturatives be rypeninge medycynes for as much as they
have a temperate heate and a certyne Substance thoroughe
the wch stoppinge the poores they keepe a kyndlie heate
in his place, and soe they mature and rypen the thing
alsoe by little and little till they loosen it. Wherby it
is often found that one kynd of Emplaster dothe some-
tyme loosen and Somtyme ripen that is to saye
resolveth and maturethe.

These followinge are Resolutiues: Viz.

Camamyle, Howslieke & their flowres, Oyle Annett and his
Seede, the wyld Mallowe leaves, Peritorie, Bren Goose
grease, Duck grease, hennes grease, Elder leaves, Hollyhock
Cole leaves and his seede, Storax liquida, Ladanum, Mastick
ysope, Hinda and others





How to make a Resolutive Emplaster. /

Take wyld Mallowe leaves, Cole leaves, Camamell flowres of eyther
a handfull seethe them in runninge water, then grynd them
small, Then take poulder of Anyse seede, and take one part
therof and an other part of Cole seede, & two part of
Brenn or meale, and make Emplaster therof. /

A Resolutive Oyntment.

Take oyle of Camomyll or of Anyse vj.oz. of duck grease oz j
of henes grease oz j of the poulder of Anyse seedes oz ij
of Camamill flowres made into fyne poulder ij ounces
And make Oyntment of all these.

A Resolutive Emplaster.

Take a handfull of Mallowes as much of Colewort leaves,
and the rootes of Ramsies, Seethe them in runninge water,
Stampe them and make Emplaster therof.

To make good Diaquilon that resolvethe Cold matters
and loosenethe and softnethe wher it is hard.

Take fyne flowre, of Lytarge xij oz oyle of Camamill,
of Anet, & of Iring viij oz. the iuyce of wyld mallowes,
of lyne seede, of ffenegreeke, of old dryed figges and
Raysons wth their stones, the iuyce of Gladium, the iuyce of
Spanishe Terpentyne iiij oz Seethe them togither that it
be harder than an Oyntment and softer then a plaster.

Note. And know you that all Emplasters Resolutives, Maturatiues
& Molificatives that is to saye, loosening, Ripeninge and
Softninge, ought to be made in a meane betwixt hardnes
& softnes, for yf they be to hard, then they prick, and
make the matter thicke, and flye awaye and abyde not
on the place. And it is to be considered that some tyme in
resolveinge of a matter Some part therof is softned and
the other hardned wch ought alsoe to be made softe, Therfore
washe the member softlye wth warme water till it wex redd,
and after annoynt it wth an oyntmt of iiij part of oyle and
one part of waxe.

An Other princypall Medycyne to Soften
Scrophlies, That the King evill and kernell To
make them reddie to be resolved:

Take a pound of Oyle in a glasse, and put therto the rootes of
wyld mallowes, and the rootes of wyld nept cleane washt
and scraped of eyther j.oz. Then put the glasse wth the oyle
and the rootes into a vessell of water, and boyle them till the
rootes be somewhat scorched. Then Strayne out the oyle, and
put to that oyle iij oz of wax in Somertyme, and but ij oz in winter
and this makes a Soveraigne Molificative, wth the wch you shall
one daye mollyfie and a nother resolve, or ij or iij dayes as you
shall soe neede. and yf you put therto the x. part of Euserbium
it will make an excellent medycyne.

These be maturatives eache by him self Viz.

The Mallow of the Garden, the roote of the wyld mallowe yt is
called the Marshe mallowe, the roote of the dock, the roote of the
White Lillie, Garlick, oynions, Rapes, lyn seede, Skerwitt,
ffenegreeke, whete flowre, or whete Leven, Swynes grease, eyther
of these be Maturatives by it self wch is a rypeninge medycyne,
that rypenethe a Sore, Or else yf you meddle ij or iij of them
togither wth grease, you may make Emplaster therof. /

A Compound Medycyne, that will rypen a bloodie
Apostume, and everie Apostume t us reddie to quitter. /

Take Mallowe of the Garden and Violett of eyther .M.
and ii Rootes of Marshe Mallowes seethe them perfectlie well
in water, then grynd them small, and presse out the water
& iuyce thatt is in them, Then take one li of that iuyce
of oyle iij oz. and of Swynes grease iij oz. of lyn seede
of meele of ffencreek ana, j oz. of wheate flowre ij ounces
and meddle all those wth the hearbes and make a Playster
therof neyther to thick nor to thinn, But looke that
you put noe ffenecreeke in the Maturative yf the Sore be
burneinge of it self.

Alsoe thus





Alsoe thus cold matters ar matured

Take an Onyon and a garlicke gead in lieke quantitie and
two Egg and roste them altogither in the ashes, then pill them &
grynd them altogither, then take as much wheate Leven as is of
all the other and stampe them altogither wth oyle oliffe, and therof
make plaster maturative.

Thus you may rypen an Anthrax a fellon or a
Carbuncle, for as much as they are gathered of greate
store of matter and hard to touche and alsoe they
are reddye to inflame the matter and they are not to
be rypened wth any medycyne but such as will cleanse
and rypen bothe togither. And that is found in
one thinge a lone. Viz. In Tart Levened dowe
and Somewhat Clammye, becasue of the Sweetnes
wch dothe rypen the Sore best, and the tartnes, dothe
cause it to clense and drawe out the matter, Soe t
it rypenethe equallye togither. Wherfore in everie
hard matter Tart leavened dowe is the foundation
of matter of Emplaster. Then make thus a Compound
Maturative, that best servethe for Anthrax, ffellon or
Carbuncle.

Take ffat drye ffigges xij, an ounce of Reysons, as much
of tart Levened dowe as is of bothe the other Stampe them
small and put therto as much old oyle and vinegre as
will Suffise to meddle wth the plaster./

Alsoe take harie Scabious that hath some what broad leaves
neare the earthe, and hath a square stalke the lengthe of
a Cubitt, the leaves a finger longe, a flowre lieke to e
Skye, and the higher that it wexethe the lower wexe
the leaves. Take this Scabious and grynd it wth
freshe grease, and make an Emplaster therof, And t
will rypen an Anterax a ffellon or Carbuncle, often pved.





Cap: III 20

The Third Chapter is of Mundificatives that rypen and clense
the matter t was not fullye matured, and these are verie necessarie
to be had at such tymes are you are constreyned to deale wth an
Aposteme before it be fully rype, and when the matter is greate in
quantitie and not handsome to be matured: As in Skarfullis, Kernell
and hard Byles.

A Mundyficative of Apostemes that be well rypened, and of newe
Sores, for it clensethe well and ceasethe ache, and fleshethe the place.

Take e yolkes of iij hennes Egg and wheate flower and make a salve
therof and laye it therto.

A generall Mundificative that rypenethe and clensethe Apostemes
that were to longe before they were opened, and it clensethe alsoe
Anterax, ffellons, Botches and old Sores, if it be possible to heale
them.

Take good white honey ij oz the yolkes of ij hennes Egg and meddle
them well togither with wheate flower as is aforesaid. Then put therto
iiij oz of Smallache and boyle it over an easie fyre, till it be thick and
make Emplaster therof and laye to the Sore.

And yf you will clense deceyvable wound the malice wherof you thinke will
turne unto a Cancer. Then put in the Mundificative the iuyce of
Absynthiu in stead of Smallache, And yf you will rypen hard sores, as the
king evill, or Botches, Put in the medycyne in stead of Smallache
as much as thone half of the medycyne dothe amount to of Onyons and
white lillie rootes, that have bene well sodden in water and after
well ground vppon a Moller stone, and therof make Emplaster. /

An other noble medycyne for deceyvable and venemous wheles that
be hard for to heale. /

Take de li of Ceruse, of Olibanum and Mastick ana, diz. of Bolearmo-
niake ij oz. of Redd lead di .oz. of water of Roses ij oz & di and put therto
as much honey as will suffice and temper it vpp wthout heate of the fyre. /

An other Mundifiative that comfortethe the place wher it is put.

Take iij oz of Mol Rofat strained, and one .oz. of Barlie flowre, meddle
them well togither, and yf the wound be amongst the Synnewes,
it needethe to be clensed, and then put therto the fowrethe part of the
Recipt aforesaid, of Terpentyne well washed, and yf you will make
it more stronge to mundifie, then put therto Sarcole, as much
as the vjth parte of the whole dothe amount vnto. /

An other Mandifi:





An other Mundificative of fistulaes that be runninge, Sores
mortyfied wth Cankers, that be brenninge, and alsoe Sores
that have the Crust, And many other Sores, And it is called
vnguentum Aposhlorum wch St Peter and St Paul made in this
wise viz.

Take white waxe, Rosen Armoniake t us a gum; of eyther of them xiiij .
of Vertegreece iij .oz. of ffrankencense vj .oz. of Astrologia longa
vj oz of lyturge ix oz of Myrr and Galbanu, ana iiij oz of Bedellu
vj oz. and putt them in stronge vinegre, and disolve it. And make e
Oyntment, wth ij li of Oyle in this Manner: First make poulder
of all thinges as is afore said, that wilbe pouldred and sersed
and alsoe disolve all the gumes wth fyre and when they be disolved,
then put in the wax And then put in the Oyle, and straine it all,
and when it begines to be Luke warme, then shake in the poulder
easelie, and stirr well all the confection till it be cold and keepe
it till you neede to vse.

Cap: IIII

The fowrthe Chapter of Conglutmatiues & Regneratives that
is to saye of glueinge or knittinge togither wexinge and healinge
vp agayne of the fleshe. Noe well that Conglutmatives and
Aggregatives are all one, and have a difference from medycynes
Regenerative, Incarnatives wch be both one, And as well they as the
first medycynes have a difference from Consolidatives & Octatrizatives
and Sigillatives wch Three are much lieke in healinge. /

The first Medycynes that be called Conglutmatives and Aggratives
that is glueinge or knittinge the lyppes of wound togyther in places
wher the fleshe is not cutt awaye, but onlye slitt in sonder,
They be desectatives wth a manner of Clamenes and clensinge,
And thebe some Simple medycynes that worke mervelouslie As
lyme, leaves of white lillye, leaves of Planten, leaves of Apletree,
Cipresse tree and such lieke /

A Compound medycyne.

Take one parte of ffrankencense, ij part of Sandragon, and
iij of quick lyme, all three meddeled in poulder





A Common Medycyne Conglutmative to knitt the
lippes of a wound togither  21

Take the lynte of old fyne lynnen Clothe that is purelye white
put therto the oyle of Roses and a little Galbanum, melted and
mixed togither in manner of an Oyntment, And yf you will
put therto a little poulder made of the Rynd of the ffranken-
cense tree, & of the Clove tree, or best of Asbrolognia brent the
medycyne wilbe much e stronger. /

Regenerative Medycynes are these that increase fleshe in a
wound, but it is needfull that they be clensinge and dryeinge
wypeinge awaye the greate Superfluitie, and alsoe dryeinge
that wch waxethe all daye in hollow wound,  But this they
doe in diverse wayes, ffor a wound that is in a moist bodie, as
in a man that is naturallye fatt, or in him that hath the
dropsey, or a woman or a child, Then it is needfull to have
medycynes that be somewhat dryeinge, As ffrankencense
Vermnshe, ffenegreeke, Lyturge, Mastick, Myrr Aloes,
Beardflowre and such other thing / The wound of a drye
bodye us requisite to have a drye medycyne, Viz. Astrologia,
Gladiu e mele of Orabus, and Lupynes and such other, and
namely yf the brent they be dryer /

Alsoe yf a wound have much quitter it needeth to have the
more dryeinge medycyne, And yf it have little quitter
then it needethe the lesse dryeinge. /

This is a good Compound Medycyne /

Take x part of oyle ij part of poulder of vertegreece and
grynd them well togither, And this maketh a good Regenera-
tive of good fleshe, And a Corasive for evill fleshe t is to saye
frettinge.

A Regenerative Poulder /

Take ffrankencense, i oz. Vernishe ij .oz .ffenecreeke ij oz and
make fyne poulder therof well mixed and put enoughe
therof in the wound.

Alsoe litergium





Alsoe Litergium & Nitritum makethe fleshe to growe,
and increasethe it, and fretethe awaye evill fleshe
And removethe Scabbes from the bodye, and thus it is made,

Take poulder of Litarge as much as you will and grynd it in
a morter wth oyle of Roses till it be lieke an oyntment,
And that Oyntmt bringethe old Sores to a good rule. /

Alsoe of these Symple thing you make an Oyntment Viz.
Take of e poulder of lytarge, j oz of oyle of Roses iiij oz. of wexe
j oz. in Somer tyme and in winter oz secu and this is the
proportion of all Oyntment. And if you will make it more
dryeinge put therin more of the poulder and lesse of the
Oyle & wexe, And yf you will more vnctious put therin
more of the waxe and oyle, and lesse of the poulder /.

Medycynes Consolidatives & Sigillatives and Cicatriza-
tives or such that makethe e Skin to wex be these

Lytargie ffeverfewe Brent Bones, Ceruse of the roote of
pyne tree, drye Rosen, Suthurwood, the rynde of ffrankencense.
Balaustia, Galles Nuttes, Cucumer & many other All
these Simples are good by them selves /.

Of Compound Medycynes in some vnguentum
Rasis is the best, yf it be made of the Ceruse for it will
make Skin to wex, and it is good for breninge & scaldinge
of fyres, water, or oyle, And for everie place that wantethe
the Skin, and for hott whilkes, that wydeth the Skin in every
place, and for many other thing. And it is thus made,

Take Oyle of Roses iiij oz. of Ceruse and wexe ana, j oz.
mingle them togither and put therto the whites of ii Egg
and a drame of Camfer, and make an oyntment therof.

An other.

Take white Rosen boyle it in stronge vinegre, then annoynte yo hand
wth oyle of Roses and gather up the Rosen and drawe it longe
betweene yo hand & keepe it to yo vse, And in Somer
tyme put therto half as much waxe as ther is of the Rosen. /
