Caput. v

The fifte Chapter of Molyficatives t makethe softe any member
that hath bene broken or out of ioynte, or wounded and is healed &
hath noe moveinge in the ioynte, that needethe molificatives
to comfort the Synnewes, and comfort the matter that is gathered
into the Synnewes, Of the wch Molificatives this is owne
that hath bene often proved. /

Take Bedellum Opopanac, Gum Arabec ana. j oz. and disolue
them in wyne then melt ij oz. of Swynes grease, and an ounce of
Duckes grease, hennes grease and goose grease, and mixe them
wth the gummes, for all Gummes doe greatlye profitt the Synnews
and especyallye Terpentyne. /

Alsoe take one oz. of wex and another of Terpentyne, and put therto
j oz. of ffenecreeke meale j oz of lynseede poulder of ffrankencense
oz fe. of Mastick oz fs. mixe altogither and make an oyntment
therof wch is an oyntment for ache proved often tymes

An other

Take goose grease, duck grease, hennes grease, and Swynes
grease t us vnsalted, and as much of yellowe waxe and
melte them altogither, then straine it, and when it is cold it is
a Soveraigne oyntmt for many thinges.

An other /





An other

Take the Rootes of Marshe Mallowes and of wylde nepe
and grynd them and steepe them in a glasse with oyle in
a Cauldron of water with iiij part of oyle, and one
part of waxe, and this makethe a soveragne
molificative, and yf you put therto the xij part
of the medycyne of Euforbium, it will make it
a pretious oyntment for the Crampe, whersoe=
ever it bee. /

Now for Molificatives these Medycynes suffice,
But take heede as often as you will molifie a
member with these foresaid medycynes. ffirst
washe well the member with hott water that Marshe
mallowes and other mallowes, the flowres of
Camamell, lynseede, and ffenecreeke hath beene
sodden in and afterward you may annoynt the

Cap: VI.

The sixt Chapter of Cauterizinge t is to bren wher
neede requires Medycynes Cauterizinge wch bren
the skin  without fyre are these. viz: Sivers, nepe, Apiu
Emerodarum, puttockes foote, Crowes foote ffaturisworte, Can-
tarides Oynions, Garlick, Spanishe Oynions Squlle,
Anagardus and hard pitch, meddle them altogither in
lieke quantitie and yf you have noe honey of Anagardus
reddye, make it in this wise:

Take a payre of smithes pinsons wth broad bittes and heate
them reasonable hott, and putt in them Anagardus,
then presse the Bitt togither & therof will come honey
Take t honey well and equallye mixed wth hard pitche
and it makethe a good Cauterize, These two alone wthout
Iron will open Apostemes.

An other Cauterizinge Medycyne

Take blacke sope and quick lyme and temper them togither
and that dothe make a good Ruptorie medycyne.





23
Medycynes Frettings stronglye and not Violentlye and doe well
avoyde dead fleshe

Take Hermodactilis Verdegreece, Astrologia rotunda, in lieke quantitie
make poulder of them.

A medycyne t is stronglie Corosiue and Verie good for Crust old Sores
ffistulaes and Cankers.

Take e iuyce of Asfodill oz vj. quicke Lyme oz ij. Orpement oz ij. and make
small Cakes therof and drye them in the Shaddowe in the Somer tyme,
and when you have neede to remove rotten fleshe, or to close old Sores or
Crustes, make poulder of one of the Cakes and strow vppon it and about
it, Neverthelesse when soe ever you vse this, medycyne you can never Voyde any
thinge any thinge therof awaye, till the Crust that is made and gathered
theron doe fall awaye of it self, And yf it hathe drawne vp all the fleshe
then is it well, or els put more thero as you did before. Albeit put lesse
of the medycyne therin and then mundefie it, and heale it up as other
wound.

And thus endethe the Antidotorie of Chirurgerie and of many excellent
Medycynes verie necessarie for Chirurgions and such as take Cares in hand.





Here followethe the makeinge of diverse
Salues for Sores & Woundes

A Salve that causethe a wound to give Matter

Take Turpentyne iij ounces, Hart Suett an ounce of Oyle
of Roses x. ounces, white ffrankencense in poulder halfe
an ounce, melt the Suett e oyle of Roses togither with
the Turpentyne, and put the powlder to it, and wth Lynte
laye it on a little warme.

A drawing plaster wch drawethe vnto it matter
& corruption & all vncleanes in a wounde:

Take Rosen a li. waxe halfe a li. Sheepes Suett Hart
Suett or Deere Suett or May butter eyght ounces, Ter-
pentyne iiij ounces Aloes in powlder a dragme, Mastick
and white ffrankencense of eache an ounce Myrra halfe
an ounce. Melt the waxe Rosen and Suett togither and straine
it thoroughe a Clothe Then put therin the Turpentyne
& the other parcell in powlder & oyle of Roses ij ounces stirr
them altogither till it be cold & put to it a dragme of Saffron
in poulder. /

A Salve that causethe fleshe to growe & healethe.

Take Planten, Valerian, Mouseare, Mallowes,
Daysie and Grunsell, then take yellowe waxe twelve
ounces, good Oyle Oliffe a li. Turpentyne iiij ounces,
Rosen five ounces, white Frankencense, Mastick of eache
ij ounces, Saffron a dragme, Ten ounces of the iuyce
of Orpen, and melt e wexe, Oyle and Rosen togither
wth the iuyce of Orpen, And after put therin Terpen-
tyne and sett it from the fyre and clense it well, and
make the other parcell in powlder & put therin when
it is halfe cooled and then straine it.

A Salve to take out thornes, Splinters or bones

Take waxe and Rosen of eache x. ounces & an ounce of
Hart Suett melt them togither, Then take of the powlder
of Adamant stone halfe an ounce, The roote of okeferne,
the roote of white dittane and the roote of Rethe of eache a
dragme in powlder, and an ounce of oyle of Hempeseede
and make therof a plaster.





To breake & to rypen Apostumes or any other Sore. / 24

Take ij Ounces of Galbanum halfe an ounce of Rosen halfe an ounce
of ffenegreeke meale, halfe an ounce of Terpentyne & a little
Swynes grease, and seethe them togither, and then put to them
the Turpentyne & Galbanu & the Rosen and seethe it togither
& straine it and wth leather laye it to the Sore as brade as you
will have it broken. and it will rypen and breake any Sore pved.

A Salve to drawe and to heale and to make Skin./

Take Planten, mouseare, a little mallowes and Pimpernell
Stampe them in a Morter then seethe them in Swynes grease
and Sheepes Tallowe half a li of waxe, an ounce of Terpentyn
halfe an ounce of white ffrankencense, a little dragagant
a little Mastick and a little Rosen, all in fyne powlder,
melt that wch is to be moulten wth the Suet when it is greene,
put the poulder to it and straine it.

To make fleshe to growe in all hollow wound and to Skin.

Take Mallowes Valerian, Byttanie Planten most of all,
daysie leaves, mouseare, Growndsell, & Scabious, Stampe all
these in a Morter wth Pimpernell & Egremonye; Then seethe
them wth deere Suett, or Sheepe Suett or May Butter till e
Oyle be greene; Then put therto a li of waxe, xij ounces of
Rosen and Seethe them wth the Oyle, then put to it half a
li of Terpentyne, Mastick and white fffrankencense,
of eache two ounces in poulder, halfe a dragme of Gumm
dragagant, halfe an ounce of Gumm Elempini or of Myrha
in powlder, Then put all these togither and put it in the waxe
and Rosen and strayne altogither agayne in fayre water, and
this will heale any Sore, And yf you will put to it an ounce of
Oyle of Roses: it is verie good pbatum.

A Salve to Skin and to heale. /

Take waxe and Sheepes Suett of eache a li. and these herbes
Planten Egrymonie, Mallowes, Mouseare, & Pimpernell,
Stampe them & seethe them wth the waxe and Suett till
they be greene, Then put an ounce of Turpentyne, a little
Mastick and ffrankencense in powlder, and a little Gumm
Dragagant, And put all these to the waxe wth a little honey
and lett them seethe a little togither and straine it in fayre
water.





A Salve that clensethe matter & maketh fleshe to growe

Take Terpentyne and honey of Roses strayned of eache ij ounces,
Myrra and white ffrankencense of eache iij dragmes and a
little Saffron, all these in fyne poulder, and e yolke of an Egg
and temper all these togither, And yf you will have it to matter
put a Tent in Oyle of Roses & putt it in the wound, wth a plaster,
And yf you will Clense it put this foresaid Salve to it wth fyne
Lynt dipt in this Salve. /

When you will laye any Plaister to any wound laye about
it the defensive Oyntment.

A Defensive Salve for all Sores.

Take iij ounces of oyle of Roses, Bolarmomiak ij ounces Terra
Sigillata a dragme Camfer a dragme, Vinegre halfe an
ounce, Nightshade and Howselicke of eache a handfull, Stampe
them and take the iuyce and mixe it togither and of all these
make a fyne oyntment in a Morter. It is cold. /

An other Defensive /

Take Sape of Nightshade or sape of howselicke iij ounces
Oyle of Roses j. ounce Bolearmonick j ounce, vinegre halfe
an ounce, Meddle all these togither and laye about the wound
wth the white of an Egg.

A Salve that Skinneth & makethe fleshe.

Take Rose honey vj ounces, Terpentyne wexe and Rosen of eache
ij ounces, ffrankencense and Masticke of eache on ounce, Myrha Sercole
and Mumme of eache a dragme, of these make a Salve and strike
it on a Clothe. But first put into the wound warme Oyle of
Roses meddled wth the white of an Egge and temper it and
put it in the wound wth Lint dipped into it, and about the wound
shall you strieke defensive

A salve to make a wound to matter. /

Take Hartes grease and Terpentyne of eache two ounces, Oyle of
Roses an ounce, Mastick and white ffrankencense of eache halfe
an ounce in fyne powlder. /

A Salve to make fleshe growe in all manner of wound.

Take Valerian Planten, Mouseare, Mallowes, Bitteny, Verven & Pimpernell
and stampe them and seethe them wth Sheepes Suett till they be greene oyle,
then straine it, and put to it Turpentyne iiij. ounces, honey ij. ounces,
white ffrankencense, and Mastick in poulder x. ounces, Aloes, and
Saffron the third part of a dragme in poulder, Oyle of Roses and wexe
ynoughe and make a Salve therof. probatum. /





A Salve to make Skin fayre and to heale. / 25

Take white ffrankencense and myrrha of eache an ounce, vj ounces of Rosen, iiij ounces
of wexe and x. ounces of oyle Oliffe, and therwth make a Salve; Or els wth this powlder,
Take Masticke white ffrankencense, dragagante and myrra of each halfe an ounce,
and make therof a fyne powlder, sersed; or take Lytargium medled wth oyle of Roses,
This Salve dothe heale the wound, And laye it nott on the wound till it be cleane
& full of fleshe, then laye it on. /

Yf the wound be deepe. /

Take Oyle of Roses mixed wth the youlkes of Egges warme, and fill the wound &
dipp a Tent in the Oyle and put it in the wound, till the tyme the matter come
out. And then clense it everie daye wth a clensinge Plaster; It is made of
Rose honey Barley meale, and Sometyme Turpentyne, Myrrha, Sarcacole,
And about the wound a defensive, But yf the wound be not deepe you may
heale it wth the greene wound Plaster, or Iuyce plaster.

For a newe wound. /

Take Planten greate and Small, St. Johns worte Red Coleworte, white Coleworte,
Suckleworte Sickleworte, Mallowes, Bittenie, Scabious, Sauge, Daysie, Tormentill, Orpen.

A good drawinge Plaster wch drawethe to it e matter of all uncleane wound.

Take Rosen x. ounces Waxe vj. ounces, Sheepes Suett viij. ounces, Turpentyne
iij. ounces, Masticke and White ffrankencense in fyne poulder of eache half an
ounce, Mirrha and Aloe Sicatryna in fyne poulder of eache ij dragmes, or els
halfe an ounce of them bothe, Melt the Rosen waxe and Sheepe Suett
togither & straine it, Then put therin Terpentyne & the other parcell in
poulder, and oyle of Roses x. ounces and stirr all togither, and after it hath
sodden a while Skim it cleane and put it into a boxe, Yf you will have it
greene, Take Planten, Orpen, Valerian, Mallowes, Bittenye, Howselieke, or
Daysie leaves Stampe them and seethe them wth the Rosen, waxe & Sheepe
Suett till it looke greene and straine it

To make a Salve wch makethe fleshe to growe in all Complexions

Take waxe and Rosen of eache x. ounces of good oyle Oliffe xj. ounces, honey a dragme,
melt them togither and Skim it cleane, and straine it, then sett it on the fyre,
put therto iij ounces of Terpentyne, Mastick and White ffrankencense in fyne
poulder of eache x. ounces. Then take it from the fyre, and put therin Saffron
and Aloe Sycatrina, of them bothe twopeny weight in fyne poulder, and
stirr it and put it into boxes. If you will have it greene, Stampe Planten,
Valerian, Bittenie, Pimpernell, Mallowes, Mouseare, & daysie leaves,
Seethe them wth the Rosen waxe and oyle till it be greene, And put to
it a little gumm Elempanie in poulder.





To make the skin to growe.

Take waxe iij. ounces, Terpentyne ij ounces, Gomm Elempanye
& white ffrankencense of them bothe an ounce in fyne poulder,
melt the waxe wth a little oyle of Roses, and put e Terpentyne
& the poulder into it and lett it Coole, and if you will have it greene
put the iuyce of healinge hearbes unto it

A good drawing Plaster.

Take honey two Spoonefull Mastick, white ffrankencense and Myrrha all
these in fyne poulder, an ounce of Aloe in poulder a Spoonefull Terpen-
tyne ij ounces, and put them altogither & stirr them well, Then take
the youlke of an Egge and an halfe pennye waight of Saffron &
put to it, and temper it agayne & thicke it wth beane flowre, and make
a Salve of it, and put to it oyle of Roses a small quantitie.

An other pure good Salve that dothe heale newe and
old wound & hath bene well pved. /

Take Planten Small & greate Selfeheale, Auence, Bittenie, or
Verven, Valerian, daysie leaves, mouseare, Pimpernell, mallowes
or Tansie, of eache of these as you thinke good, Stampe them and put
them into a Pann wth Sheepes Suett or deere Suett, clensed Rosen
viij. ounces, prosin iiij. ounces, waxe x. ounces, white ffrankencense
ij. ounces in fyne poulder, and seethe them altogither till they be greene.
Then straine it and put to it oyle of Roses and honey of eache an ounce,
& iiij ounces of Terpentyne, and lett them seethe altogither
& skim it cleane, Then put to it Mastick gumm Elempaine,
or Mirra one dragme, and lett it seethe a walme, and put in
it a dragme of Campher, and lett it Coole, And then melt it
agayne and straine it & put it in boxes, & it will heale well.

from louing frend

All these hearbes are good for Cutt and hurt, Planten Small &
greate, Pimpernell, hearbe Robert, Tansey, Tutsorne, Selfeheale,
Mowseare, Auence, Tabacco, And these be healers, Dasie, Bitterne,
Salendyne, Vervyne, Scabious & orpen, And these be Clensers
& purgers Valerian Mallowes and Coleworte, And these doe
incarnate Howselieke, daysie, Growndsel, & Nightshade, and
these are cold and greate abaters of Swellinge





Flos unguentorum the purest Salve that may be. 26

Take Rosen & prosin of eache halfe a li, virgin waxe and ffrankencense
of eache a quarter of a li Masticke an ounce, Hart Tallowe or deare Suett
or Sheepes Suett one quarterne of a li. Clensed Campher iiij dragmes
melt that wch is to be melted & make poulder of that wch is to be pouldred,
and boyle it altogither and straine it thoroughe a Cleane Canvasse Clothe
into a quarte of white wyne. Then boyle the white wyne & all the other
stuffe togither. Then lett it Coole a little and then put therto a quarter of a
li of Terpentyne, but let not the stuffe be hotter than you can suffer yo
hand in it. Then stirr it all well togither till it be cold and put in the Cam-
phire and keepe it for yo vse, and keepe it for the best plaster that can be
made for newe or old wound. ffor of all Intreat it is most ingendringe
& clensinge of ffleshe, And it healeth more in a Senight than other Salves
in a Monethe, And it will suffer noe dead fleshe in a wound nor
noe corruption to be ingendred in it, and it is good for all broken
Impostumes, on warrantise Probatum.

A greate drawer, And this Plaster is good for
old wound for it clensethe and healethe.

Take Rosen & Sheepes Suett of each j. li Waxe dim. li Butter iiij ounces
ffrankencense and Mastick of each an ounce in fyne powlder, melt
the Rosen, waxe, Butter and Sheepes Suett togither, & when it is well moulten
straine it, Then put therto an ounce of honey and of Terpentyne iij. ounces
and seethe them altogither agayne, and Skim it cleane. Then put in
the Mastick and ffrankencense, & Myrre two dragmes in fyne poulder
put into it, And put it in boxes & lett it Coole, and yf you will you may make
it greene wth those herbes before mentioned.

A Intreat t will heale any newe wound.

Take Planten verven, Bitteny, Selfeheale, Valerian, of eache as you thinke
good, daysie leaves one handfull, then stampe them and boyle them in
white wyne, till the wyne be wasted, & vij. ounces of Sheepes Suett wth
them, Then straine it and put to the liquor Rosen and Waxe of eache
x. ounces, Profsn iiij ounces, and melt them wth the other Stuffe and
when it is molten Skum it cleane, and put therto of white ffrankencense
& Mastick in poulder of eache one ounce, Myrrha in poulder a
dragme, & in the Coolinge put in iiij. ounces of Terpentyne &
a dragme of Campher in poulder, and stirr it well & put it in a boxe. /





Flos vnguentorum this Intreate is good for old and newe
wound for it clensethe and healethe, and Sufferethe noe
corruption to grow in wound, nor evill fleshe. /

Take Mallowes, Planten small and greate, Pimpnell, Sanicle
Bitteny, Daysie, Verven, Valerian, Mouseare and Egrimonie of
eache of these as much as you thinke good, And stampe them and seethe
them in white wyne, And when it is hott put therto viij ounces
of hartes, deares, or Sheepes Suett, and when the wyne is con-
sumed, put in of Rosen Perosin waxe of eache half a pounde,
and melt it & straine altogither, & put it into a cleane vessell
and sett it on a softe fyre and skome it cleane, And put vnto
it Olibanum & mastick in fyne poulder of eache an ounce,
Mirrha in poulder two dragmes & stirr it, well, And in the
Coolinge put two dragmes of Campher, And put it in a boxe
for a pure plaister that hath bene diverse tymes proved.,
And looke you make you Salve as cleane as you may, and as
greene as you can, wth the herbes, And yf you have noe wyne
seethe yo Rosen, waxe and Suett togither wth the herbes till
it be greene and straine it, And soe you may doe all other
Salves yf you have noe wyne, And if you have wyne, make
yo Salve as greene as you can, Soe you burne it not, for
if you burne it you mare it.

ffore a Sorenes that is Impostemed inward
and the Skin not broken

Mynce Rewe, Sauge & the fatt of Salt Bacon togither, and
after Stampe them small as you can, And then take a quantitie
of black Sope & labor them togither wth yo hand till they be
well wrought, then strike it vppon white leather, and laye it
to the grieved place, whether it be brused wth hurt or fellon,
Proved.

Item for a bruse Hocsycoksion is good

Item for a fellon, take a black Snayle and slitt it and
laye it to the Sore place & it helpethe.

A Salve that Clensethe Rotten Fleshe

Take waxe and Rosen of eache an ounce, Spanishe greene halfe an ounce,
Mastick, Mirre & galbanu of eache an ounce, ffrankencense & Lytarge
halfe an ounce, Then take oyle Oliffe iiij ounces, and put the galbanum
waxe & Rosen to it, & melt them & straine it, and then put to it an ounce of
Terpentyne & the Spanishe greene or greene Coperise & allume brayed
in poulder, Then put e ffrankencense, myrre mastick & Lytergium in
fyne poulder vnto it. /





27
A Salve that makethe fleshe to growe in all wound and hurt:

Take waxe and Rosen of eache x. ounces, Sheepe Suett or good Oyle Oliffe
viij ounces, and Stampe these herbes Planten Small & greate, daisie
leaves, Valerian, Mallowes or Coleworte, Sanicle, Pimpernell, or Egre-
mony or eache of these as you thinke good, Then Seethe all these togither
and when it is well sodden then Straine it and put it into a cleane
Vessell, then Skome it cleane over a softe fyre, Then put vnto it
iiij ounces of Terpentyne, but looke t the Salve be not to hott for
burneinge of the Terpentyne, & the Salve, and in the Cooleinge
put of ffrankencense and white Masticke in fyne powlder of
eache one ounce, And stirr it well and put it in boxes. /

This Plaister will heale all manner of Sores

Take Galbanum an ounce, Armoniacke halfe an ounce, and dissolve
the Galbanum and Armomiak in vinegre a daye and a night, then
melt waxe iiij ounces, Rosen ij ounces, then take it from the fyre
& in the Coolinge put in the Gummes dissolved and then sett it
on a soft fyre & alwaye stirr it till the vinegre be consumed and
in the Coolinge put in Myrre, Mastick Olibany & Terpentyne,
and stirr it well & make it vp in Rowles. /

A Salve Mundificative

Take Rose honney iij. ounces strained & a little Terpentyne,
Mastick & ffrankencense of eache halfe an ounce, mirra a dragme
all in fyne poulder. /

To take the dead fleshe out of any Sore to clense it. /

Take Allume in poulder one ounce, greene Coperas in fyne
poulder halfe an ounce, Honie iiij ounces, & a little Turpentyne
& white wyne a pynte, and seethe them altogither over a softe
fyre, till the honye be thick but burne it not and when it is cold
wth lynte laye it on and lett the lynt be tempered wth the honey
& lye to it xxiiijtie howres, And then take awaye the dead fleshe wth
a knife, & laye a clensinge Salve to it, and yf the dead fleshe
sticke fast to it, laye the Oyntment wth the Lynt on agayne And
this Oyntment shall take awaye the dead fleshe & clense it, put
to it beane flowre. /





If a Sore legg be in greate heate, lett these herbes be in
yo Salve. Viz.

Violett leaves, Growdsell leaves, nyghtshade leaves or elder leaves,
and these shall kill the greate heate of any Sore, The iuyce of
these herbes & Planten leaves small & greate are verie good in this
Case.

A Salve for all manner of Sores, and tis a drawer.

Take oyle oliffe, Turpentyne, Rosen, Waxe, Deere Suett & Sheeps
Tallowe, of eache one as you shall thinke good. Then take Smal-
lage, Bugell, Bitteny, Ragworte Planten & Orpen, And
take the iuyce of all these herbes, but first take all that is
to be molten & melt them vppon a Chaffinge Dishe wth Coles,
And put the oyle & Terpentyne vnto it, and then take the
iuyce and put to the Same, and soe lett it boyle softlye togither
till it be mixed, & stirr it for burneinge, and when it is
thick take it of and make it in Rowles & put lynt into it.

A greate drawer For a Sore legge a verye pure Salve & it will heale and drawe

Take Smallage Bugell, or Ragworte, Planten Rose Campen or
Poochewort orpen, Bitteny & Valerian of eache of these one
handfull, and stampe them, and take e iuyce of them and
put it in a dishe, Then take of oyle Oliffe, honey and Terpentyne
of eache Sixe Spoonefulls & put them togithers, Then take
of cleane waxe and Rosen a good quantitie or els ffrankencense
for yo waxe, if you take them all three, quantitie for quantitie
you shall doe well, Then melt yo Rosen waxe & ffrankencense,
in a dishe wth a little Deere Suett, Then take yo oyle Oliffe
Honey and Turpentyne & put to the waxe &c. Then take
the iuyce of e herbes & putt into it and seethe them fayre
& softlye for burninge, & stirr it often and lett it seethe till it
be thick, Then straine it agayne, and lett it Cale in a dishe,
Then put it vp & keepe it and it will heale any Sore. /

To make a pure Salve that will heale and drawe. /

Take Rosen, waxe freshe Butter and Turpentyne Sixe
Spoonefulls and melt them all vppon the Coles & then put
therto lynte, and lett it seethe till it be well mixed togither,
Then take it of an lett it be cold, and then temper it well
and put it in boxes. /





ffor all manner of woundes a greate drawer. 28

Take ffreshe Butter and ment it in a Chafinge dishe Then take
ij Spoonefull of Tarr and ij Spoonefull of pitche & cleane waxe Rosen
& ffrankencense as much in bignes as xiij walnutand of Ter-
pentyne sixe spoonefull & melt them altogither wth the butter
and lett them seethe fayre and softlie for burneinge & stirr it
often and when it is well myngled togither put fayre lint into
it then take it of and lett it Coole, and yf ther be dead fleshe
in the sore, then burne them and make poulder therof and laye
it to the sore place & a plaister vppon it, and lett it lye xxiiijta
howres. Then take e salve and applie it alone vnto it &
it will drawe the dead fleshe awaye & make all cleane,
Then take this Salve or the salve before written and it
will heale it vppon warantise. Probatum./

ffor to heale all manner of Sores

Take a quantitie of Redd Leade and a pynte of Oyle Oliffe
and mingle them togither till they be thick, then put in
lynnen Clothe, in lynte, ANd this is a true plaster for
all Sores.

To make a plaister for kankelinge and for Bladders
and for rysinge of heate in a Sore legg.

Take Archangell that is the dewe nettle, and seethe it well in
water Then take it out of the water & lett the water runn cleane
out, and braye it well in a Mortar, and make a plaister
therof, and laye it some what to the Legge and soe doe
often and it shall destroye e akeinge e frettinge and the
fowle red water that is therin and heale it fayre and cleane.

ffor a newe hurte.

Take Creame and honey Bitteny and Bugell, oxpen valerian
& planten, and seethe all these in the Creame and honey
take all these and straine them when they be halfe sodden
awaye and when the liquor is cold laye it to the Sore wth a
linnen clothe, and soe change it in the morneinge and it
will heale it, and if you put a little oyle oliffe vnto it and
a little Terpentyne it is verie good. Proved./






For a Sore Legge, that dothe heale many.

Take ground Ivye and Bittenie or hearbe Ivie and Bugell,
orpen and Groundsell, Valerian Planten or Pocheworte
and beate them all in a morter, and take a dishe and straine
the iuyce into it. Then take Oyle Oliffe iiij Spoonefull &
Deere Suett as much as five wallnut and these into a
fayre wth a little butter t was never Salted vppon
a Chafinge dishe of Coles, and lett it melt altogither,
and stirr it often for burneinge, and when it is moulten
put the iuyce of the herbes into it, and lett it seethe upon
the Chafinge dishe softlye & stirr it and when it is thick
take it of & straine it, Then Rowle it vpp in yo hand
when it is cold and keepe it, and it will heale any Sore. /

A Specyall Salve to heale any Sore. /

Take Rosen and prosen of eache halfe a li. Virgin waxe and
ffrankencense of eache a quarter, of Mastick one ounce,
Tallow a quarter of a li. Campher ij dragmes, melt
that wch is to be melted, and make poulder of that wch
is to be made in poulder, and boyle it well togither over
a fyre, & straine it thoroughe a cleane clothe into a
pottle of white wyne. Then boyle the wyne and the
Stuffe altogither, and lett it Coole a little, Then put
therto a quarter of Turpentyne and lett yo stuffe
be but blood warme, and stirr it well togither till it be
cold. And keepe it for e best Salve that may be made,
it is proved. It must be made vp in Rowles. /

ffor a Sore Legge.

Take ijd of Terpentyne oyle Oliffe waxe and Rosen Tallow
of a Sheepe or deere Suett, and the iuyce of these herbes
Smallage, Bugell Orpen, Campen & Bittenie. But
first take yo Terpentyne waxe and Rosen and sett them
over the fyre togither to melt. Then put in yo iuyce
wth yo oyle, and lett them all seethe togither as longe as
the Skum rysethe, Then take it of and make it vpp in a Rowle
or Rowles, and it will heale any Sore that hathe not
dead fleshe in it. pved. /







If a Legge burne in greate heate when it is hurte. / 29

Take the iuyce of all these herbes into yo Salve; Planten, Howselieke
Nightshade, & orpen. And it will take awaye all the heate and
make it in temper.

ffor a Sore legge, for a Cutt.

Take e iuyce of these herbes, Growndsell or Pocheworte, Bitteny
& Bugell, or orpen and put therto honey, e yolke of an Egg
& Saffron, & temper them, Then take oyle oliffe iiij
Spoonefull and Terpentyne wth a little Butter and as
much wexe and Rosen as seven wallnutt in bignes,
and melte the Rosen waxe & Butter togither vppon
a Chafinge dishe of Coles, Then put the iuyce and the
oyle ye oyle vnto it, and let it boyle together very Softlye
till it be thick, and then take it of, and wth freshe Butter
Rowle it vp betwene yo hand. Proved.

For a Canker, for the Poxe; for dead fleshe in a Sore /

Take a Gallon of Smithes water and put therto a quarte
of Englishe honey and ij handfullx of Sauge, and let them
boyle altogither a good while and as the Skum risethe
take it of and it will be as cleane as water, when it is sodden
ynoughe put therto a pynte of white vinegre, and a penny
worthe of allome in Lyme powlder not brente, and lett it
all seethe from a Gallon to a pottle, and then take it of
and put it in a glasse and washe e Sore wth this twise a daye
morneinge and eveninge, And it will helpe many Sores, And
it will keepe good for three yeares. pbatum. /

A medycyne for a Sore Legge /

Take one handfull of grownd Ivie, and two handfulls of Growndsell and
stampe them in a Morter and straine them, & take the iuyce sett it
uppon the fyre, and put therto one Spoonefull of honey & a little quan-
titie of fyne whete flowre, and let it boyle togither till it be thicke
& stirr it uppon the fyre for burneinge and soe take it of and make a
plaister, as broad as the Sore, and laye it to the Sore legg and it will
heale it. It hath bene often pved. pved. /





To make the Skin to growe & to drawe and heale any Sore place.

Take Bitteny Orpen Rose Campen of eache two handfull and
take the iuyce of them and put therto two Spoonefull of stone
honey & the youlke of a newe layd egge, and sixe Spoonefull
of Creeme and ij ounces of Cleane Rosen, and of wex vj ounces
and of deere Suett or May Butter or freshe butter vj ounces,
and melt all these vppon a softe fyre, till it be somewhat
thick, and stirr it for burneinge, And this will make the skin
to growe yf ther be noe dead fleshe in it, And yf you cannot
gett the hearbes get every thinge els and it will heale a Cutt
verye well. And if you take yo herbes and stampe them
wth freshe hogges grease, it will make the Salve to be the
better; for it is better for a Salve then any other grease,
And you must strayne yo salve when it is sodden And
looke that you Lint then betweene the Salve and the Sore
and laye the plaster vppon it, or Spred the salve vppon
the Lynte. pved. /

To make Skin to growe quicklie vppon any Sore
that hath noe venome in it. –

Take of waxe and freshe Butter of eache iij ounces and
an ounce of pure Rose water, and ij ounces of Creeme,
And let your water be hott before you put in your waxe, butter
& Creeme, and then let them seethe till the water be consu-
med, And laye this vppon fyne Lynte vppon the Sore
Probatum. /

To make a good Salve for a Sore legge. /

Take Valerian or Selandyne, Bitteny Rose Campen Orpen Small-
lage, Archangell or femytorie, of eache of these one handfull,
Then take of oyle oliffe, and white wyne of eache a pynte &
seethe them in it, Then take cleane waxe, Rosen & ffrankencense
of eache of them ij ounces, and put to the herbes then, Then
take vj Spoonfulles of Terpentyne, & iiij. of Honey and put
to it, and seethe all these togither, till it be thick, Then strayne
it cleane and put it in boxes, This is a pure Salve, or
Oyntement yf you melt it, for any Sore, And it will heale
& drawe mervelous well. And it is good alsoe for a Swelling
If you make a Seare Clothe of the Salve, as broad as the place t
is Swollen,





30
A verie good Salve for a bruse or a Swellinge or any other Sore /

Take Bitteny, Rose Campian & orpen of eache iiij handfull & Take
the iuyce of them & put it in a dishe. Then take waxe and fran-
kencense of eache of them iiij Ounces & melt them wth freshe
Butter. Then take blacke Sope iij Spoonefull of prosen or Rosen
as muche and put them to the waxe & let them seethe together,
Then take the iuyce of the herbes wth one ounce of Oyle of Roses
wth as much of oyle of Cammamell or oyle Oliffe and seethe them
togither till it be thick. Then straine it and let it be cold. Then
spread it vppon a lynnen clothe, as broad as the swellinge or bruse
or ache is, and laye it to the Sore place, and it will helpe it within
fowre tymes layeing to it. But warme e Seare clothe alwayes
when you laye it to the Sore If you can gett noe oyle take sheep
Suett instead, and seethe all these thing in it till it be thick.
But you must put to all these thing above written, three
Spoonefull of Terpentyne for that must be in yormedy-
cyne at all tymes.

How you shall make a salve to drawe well

Take oyle of oliffe a pynte and of white wyne as muche and put it to
yo wyne so you make yo Salve with herbes: Take the iuyce of herbes
iiij. ounces of Turpentyne as muche and put them to the oyle and
let them seethe altogither vppon a softe fyre a quarter of an howre
and stirr it for burneing: Then take of virgin wax or cleane
waxe vj. ounces, ffrankencence, Rosen and prosen of eache iij
ounces of Campher ij dragmes. And melt all these wth Tallow
or Sheepes Suett or freshe or May Butter vppon a softe fyre
by them selves/ when they be molten put them to the oyle and
lett them seethe agayne altogither softlye for burneinge & stirr
them often, & when it is something thicke then strayne it into a
cleane dishe and lett it Coole and then put it vp in a Boxe or
rowle it vp. If you cannot get oyle or white wyne; Then take
iij ounces of Turpentyne, as much of Tallowe mynced and let
them seethe togethers, and then put the other thing before written
into it & lett them seethe altogithers. And this is the best make-
inge of Salves t can be made. And you may make yo Salve as
little as you list, Soe t it be tempered after this manner specyfied.





A Salve to make e Skin to growe and drawe
and heale any Sore place in shorte tyme.

Take Biitteny Orpen, Rose Campen,planten, of eache
of them two handfull & take the iuyce of these herbes yf
you cannot gett these herbes: Take Smallage, Ribb worte
Valerian and Mallowes, and seethe these herbes in a
Gallon of white wyne till they be tender. Then take
yr herbes & wyne & beate it in a morter. Then take
a cleane skillet & put in the liquor as it is, and put of
prosen & Rosen a qrter of a li of Cleane waxe a qrte
of a li of honey iiij Spoonefull And lett the perosen
& Rosen be beaten to a poulder & put them altogither, and
let them seethe till they be melted. Then put in of oyle
Oliffe an ounce, of Deere Suett a quarter of a li of
Terpentyne ij ounces. And lett them seethe altogither
till it be very thicke. Then strayne it into a Boxe
And when you will vse itt make fyne Lynte and
put the salve vppon it, & laye it to the Sore, But you must
keepe lynte vnto it alwayes. And it will suffer noe
dead fleshe to abyde in it, If you want the other
herbes. Take Ragworte Burr leaves Grownd Ivie
and Growndsell in like quantitie as afore saide. And
alwayes stir yr stuffe well for brerninge.

ffor to make a Salve to heale a Cutt or any other hurt,
and to drawe./

Take of Cleane waxe & of Cleane Rosen of eache of them iiij. ounces
and of oyle vj. ounces dimi: or els deere Suett, Hart Suett
or Sheepes Suett and take the waxe and the Rosen and melt
it with the Suett. or if you will have it drawe well wth oyle.
And if you will have it drawe much, then put in more of Rosen
then of waxe, with one ounce of Terpentyne. Alsoe ffranken-
cense and perosen beaten to fyne powder ij Spoonefull
and melt them togither. Then take iij ounces of iuyce of
these herbes & put to the salve while it is .. seethinge. planten or
Rose Campen orpin & Bittenye & let them seethe altogithers a little while
Then put to it a Spoonfull of Stone honey & lett them seethe till they be somewhat
thick and stirr it for breneinge. Then straine it thoroughe a fyne clothe
and keepe it till you have neede. Probatum./





To make all Salves pure and good 31

If you will make Salves pure wth herbes lett them be
sodden wth white wyne or water till they be tender, or els
stampe them & seethe them in Swynes grease deeres Suett or
Sheepes Suett And strayne it and make a salve therwth
or els when they are sodden tender, let the Salve and it
seethe togither & straine it. And this will make any Salve
pure that is made wth herbes

The waye to make Salve for Sores

Take waxe and Rosen, If you will have yr Salve more healeinge
Note then clensinge, Then take more waxe then Rosen, And yf you will
have a Salve to clense more, as in deepe wounds, then take waxe and
Rosen. And  yf you will have fleshe to growe then take waxe and
Rosen in lieke quantitie. And if you will have Skin to growe
put waxe and noe rosen, If you will have have it clense muche
put to the Rosen iij ounces of myrrhe and little waxe.

To make a white salve

Take white leade iiij ounces Lyttergium ij ounces  white ffrankencese
iij dragmes, Mastick ij dragmes, make eache parcell into powlder
by it self, and the white lead wth oyle oliffe, and put therto lytter-
gium, Mastick, and ffrankencense and stirr it often tymes and at
eache stirringe put therto a little rose water, and when it be-
ginnethe to be thick, put more oyle therto & at eache tyme a little
Rose water, and make it soe that it be neyther to thick nor to thin.

An other white Salve

Take of oyle oliffe a li. Stampe white leade a li, waxe halfe a li
Campher iij: dragmes. iij youlkes of Egg Stampe these togither
in a Morter, & last of all put in yr Campher poulder.

A greate draweinge Plaister.

togither, Straine it and put to it the Terpentyne & the other pcell
in poulder and oyle of Roses ij ounces & stirr it till it be cold.

A Salve that taketh awaye vncleanes in any wound 32

Yake vinegre halfe a li. honey a li. Spanishe greene ij. ounces
Allume one ounce Seethe them alstgither on the fyre till it is
redd, for it takethe iij kynd of Colors in the seetheinge, If
it be sodden a little it seemethe greene, If it be sodden well
it seemethe Redd, if to muche then it will be black./

To make a Salve to drawe and to heale./

Take a quarter of a pound of virgin waxe and ij ounces
of Sheepes Suett & melt them tofither a little then take
it from the fyre, & put therto an ounce of frankencense,
& stirr it well togither & put it in Boxes, or Boxe./

For to make the Skin to growe on any Sore./

Take Virgin waxe or yellowe waxe one li and melt it over
a Chafinge dishe wth Coles, Then put to it ij ounces of the iuyce
of Planten, Of Oyle Oliffe & Rose water of eache one ounce,
& one Spoonefull of honey & lett them all boyle togither,
and when it is somethinge thick, take it from the fyre but
be sure to stir it often for breninge.

To make a Salve to draw well

Take waxe iij ounces, Rosen iiij. ounces, ffrankencense in poulder
an ounce, Turpentyne ij ounces, honey one ounce, mastick ij
dragmes, Campher ij dragmes, oyle oliffe ij ounces, Sheepe
Suett, or deere Suett or oyle of Roses, or May Butter, or fresh butter
two ounces, Then melt all these on a soft fyre, And beate
the Rosen, frankencense, and mastick in poulder and put
them to the waxe wth all the other, after the makeinge of
the other Salve, Then take white wyne a pynte & take
Planten, Bitteny Tose Campen & orpen of each ij
handfull and stampe them & straine them and take the
iuyce. Then Seethe the residue wth the wyne till it be tender
Then Strayne them agayne and put therto a little oyle oliffe,
Then Seethe e iuyce wth the residue of the other pcell aboue written
till it be thicke & not to much and stirr it often for burneinge & straine it.
And this is a greate drawer & a healer, But make not yo Salve as the
Chyrurgions of London doe, for they make them drye & then they
never drawe well, but make them moyst and then they will drawe &
heale well. pbatum.





To make a Salve that is a greate drawer.

Take a Sawcerfull of good Cream & honey iij Spoonefull
Virgin waxe an ounce, and Seethe them well together
on a Chafingedishe of Coles till the Creame & the rest be
well myngled & thick. Then take a little wheate flower and
put to it, stirr it together till it be thick and put in e
youlke of an Egge, and temper all togither agayne &
when it is colde rowle it up betweene yo handes.

A reddie healer of wound./

Take Mallowes Valerian & Planten & seethe them well
then Stampe them, and then take old Barrowes grease,
and a litle Barlie meale, & myngle e iuyce, e grease &
meale altogither and make a Salve thereof. But if you
take the iuyce and the grease, & melt them togither and
Skum it cleane, put e meale to it & straine it altogi-
thers this is the better, or take one ounce of waxe & melt
it wth the Grease it is good.

A Salve wch cleansethe the matter and causeth fleshe
to growe, and drawes to it the matter in all uncleane wound

Take white Rosen or other Rosen a li. Mayden waxe or yellow
waxe halfe a li. Sheepe Suett viij ounces, Turpentyne
iiij ounces, Mastick and white ffrankencense of eache
an ounce, melt the waxe Rosen and Suett togither and
straine it thoroughe a Clothe, Them putt therto Turpentyne,
the other. pcell of poulder & oyle of Roses ij ounces &
stirr them altogither till they be colde, And yf you will have
it greene put Sappe of orpen, Elder, howselicke valerian
Bitteny, Planten & Mallowes, Lett them be stamped &
sodden wth the Suett, And when it well sodden, straine it,
& make the Salve wth the same Suett.

ffor to make the Skin to growe on any Sore as hathe
bene well proved

Take waxe ij ounces, white ffrankencense & mastick in poulder
of eache halfe an ounce, Mallowe, Planten, Valerian
& Tatsane of the iuyce of them iiij ounces sodden wth
Swynes grease and when the grease is greene, put

Sore. Probatum.

These hearbes are all verie good in any Salve. viz.
to heale any wound.

Sickleworte, Loveache, nightshade, Matfellon,
Egremonie, woodrothe.

ffor to make an other Salve Take of these herbes viz.

All these are greate healers wth or wthout herbes. viz

To heale a Sore./

Take Bitteny Selfheale and valerian, Stampe them small and grynd
them well and then put to it a little honey and youlk of
Egg and grynd them all in a morter agayne. Then put to
it a little wheate flowre or a little Barlie flowre & a little
Turpentyne and temper them altogither. And it is a
pure Plaster. Pved diverse tymes.

For wound in generall that are in greate heate

ij ounces, & seethe them togither. Then take ij ounces of

A Salve to bringe wound that bee Rankled or brenninge
or akeinge in to their kynd temper agayne and to
cause them to leave Rankelinge and burneinge

Take e iuyce of Smallage, nightshade and Planten
of eache in lieke quantitie Then take honney
& the white of an Egge of eache in lieke quantitie
wth a little Turpentyne, and mingle them to-
gither till it be thicke, and let it come at noe fyre,
Then laye it to the Sore & it will close it, and leave
the ache and bringe it to the kynd temper and
heale it fayre, ffor it is a principall Salve for all
old Sores; wallworte is good alsoe.

To heale wound

ffor a legge that is in greate Heate, To take e payne

ffor to make a pure Salve for a Cutt, and it will heale
it in short tyme./

Take iiij. ounces of Cleane Waxe, of Rosen in powlder Seven ounces
Oyle Oliffe, oyle of Roses & Turpentyne of eache ij ounces, mastick
and ffrankencense in powlder of each an ounce, honney clarified
halfe an ounce, Sarcocole in poulder a dragme. Take the waxe
Honey and oyle, and melt them togither and Clarifie them
If you will have it greene; Take Planten Small and great,
Mallowes, Orpen, Tutsane, Bittenie, or Solendyne,
Valerian and a little Nightshade, of eache as you thincke
good, and Stampe them and seethe them wth oyle, Rosen,
and waxe over a soft fyre, till the waxe be greene, Then
straine it and put the Terpentyne and oyle of Roses
vnto it, and lett them seethe togither a little & take of e
Mastick, ffrankencense and Sarcocole, and when it
is from the fyre put in the poulder & stirr them altogither,
And when it is cold, With Lynte laye it on the Sore,
and it will heale it. pved.





To make a good draweinge Plaster

Take yellow waxe, Rosen and ffrankensense of eache ij.
ounces & melt them wth oyle of Roses & Swynes grease
or Sheepes Suett, or deere Suett, Then take Birdlyme
& Terpentyne of eache iij. ounces, And then put yo
Sappe of herbes to it yf you will and straine it.

To Clense a Sore from the matter

Yake Planten, Valerian and Nettles or els Sellendyne
& Bitteny & white Salt and stampe them altogether
and laye it on the Sore, or els take Nettles & Bitteny
& Stampe them wth Salt and laye it to the Sore
and it willl not Suffer it to breed any dead Fleshe,
and it taketh awaye all corruption. And put a
little honey to it pved dyverse tymes.

To Clense a Sore when it is newe

Take a little Rosewater & the white of an Egge made
in oyle and a little flaxe and laye it on the Cutt
Alsoe to Stopp the blod put to it a little Bolus Armoniak
in poulder wth a little Vinegre, & a little of the iuyce
of Bitteny & Planten, & a little oyle of Roses
and put all these togither & dipp the flaxe in it and
annoynte the Sore wound about.

An other to clense a Sore wch is pure good/

Take Rose water the white of an Egge made in oyle, and
a little Terpentyne washed in Rose water till it be
White and a little oyle of Roses & put them togither
and wth a little flaxe laye it to the Sore and it will
clense it.





36
An other to Clense a Sore

Take Strayned Rose honey, Terpentyne iij. ounces, a little Barlie
meale, a little ffrankencense in fyme poulder, and a little myre
in fyne poulder.

ffor vncleane wound to puryfie them & preserve m from
evill fleshe.

Take honey water or honey nettles, Woodbynes, wyld Sauge, orpin, dasie
Scabious Cinquefol, Red Colewort and Cammamell water.

To keepe a wound cleane from Rottinge.

Tale Bolarmoniake an ounce of white Coperis a little quantitie & beate m to
fyne poulder, Then take of Campher a oz. and beate it to fyne poulder, but
you must beate iij. Almones in e Morter before you beate the Campher, Then
make it all in a poulder & seethe it on a little water or Rose water, or in e
iuyce of dasies. and when the poulder is sodden a little take it of and let
it Cale, and wth Lynt wett in e water laye it to the wound & when it is
drye laye on more, and this will clense any Sore, and keepe it from dead
fleshe. As hath bene pved divers tymes.

ffor to take out dead fleashe wthout any payne.
Take Woodbyne leaves and small Planten iiij. handfull & Sallendyne ij. m.
and beate them in a Morter & seethe them in a pynt of honey till they be well min-
gled, Then straine it and with Lynte laye it to the wound. The poulder of honey-
sokles is pure good to eate dead fleshe.

ffor to take dead fleshe from e Sore./

Take e iuyce of woodbyne leaves & of Smallage of bothe halfe a pynte, a quartr
of an ounce of Coperas burnt in fyne poulder, a halfe penny worthe of honey
& a Spoonefull of vinegre. boyle all these togither till they cpme to the thicknes
of life honey. Then take it of & keepe it for any old Sore or newe if any dead
fleshe or corruption be therin. As hathe bene well pved.

To drawe and put away dead fleshe.

Take a pynte of white wyne, as muche of honey & iij. Spoonefull of Allom beaten
to poulder & of drye white Salt a Spoonefull in poulder & of madder as much as e
bignes of a wallnutt or wyldinge and put it in a Lynnenclothe fast bound
and let them seethe togither a qurtr of an howre, and as the Skum rysethe take it
of, and when it is almost sodden awaye take it of and straine it and put it in
a glasse Then take Allom & Coperas & burne them bothe vppon a hott
stone & make poulder of them. Take of the poulder & put it wher the dead fleshe
is. Then take of e oyle wth lynte & put to it soe t the lynte be mixed wth
the oyle and laye it on the Sore xxiiijtie houres. And this will take awaye
all dead fleshe and clense it. Then take a knife and loosen e dead fleshe, & if
it sticke fast laye on freshe ffor this hathe bene pved diverse tymes & alway
helpeth.

ffor a Sore full of dead fleshe and waterethe an wosethe.

Take a pynte of white wyne or runninge water & as muche of honey & ij Spoonefull of poulder
of Allome burnte and beaten & all the other thing as is aboue written in the other & when you
will vse it, put some into a Sawser and put lynte into it cold, and soe laye it vnto e
Sore place, Then laye a plaster of Diaculum vppon it or any other plaster, and you
must wringe out e Madder into e wyne & honey when you have sodden it. pved diverse
tymes





ffor e Synnewes An oyntement for Synnewes

Take Mallowes, the myddle Mugworte, Butter, Rosemarie
Holihockes Roses and Rewe, and make oyntement wth Maij butter..

ffor Synnewes that Shrinke, an oyle or Oyntemt.

Take ffive Swallowes out of the nest & Rosemarie and
Red Rose leaves of eache a handfull, Strawberrie leaves,
Mallowes, Howselieke & Holihocke of eache halfe a hand-
full, and beate the hearbes & Swallowes together in a
Morter, Then frye them wth Maij butter and a little
Swynes grease, ffrye the Swallowes fethers, Gutt and
all wth the herbes, and burne them in the fryeinge. Then
Straine it and let it stand in the Sonne Tenn dayes,
in a glasse, and Clarifie the oyle agayne as cleane as
may be from all manner of filthe. and put to it a little
oyle of Turpentyne if you may have it. And this oyle
will make the Synnewes to stretche, and helpe them if
they it be well made, And put to it a good ounce of oyle
of Roses, and this is a pure oyle as hathe bene well pved.

An other makeinge of oyle of Swallowes
for Shrinkinge of Synnewes

Take young Swallowes out of the nest a dozen & of Rosemarie, Lavender
Cotton & Strawberrie leaves e longe string and all, of eache of these
a handfull after e quantitie of e Swallowes. Braye the Swallowes
gutt and all togither. with the herbes in a Morter till they be Small
and then frye them wth Maij butter and not to muche, and then
put it into a pott & stoppe it close ix dayes, and then frye it
agayne in more Maij butter, & frye it well & straine it and
then you shall occupie it Chafe it agaynest the fyre, and
annoynte the Synnewes that Shrinke. Oyle Rosett is good./
Maij butter is puer to make an Oyntmt or to put into any Salve.






An oyle good for Synnewes that doe Shrinke after they be hurte & healed.

Take Ensence t is white, frankencense and mastick of eache of them 37
in poulder and ounce, Turpentyne Virgen an ounce, Sheepes Suett
old Swynes grease and freshe butter of eache of them halfe an ounce
Then take an ounce and a halfe of waxe, and an ounce of oyle
of oliffe. But a good ounce of oyle of Roses is better. Stampe
them, and boyle all these thing togither & make it in manner of
an oyntement, hard to make plaster, and make therof a plaster
& laye it on the Synnewes t be hurt .xv. dayes and they will
stretche out; And Seethe Mallowes and Planten till they be
tender, then stampe them & put the iuyce into the oyle

An oyle for Hurt Synnewes or for all manner of
Swelling or Bruse.

Take Pimpernell, Grownsell, daysie, Cinquefol, Planten,
Bitteny, Mallowes, Camamell, wylde Sauge, woodbyne
leaves, Rewe, Redd Rose, Rosemarie, Sowthistle, & Egremony
these kill the greate heate of the Swellinge. Take of all
these herbes as you shall thinke good for they are pure good all
and stampe them, and with Maij butter seethe them till the
olye be greene, Then straine it, and put to it oyle of Camamell
& oyle of Roses of eache an ounce, and sett it in the Sonne xxtie
dayes, & straine it. If you take all these herbes, take of wyld
Sauge, Nyght shade, howselieke, Woodbyne & orpen, but a
fewe for they are verie cold in operation, or lett them agree
in quantitie the hott herbes wth the cold. Some doe take but
these herbes, Pimpernell, Howselieke, Planten Cinquefol
Camamell Mallowes, Grownsell, Rose leaves, Egremony,
Woodbyne leaves & Rewe, and stampe them & seethe them
wth Maij butter, And when it is greene put oyle of Roses
& oyle of Camamell to it, and sett it in the Sonne xxtie dayes.

ffor Synnewes an Oyle./

Take Muggwort Mallowes, Cammamell, & butter and
make oyle wth oyle oliffe or Porkes grease./





* Nerue - oyle ffor to make an Oyntment called *Nervall
wch is a pure oyntment for Synnewes

Take wylde Sauge, Camamell, Muggworte, Sauge, Mallowes
Holliock, Rose leaves, Rosemarie, Sothernewood and
wallworte. of eache half a quarter washe them cleane
& stampe them wth halfe a pound of maij Butter &
put therto a quarter of Oyle Oliffe and melt them well
togither, and put them in an Earthen pott, and cover
it, and lett it stand in a moyst place nyne dayes.
then take it out of the pott and putt it in a pann, &
sett it over a fyre, and lett it frye well togither and
stirr it that it cleave not to the Pann, and when it is hott
ynough straine it into a vessell and sett it on the fyre
agayne, and put therto halfe a quarterne of waxe, a
qurte of weathers Tallowe moulten pure, and a little
honney and a qrte of ffrankencense in fyne poulder,
and stirr it well till it be melted, Then straine it and
let it Coole and let out the wate and clense it on the
nether side and take awaye the filthe, and sett it over
the fyre agayne till it be moulten, and wth a fether
Skum it and put it in Boxes. And this is the kyndest
Nervall that can be made./

To make an oyle for a Swellinge or for a
Sore in greate heate, or for Synnewes hurt.

Take of Planten small and greate iij. handfull of
Howselieke as much of Mallowes a handfull, Stamp
them and Seethe them with Swynes grease till the oyle
be greene and straine it for it is pure oyle, If Synnewes
be hurt put to it an ounce of oyle of Roses. pbatum./

An oyle for Swellinge if the Patient be in
greate heate.

Take Redd Rose leaves iij.m. Planten as much,Bittemy
Grownsell, Violett leaves, Howselieke & Egremont of eache
an handfull, ffemetorie and woodbyne leaves of them
a handfull, and j.m. of Cammamell, And wth Maij
Butter & an ounce of Roses e oyle, and as much of the oyle
of Cammamell, Seethe them altogither with the herbes
Stampe them till the oyle be greene, and stirr it often
for burneinge, and straine it into a glasse and sett it
in the Sonne xx dayes & straine it Probatum./

An other oyntment for Swelling And tis cold.

Take Smallage, Planten daysies, Howselieke & Grownsell
and frye them wth Maij butter or freshe Swynes grease
and straine it and laye it on cold vppon the patient
and if you frye a handfull of Rosemarie, or put oyle
of Roses to it, it is good.

An oyle for Swellinge Bruse or Ache

Take a pynte of good Oyle Oliffe and put it in a glasse in Maij
and take younge baye leaves, youhge Sage, Rosemarie, Rose leaves,
Rewe and Camamell mynse all these Small and put to the
oyle Roses and Rosemarie, of eache a handfull at a tyme, of baye
leaves & Sauge halfe a handfull, at a tyme, Rewe & Camamell of
each a quarter of a handfull at at tyme, or els you may take of these
herbes a little quantitie at a tyme, but e greatest part let it be
of the Roses, Rosemarie and baye leaves, Then you may mynse
the oftner






the oftner and put it into the oyle, for it will make the oyle e
better for the freshe Savor, and when you put the herbes into

in freshe herbes agayne, and let it stand xv. dayes, then
straine it and put in newe herbes agayne, And soe doe
often as it is before written and Cover the glasse Close
and lett it stande in the Sonne vj weekes./

ffor all Swellinge in the Legg Armes or any other part or
place of the bodye, This oyle followinge is good, and tis cold.

Take Cammamell, Rose leaves, Rosemarie leaves and flowres
Howseliek, Mallowes, Planten, but most of all violett

Cinquefol, & a little Fumetorie; Stampe these and seethe

of Roses, and as much of Maij butter, and seethe the first
oyle and them togither And sett it in the Sonne after
you have strained it ageyne xxxtie dayes, and put to the
herbes a handfull of Grownsell, And keepe it for a pure
Oyle Probatum.

An other oyle for Swellinge of the Legge or Armes.

Take Camamell Rose leaves Howselieke Planten night-
shade, Mallowes, Hollyhock, Mugworte, ffemetorie, Rosemarie
and Grownsell Stampe them and temper them altogither
and put to it a good quantitie of Maij butter, and let it stand
in a glasse in the Sonne x. dayes, & then seethe them
till the oyle be greene, & straine it, and put it to the oyle an
ounce of the oyle of Cammamell & an ounce of oyle of roses,
and sett it in the Sonne till it be purified and straine it often.

Butter is hott in e first degree and cold in the second; The newer
it is the better. It is good for the Shrinkinge of Sinnewes,
ffor it clensethe, Supplethe Abatethe the Aches and Softnethe and
Rypenethe.

ffor Swellinge not broken, and for to drawe an Issue.

An other Medycyne for Swellinge.

Take a pottle of tunninge water and a greate handfull of Sauge
and let it seethe to a quarte, & then straine the liquor & put into it
a pynte of white vinegre & iij. Spoonefull of honney, and
sett all these vppon e fyre e space of halfe an howre, Then
take a Scarlett Clothe & wett it in the warme liquor, and
laye it vppon the Swellinge warme, and soe doe it iij or iiij.
tymes in a daye, alwayes warme, and the patient shall
fynde greate ease in it. Proved.





An other for Swellinge. 40

Take Mylke, Ote meale and Vyolet if you can gett them, if not

of eache of them an ounce, and let all these have a boyle
togither and then stryke it vppon a lynnen Clothe, and soe

and Eveninge, and it will helpe in thrise layeinge on
pved dyverse tymes

ffor Swellinge not broken, and for an Ache/

ffor a Swellinge, or for a Sore in greate heate or for

ffor a Swellinge or for a Bruse, or for akeinge./

Take a quarte of oyle Oliffe, and put into it Baye leaves &
Rose leaves, Parselie, Rosemarie, Cammamell, Sauge and Rewe
and let all these herbes be mynsed small & put into e oyle
let it stand vij dayes in the Sonne in a glasse, Then put

the Sonne nyne dayes And this is a pure oyle for any Ache
diverse tymes proved.
Change yo herbes putinge freshe Seven tymes, At everie
Seven dayes ende change and straine them as you change./

ffor a Swellinge that is of an Ague/

Take of the powlder of Bolus Armoniacus the oyle of Roses the
youlke of an Egge & beane flowre, and a little vinegre,
and temper them altogither, then warme it a little and laye
it vppon leather, and laye it to the Swolne place.
If you will have it to drawe muche make it thick of the
flower; If you will you put a little oyle of Cammamell
vnto it; Or thr iuyce of Howselieke or nightshade pved.

ffor a Swellinge Ache or Bruse.

Take Rewe, Sauge, Worme woode, Smallage, Brookelyme, Rosemarie
flowres or e leaves, Cammamell, Roses of eache of all these a
handfull, & bruse them in a Morter, Then take Pisse or a pottle
of white wyne, & seethe them till they be tender, Then take
a good quantitie of Sheepes Tallowe, & put to it and lett
it seethe till it be thicke, and stirr it for the burneinge, and you
must have Sheepes Suett reddye to keepe it moyste, as
it seethe awaye. Then take this Medycyne & beate it in a
Morter, and laye it as warme as the patient can suffer it,
and it will asswage the Swellinge and kill the Ache wthin
three or fowre tymes dressinge. But looke you keepe it warme
wth Clothes a weeke togither, and laye yo Medycyne alwayes
warme vnto it. This hathe bene often proved.

before you laye on e plaster, and laye the plaster warme
to it. An other waye:

To make yr Salves pure good for Bruse or Swelling

Take of Swynes grease or Sheepes Suett a good quantitie,
and Shred your herbes and put into it, and seethe it togither
till e oyle be greene, then straine it and put it vpp. And
this is the best makeinge of some oyles, Other oyles are
other wise made./

ffor a Bruse

Take a pottle of oyle oliffe, and a pottle of Gascoyne wyne,
and Cammamell and Sotherwoode of eache five handfull
and of wormewoode and fetherfewe of eache fowre handfull
and Sixe handfull of newe Rose leaves, then mynce them

and then straine it and put it in a glasse, and therwithe
annoynte the bruse, and wette a clothe and laye it to it, and
soe Rowle it vpp. And it will heale any bruse very well.
Alsoe take with those herbes Brookelyme and Smallage, and
laye a plaster of Hocsicroction vnto it

ffor an ache in the Armes

A Seare Clothe for Ache./

ffor Ache in the bones a princypall oyntmt

ffor an Ache in the Bones of the Legg

Take a blacke Sheepes head wooll and all, take out the braynes

to it Burr dock leaves, and Primerose leaves & Rootes, boyle

the broathe bathe the legge well agaynst a good fyre, Then laye the wooll the fleshe and herbes about the legg and
soe lett it lye xxiiijtie howres. And soe vse three or fowre
tymes and every tyme washe it in the brothe as hott as
you can suffer it./

 Flos vnguentorum good for all manner of Wound newe
or old, it is good for to put in all Treat. it Sufferethe noe
Corruption nor dead fleshe to grow: And it healethe more in a
Senight Space then others doe in a monethe. And it healethe

To make Gratia Dei./

Take of Turpentyne & Rosen in poulder of each a li. Mastick in poulder ij. ounces,
Virgins waxe x. ounces, olibanu in poulder x. ounces, and Bitteny Planten
verven, Pimpernell & mouseare of eache in lieke quantitie & stampe them well
in a morter, Then Seethe them in a pottle of white wyne to a quarte, then take
them out & straine the iuyce into a vessell & set it over the fyre, & make a softe fyre
vnder it & soone after put in the Rosen and the Terpentyne, the second tyme
put in e Mastick olibanu & the waxe, & let them melt altogither, and straine
it agayne. And this is pure Gratia Dei. And if you will have it soften,
Rypen and drawe muche Put in an ounce of Galbanu, as much of oyle
of Roses and a little of the poulder of Myrra or Gumm Elempany






ffor the wound of a Spare or any other Iron.

Waxe and Tallowe, the iuyce & e Turpentyne and honey and
straine it and when it is some what cold, put the poulder into it
& a little Boras in powlder, and Egremonie brused is pure
good agaynst a bruse beinge layd plaster wise wth grease
vnto e Sore. And it Ceasethe e Ache and Swellinge

ffor the wound of a Spare or any other Iron.
