To make a Specyall Water for Cutt or hurt in any parte
but the Poulder is better

Take an ounce of Allome and halfe an ounce of greene Coperas bothe
burnte and made in to fyne poulder and sett a quart of fayre water
vppon the fyre. put in yo poulder some and some and stirr it togither
and lett it come on the fyre adter e poulder is in and keepe it in a
glasse. And yf you will heale a Sore therwth wett a lynnedn in Clothe
and laye vppon the Sore place. And if you need a Tent take lynte &
wett it in the same & put it in e place, as a Tente. and change it eveninge
& morninge, And it takethe the dead fleshe a waye & healethe it. pved

An Excellent salve for any wound in the
flesshe.

Take a quarter of a li of Cleere Rosen, halfe a
quarter of a li of redd waxe, and halfe a quarter of a
li of Bores grease, a quarterne of fyne sugar,
or some what lesse, frye the bores grease in a pann
Then mingle them togither in a smale Pipkin over
the fire & scumm of the frothe, claryefieinge it soe as
well as you cann. Then poure it out into smale pott
and soe lett it coole. The nature of this salue
is such that it never healethe till it hath drawen
out all the corruption in the wound, and then it healethe
ymeadiatlie./





To breake any thinge and to drawe.

Take Perosen & two or three figg as you will make in quantitie
Shomakers waxe & old lynnen of eache like quantitie but most
part of Perosen, beate them altogither in a Morter till it come
to the thicknes of a plaster then spead it vppon leather pved.

To breake any manner of Sore, as hathe bene divers tymes pved

Take waxe two ounces, Galbanu layd in Vinegre a night and
a daye two ounces Rosen fowre ounces, Turpentyne two ounces
Oyle of Roses an ounce, or freshe butter or Sheepes Suett.
Take and melte the waxe, Rosen the Oyle, or butter or Suett.
Then take e Galbanum and the Vinegre and seethe them
wth the waxe Rosen and Oyle till the Vinegre be consumed
and stirr it for burneinge. put in the Turpentyne and
stirr it well togither and strayne it into fayre water
or into a Boxe. /

A very good Plaster to cause a Sore to breake,
or to breake a Byle. /

Take two handfull of Beane flowre, and one handfull of
wheate Brenn, two ounces of the meale of ffenegreeke,
and a pynt of white wyne or vinegre, three Spoonefull
of hunney & three youlkes of Egg and seethe it altogither
thicke and stirr it for burneinge and laye it to the Sore
as hott as the patient can suffer it. And it will cause the
Sore to rype and heale and bringe it to isshue, and as
often as you change bruse the Sore to voyde the matter.
Sorrell rosted or a lyllye Roote rosted in a browne paper
in the Embers, will breake a Sore.
Or take white Soape and beate it small and temper it well
wth Creame and honney and lett it seethe togither &
make a plaster of it, or temper it with Eysell./

Make a Plaster of the Rootes of Occulus Christi & verven
and it will breake a Byle, and it you will remove it
laye it two fingers from the Byle, and it will breake it
ther. pved.

To breake a fellon and heale it or any
other sore pved.

Take the strongest Ale you can gett, and boyle it vntill
(wth some stirring to keepe it from burninge) come to a salve.





46
To breake a Byle.

Take Galbanum ij. ounces and laye it fowre night in vinegre
and make a decoction and straine it in a cleane vessell wth waxe
and oyle of eache an ounce and Turpentyne and Rosen of them bothe
an ounce, and melt it and straine it and make a plaster when
it is cold as bigge as you will have it breake, and at everie Tenn
howres change it, and it will breake it. pved diverse tymes

To Rypen and loosen Apostemes or any other Sore.

Take the poulder of Perosen the meale of Fenegreake, Turpen-
tyne & a little Galbanum, wth the iuyce of Mallowes and
Swynes grease Seethe them altogither and straine it. Then
put in the ffenegreeke meale and when it is cold wth Leather
laye it to the place as broad as you will have it rype and to breake
or els make this Salve wth freshe butter or Maij butter wch is
better then the grease./

An other to Rypen Apostemes and Byles.

Take Galbanum and melt it wth freshe butter and Seethe it &
straine it and put in a little beane flowre a little honey &
Turpentyne and temper them altogither till it be thicke,
and laye a plaster of leather on the Sore pved./

An other to Rypen Apostemes or Byles.

Take ij. ounces of Galbanum an ounce of Turpentyne
a little perosen and a little waxe, and melt them altogither
in a little freshe butter and put to it a little fenegreake
meale and straine it, and lett it Coole. Proved./

To breake a Fellon./

Take Ribworte, honney and the youlke of an Egge & wheate flowre
and stampe your Ribbworte and wringe out the iuyce into e
rest and keepe it in a boxe or glasse till you have neede pved.

To breake a fellon in a night.

Take poulder made of Cantarides and temper it wth cleane
Sheepes Tallowe and make a plaister therof and laye it therto.

for a fellon.

Take of stone pitche beaten smale e quantitie of an egg, As much bores grease as
halfe an egg, as much Sheepes Suett fynelye tryed as an egg, of yeallowe waxe
the lieke quantitie, as much Turpentyne as a wallnutt, boyle them all to-
gither wth a soft fyer halfe an howre then strayne it into fayre water
& worke it into a Salve and keepe for yo vse. pved. But before you applie e
Salue take e quantitie of a hasill nutt of e best Turpentyne and laye it to the ioynt  & e Skin
of a  newe laid egg vppon yt & the Salve vppon them bothe.





To heale and drawe a Byle. /
Take ye Juyce of Growndsell, Raggworte & Pocheworte
and put therto honney, ye youlke of an Egge and Saffron and
laye all these to the Byle and it will heale and drawe.

For to Rypen Apostemes.
Take oyle an waxe and melt it then take Terpentyne and
Fenegreake meale and stirr them together and put to it
the waxe, and let them seethe a walme and let it
coole and laye it to the Aposteme cold, and you put a little
Galbanum strained to it, it is the better.

For a Byle./
Take ye white of an Egge, Turpentyne & Fenegreeke
meale, and temper it and laye to it. /

An other for the Same.
Take Fenegreake meale, Barlie Meale Turpentyne
Oyle of Roses, & honney, and make a plaster therof
if you have not the Galbanum Plaster./

For the Fyre of Hell.
Take iii quarts of the juyce of Ribbworte and a quarte of
Vinegre and a pynte of honney and seethe them
till halde be washed and put it in a stone pott, and when
you will use it, put a little in a Sawcer, And take a litle
flaxe & wett it therin and laye it on the Sore, and it will
kill the Fyer Probatum est./





For Breninge.
Take ye juyce of Mallowed, Planten & Howselicke and
seethe it wth oyle oliffe or Sywines grease.

For Breninge and Skaldinge.
Take howselicke, Planten, nightshade and Clott leafes
and frye them in freshe butter and Sheepes Suett, and straine
it thoroughe a Clothe and laye the oyle on the Sore And it will
heale it Proved
Alsoe yf you frye the herbes aforesaid with Mallowes till the
Oyle be greene and straine them, and put to the pyle a little
waxe and melt the oyle and waxe togither. It is very good/

The Makeinge of the Oyle of Egge for Breninge
or Skaldinge. Probatum:
First Roste xxtie or xxxtie Eggs till the youlkes be blewe, then
take the youlkes from ye whites and crome the youlkes into a
cleane fryeinge Pann, verye Small and sett it over the
fyre and tarme and tosse them till it come to Oyle The
Pann must be holden a slope wyse when the youlkes beginn
to tarne to an Oyle, Soe that all the youlkes will hange to
the Slyce and the Oyle will droppe and runn downe
lieke Byrdlyme, and then lett it stand in the fryeinge
pann till the Same of the heate be past. Then powre it into
a dishe and annoynte the Sore therwth. /

For Brenninge and Skaldinge.
Take of the Juyce of Planten ii Ounces, of nightshade
ii ounces, of Hempe seede beaten to poulder one ounce, of freshe
Swynes grease vi ounces, of the oyle of Egge if you can gett it ii s.
of unwrought waxe iii ounces of May butter an ounce, and of ye
Juyce of grownd Ivie, and of howselicke ii ounces, and boyle all these
togither & stirr it often for burninge, and seethe it till it be some=
thinge thicke, Then straine it and laye it uppon fyne Lynte

thinn P'ved.





thinn spread, and make it as broad as the Sore place, and
it will heale it. Alsoe take the inner pill of Elder
and frye it wth other thing written for burneinge and Skalding.

Alsoe take Planten, Night shade, Howselieke and Hollyhock
leafes and Seethe them with Swynes grease and Maij butter.

ffor a Skaldinge.

Take vnwrought Waxe, Maij Butter and ground Ive
leaves and boyle them togither and straine it, and it wilbe
lieke a Salve, and wth a feather laye it on the Sore place
And Seethe some Ivie leaves besides in water and Bores
grease or Maij butter and when the Sore is annoynted
laye the leaves vppon it, And put the oyle of Egg to
the Salve.

ffor a skaldinge.

Take Shomakers Shredes and seethe them in runninge water
tilll they be thick and tender and straine the ffatt once
or twise and make oyle of it, and laye a Cypresse
vpon the Sore, and laye the oyle vppon the Cypres
and soe laye it to the Sore.





48
A good Medycyne for a Sore Head or
for any Sore in any place

Take Oyle de Baye ij. ounces, the fatt of a Hogge half
an ounce of ffrankencense and Brimstone in Poulder
of eache a quarter of an ounce, Baye Salt brayed
a groate weighte. The iuyce of femetorye Sorrell
Planten of eache of them an ounce and mingle
them togither, put to it a little Waxe melted and
halfe a Spoonefull of vinegre and make a Plaster
therof and laye it to the place t is Sore.

ffor Breakinge out an Oyntment.

Take Sixe handfull of Sauge in Maij, and seethe
it in freshe Butter vnwashed, and when it is halfe
sodden awaye straine it and annoynte with it.

ffor a Sore Itchinge

Take Baye Salt and drye it in an Oven two or three
tymes, then beate it and temper it with a quantitie of
Hogg grease ans soe annoynte the itchinge place,
and it will Sore Itches.






ffor a Cancer./

Take A pynte of vinegre a penny worthe of Greene Coperas &
a penny worthe of Allome, and Seethe togither. and
when it is cold washe the Canker wth it, And put into the
powlder the Iuyce of Cinquefol; and a good quantitied of
howselieke leaves, else you must have burnt Allome
and put the powlder therof vppon the Sore, And it will
heale any Canker if it be well applyed Morninge and
Eveninge, Proved often.

ffor a Canker

Take halfe a pynte of white vinegre, and put it to a quarte
of honey after that the honey is softlye boyled and cleane
Skimed over a softe fyre. Then put therto halfe a hand-
full of the Croppes of Sauge, and as much burnt Allome
as a Wallnutt, and a Spoonefull of baye Salt. And lett
it boyle halfe awaye, and washe the Sore therwithe pved





49
It is alsoe expedient requisite & necessarie to Chirurgions that have cares
vnder their handes to have in tender consideration the extreame paine, smarte
and Agonie that their Patient doe susteine and Suffer in healinge their
Infirmities. In Consideration wherof it behoveth Chirurgion to make for them
Comfortable and holsome thing. As Restoratives Electuaries, Tysons
and good Syrropes to comforte them in tyme of their Agonie & payne, to
thintent they may be the sooner relieved, & restored agayne to their healthe
and for that cause I mynde to shewe you the makeinge of Conserve of Roses
and many other good Conserves &c.

Conserve of Roses is made in this wise viz.

ffirst gather e fayrest flowres of Roses that you can fynd, and take awaye all the white
from the leaves, Then take what weight you will of those flowres when they be
small brayed in a Morter, and put therto twise the weight of Sugar, and
braye them well togither. then put it in a glasse, and sett it in the Sonne by
the Space of fiveteene dayes, and every daye stirr it wth a wooden slyce
and at the xv.. dayes ende put therto the third parte of the weight of the flowres
of Roses and stirr them well togither. and put them in a Boxe well Searced
or in a glasse well closed. and soe keepe it for it will last iiij. yeares And
if yo Conserve be not strong ynoughe of e Roses in the first yeare, then
take the flowres as you did before and braye them in a Morter verye
cleane and wringe out the Iuyce in to a fayre vessell and mixe your
Conserve and e said Iuyce togither, and lett it stand in the Sonne in
a glasse vessell as longe as it did before tyme and stirr it liekewise
and then your Conserve will be the better and the stronger.

Conserve of Violett is made in this wise.

Gather the Violettes in the Monethe of Aprill and plucke awaye all the greene
Stalkes from the flowres and braye m in a fayre Morter, and put therto as much
Sugar in quantitie as you did to the Conserve of Roses, and sette it in the
Sonne xxx.tie dayes ore mre till they be perfectlie fyned. Then
put it in boxes as you did the Conserve of Roses and if it be not Stronge
ynoughe of the flowres, then you may strengthen it the next yeare lieke-
wise as ye did e Conserve of Roses.






Conserve of Rosemarye flowers thus made. Viz

Gather your flowres in the Monethe of Iune, pick them cleane and
take nothinge but the cleane flowres, Stampe them and take the weight
of them as you did before of e Roses and violett and put therto
lieke portion of Sugar and lett it stand in the Sonne as longe as
yo Conserve of Roses did till it be fyned, and keepe it as you did yo
other Conserves ffor this is Comfortive & Cordiall.

To make Conserve of Roses an other waye.

Take Roses before e Sonne Rysethe and for pncipall the budd of them
and clipt awaye all the white next the knobb, and stampe them
small the Smaller the better. then put powlder of Sugar to m
and after braye them till the Rosett hathe fully rubified the Sugar. Then take
all & put it in a Pann, and put therto a pound of Honney dispumat
and sofylie wth a lente fyre let the boyle till the honney be consumed
then take them of and putt them in a pott & you must stirr them till
they be colde. Some men vse not to boyle them, but reason will
the contrarie.

How to make Conserve of Damecynes

Gather yo Dameceynes in the latter ende of August for then they
be not fullye rype, then sett them on the fyre in a pann of water
till they boyle then put e water from them, and lett them Coole
And when they be neare cold straine them thoroughe a Cullender
and when they be soe strained put all that is soe strainedin an
earthen pann & sett it over e fyre and stirr it well, and if it
waxe over thicke put therto a little of the liquor it was sodden in
and boyle it softlie over an easie fyre, Skum it cleane and lett it
boyle till it waxe thick And then put as much Sugar as will Suffuce the quantitie
therof & stirr it well for burneinge till it be well standinge. Then take it
from the fyre, and stirr it till it be cold. Then put it in a Boxe that is well
seared or in a vessell of pewter or Glasse, or els yo labour is in vayne.
And this Conservative is verie holsome for them that have the Axes.
And if you have noe Sugar you may make it wth honney: then make it
as I said before, Save that the liquor that the honney is sodden in
must be often sett on e fyre and often taken from the fyre till it be thick
& cold; And this is the difference of makeinge Conserve of damesins
with honney & sugar. That wch is made wth Sugar is good for the Sicke
personnes, and t wth honney for them t are in healthe.





Conserve of Quinces is made in this wise. viz.

Gather yo Quinces when they be rype cleave them and pick out the Core
then pare them and put them in a Brasse pott full of water, and seethe
them therin till they be tender. Then straine them thoroughe a Cullender
and after thoroughe a strainer, then put it in an Earthen pann over
the fyre and stirr it a good while till it be neare standinge. Then put
in yo Sugar or yo honney and boyle it till it be standinge and then
it is comfortive to the bodye.

To Conserve Quinces in Syrrope allwayes reddye to be served
whole or in quarters./

After yo Quinces be pared and Cored, Seethe them till they be tender and
Softe, then laye them out till they be cold, in the meane tyme take of the
same liquor ij Quartes or more accordinge to the nomber of the Quinces
that you will keepe, and put therin the Cores and some other small quinces
cut in small pieces, Seethe them in the liquor to make the Syrrope
stronge. Straine them, and put into the liquor being two or three quart
one pynte of Rose water and for everie quarte of Liquor one halfe
pound of Sugar. Seethe them agayne over a softe fyre togither
of Coles, till the Sugar be insoporated wth the liquor, then put in yo
Quinces. Lett them Seethe softlye till you perceyve t yo liquor be as
thick as life honney, then sett them to Coole, and take them out. Laye
them in a Traye or Treene platter, till they be cold. then take j
one ounce of brused Cinna mome, and some whole Cloves put them
wth some of the quinces in the Syrrope, and when it is cold, laye a Larde
of quinces in your glasse called a Gestilon glasse, or an Earthen pott well
glased. Then strewe a little of yo Cynnamome vppon the Quinces
then powre some Syrrope, Laye on an other Lare of your Quinces and
agayne of yo Spice and Syrrope and soe forthe till you have done.
and Cover them over wth Syrrope about two fingers, Cover them close
and wthin two or three dayes looke to them and when you fynd your
Syrrope sunke put in more and soe reserve them. these are to be
Served into the table wth Syrrope.

To preserve Quinces Redd.

Take of e worst of yo Quinces and cutt them in peeces and put them into water
and boyle them till the water be of the color of a pale Clarret wyne,
then strayne it out. Then Core yo quinces and pare them, and put to
every pynt of that liquor a li. of Quinces and a li of Sugar, put m
all togither into the pann, and let them boyle open till they growe
tender, and the skum taken awaye, then Cover m and lett the whole
tyme of boyleynge them be three howers. keepe them wth turning
and boyle m as close covered as you can.





Pottage of Quinces is made in this wyse, viz
Take ye paringe & the Cores of Quinces, and Seethe them as
tender as you cann, Streyne them fayre wth all the liquor
that they be sodden in. Then putt all the matter into an
earthen pann & sett them over a softe fyre, and stirr them
well til they be thick. Then put therto a quantitie of Sugar
or Honney, and if you will have it thick shortlie then put in as
much grated bread, as is convenient, and when it is thick
put therto poulder of Ginger Cynnamone, Gallingall,
Cloves, Mace and Graynes and stirr them well togither
Then put it in a close vessell of Earthe or of woode, and it
will last halfe a yeare.

Chard of Quinces is made in this wise.
Take a quarte if Clarified honney two ounces of Pepper
small searsed mingle them well togither. Then take xxxtie
Quinces and x Wardens, pare them and seethe them in
good worte till they be tender, Then braye them in a morter
boyle them till they be well medled and thick, Then take it
from the fyre and stirr it well, and when it is neare cold put
therto poulder of Ginger, a quarter as much of Ballingale
as much of Cuvell well pouldred and mixe them well togither wth
a slyce, Then lett it Coole and put it in boxes. And yf you will make
Chard of Wardens then put xxxtie Wardens and x Quinces
and make it as before./

A good waye to make Conserve of Quinces, Pved.
Take your Quinces pare them Core them and then way them,
and take to everie pound and halde of Quinces a pound of
sugar. Then put your Quinces to the fire in what you will
boyle them in and put so much fayre water to them as
will halfe cover them, Cover them close and then lett
them boyle in that water till they be tender, then power
in your Sugar and never stirr your Quinces, let them
boyle close covered till they be of the color of good Clarret
wyne. Then keepe your fire quicke and stirr them contyn=
nullie till such tyme as they come to that height as they
come cleare from the skillett syde.





A Syrrope of all manner of informities in mans bodie.
Take Hope, Horehounde, Savorye, Loveache, and Emulacampane
Stampe them and straine them. Then take liquores well brused
Figge and Reysones well broken, and put it all to the liquorache
boyle them softlye over an easie fyre till it be well. Then Coole
it a little and straine it and keepe it in fayre vessells For
this is Syrrupus Compositus that will heale all manner of
informities that is in mannes bodie./

A Syrrope of five herbes viz
That is Femetorye, Scabius, Wylde Borrage, Sowethistell and Marye=
goulde gather thes herves in June, of eache of them in licke quantitie
but of Scabius and Wyld Borage, for they be drye therfore you must
take the more of them after the quantitie that you will make your
Syrrope. Then Stampe eache of these herbes by him self in a morter
and wringe out the Juyce into a fayre Bason. Then take
in lieke quantitie of eyther of them and put them togither
in an Earthen pann and make this Syrrope as the other
is made before, And this is good to eate everye morninge
for all evells and Corrupted Ayres.

To make green Ginger./
Take Racenes of Ginger as many as you will & pare them cleane &
laye them in fayre ale and water, and let them steepe in the same
water iii or iiii dayes & then take out the Racenes. Then boyle ye
water over ye fyre, put in all the Racenes, when the liquor is colde, &
let itstand ii or iii dayes, then take out the Racenes as you did before, and
boyle the same water agayne, and put your Racens agayne therin, and
soe use them till your Racens be softe and well Swolne. Then when ye
water is neare colde, take a pottle of the best wyne that you can have for
the better your wyne is the better your Syrrope will be, and put therto a quart
of Clarified honey, and sett it over a softe fyre and lett it boyle easely
Then put therto ii penny worthe of longe Pepper & a quantitie of
Cloves, Mace, Graynes, Canellorna made in poulder very fyne, and
let it boyle well togither till it be somethinge thick. Then take it
from ye fyre and let it stand till it be mylke warme. Then yf you like
you may put a quantitie of poulder of Ginger. Then take the Racens
and put them into the Receipts in a close vessell till you be disposed
to eate thereof./





Sirupe of Fumetorie how it is made
Gather your fumetorie in the Monethe of June, pick it cleane
and braye it in a fayer Morter, and wringe out the juyce into
a fayre vessell and stirr it well, then take the whites of iiii or v
newe layed eggs take out the Cockbreade and worke them in
a dishe till they be white, then put half the white to the juyce
of the herbes and stirr them well togither; and sett it on the
fyre till it begin to boyle. Then straine it thoroughe a fyne
strainer. Then put therto the other part of the white of ye
Egge, and as much honney or Sugar as will suffice the liquor
& sett it over the fyre, and stirr it but little, when it beginethe
to seethe take it from the fyre and straine it agayne, then sett it
over the fyre agayne and lett it seethe easelie, till it be the
thicknes of honey. But let it be well stirred that is burne
not to the bottome. And when it is cold put it in a cleane
Pott of gen or of Stone, and stopp it fast till you will use it.

Sirope of Roses.
Beate ye ;eaves of your Roses small and take the juyce from them.
Then take to two pound of the Juyce, one li of Sugar Clarified
and one li of Honey Clarified and sett them over a fyre and let
them seethe softlye, and then take them of.

Syrope of Violetts.
Take ye Juyce of violetts cleane strained, and put to it as
muche honey clarified medled wth as much Sugar clarified
as is of the juyce aforesaid, and let them seethe softlye over a
fyre till the Sugar doth predominate, then take it from the fyre
and put it up &c.

Sirope of Absinthium for the headache that
comethe of the fumes of the Stomake./
Take ye Juyce of Wormewoode cleane purged, and put therto
Honey and Sugar medled togither. Then sett them over
a fyre and boyle them softlye till the Sugar be principall
and in the seethinge put to it an ounce of Vinegre and
then sett it from ye fyre, and when it is cold put it in a
Stone Pott./





52
Syrrope of ffennell.

Take ffennell Rootes beate them small medle the Iuyce cleane strai-
ned wth Sugar & honey cleane clarified, and lett it seeethe softlie
over a fyre till the Sugar have the dominion. then put it vp. &c.

ffennell Rootes in Syrrope for the Collick.

Take ffennell Rootes cutt them in small longe peeces.
Then Seethe them in fayre water as you may Savinge them
whole. Then take a li. of cleane clarified Sugar and add therto
a quart of Clarified honey, and softlye let them seethe, that
your Sugar seethe not highe, Then put them in an Earthen pott
and eate them as you thonke good morneinge and eveninge.

Syrrope of fumetorie/

Take the Iuyce of fematorie cleane strained and take Sugar,
& Honey cleane Clarified, & put it to yo iuyce and seethe
them as is afore said, and when it is cold put it vp.

Syrrope of Vinegre.

Take Vinegre seethe it softlye, Skum it cleane, then take it
of and put therto two part of Sugar & honey as aforesaid
Seethe them in all degrees.

Raddishe Rootes in Syrrope.

Take Raddishe Rootes, seethe them in fayre water softe and ten-
der, then Seethe them wth iiij. ounces of Suger & one ounce
of Clarified honey. Skum it and put it vpp.

Oximell

Clarifie Honey fayre and Cleane and a li. of Honey must have
halfe a pounde of Sugar Clarified; Seethe yo Sugar wth yo
Honey & puer them till it be standinge most part of honey. and
then straine them and putt them vp.





Conserve of Quinces.

Pare your Quinces and Core them and Seethe them in white wyne softe
and tender, then put to them cleane Clarified Sugar, as much as
you thincke sufficient, then sett them over a fyre and lett them
boyle softlye till the wyne be cleane consumed, then let them
seethe easelie the Space of halfe a quarter of an howre.
Then take them of and sett them on a Barrell and wth
a round staffe stirr them softlye till they be neare cold.
the greate heate past, then put therto Cinomome, Ginger,
Gallingale, Graynes and longe Pepper by even portions
And stirr it till it be cold & then put it vpp to your vse./

Conserve of Wyldings

Take wylding and pare them and seethe them in fayre water
as tender as reason requirethe & as softe as may be.Then
straine them & cast awaye the Cores. Then meddle them
with Sugar & put to them a quantitie of honey & softly
lett them dissolve, then take them of and stirr them till
they be cold. It causethe an Appetite agaynst hott
feavors & hott Agues in the Stomake.

Conserve of Wardens.

Take Wardens pare and Core them & seethe them in white
Wyne as tender aa reason requirethe till the most part
of the wyne be consumed, then straine it & put it into
cleane Clarified honey or puer Clarified Sugar and lett
them boyle wth easie fyre. Then put to them round
Pepper, Cinomom, Cloves, Mace and Ginger in lieke quantity
and of Sanders in powlder as much as halfe the other
Spyces, And put strained Dates & Almond therto. Et factu est.

Conserve of Burrage flowers

Take Burrage flowers and beate them small & meddle them
wth Sugar & after wth honey as is aforesaid & soe boyle it as aforesaid.

Conserve of Buglas.

Take e flowres of Buglas beate them Small and temper them with Sugar
then take j.li of Honey dispumat & soe boyle them vp wth stirringe
after e takeinge from e fyre till it be cold. then put it vp. Et factu





53
To make Methaglin

Take Avence, Sauge, Isope, Saverie, Rosemarie, Thyme, Peneriall
Egremonie the Root & all of Englishe Madder, the Root and all
of Saxifrege of each of these one handfull, alsoe of Puliall, Mowntane
Lavender, Horsemynte, Callamynte, Bitteny, Waybread, Rootes & all
of wyld Sauge, Burrage, Baye leaves halfe a handfull of eache of
these, Alsoe of Tansey, Mouseare, daysie, Parsley Rootes, ffennell
Rootes, Hound tonge, Peleter, Verven, the Rootes of Elenacam-
pana, Origanu, Cammamell, mugworte wormewoode a quarter
of a handfull of eache of these. Gather all these herbes before
Mydsommer for then their vertue is moste in them. Pick them
& laye eache by him selfe; And this proportion of herbes will serve
for a Pipe of Metheglin. And when you have proportioned your
herbes washe them and brase them in a Morter. Then pportion
the water by the Gallon for soe much Methaglin as you will make
and sett it on the fyre in cleane vessell & herbes therwth and boyle
them well togither. Then with a Rake gather out the herbes from the liquor
and wthdrawe the fyre. Then take the herbes and put them in a cleane
vessell and put therto a payledull or two of hott water, and lett it stand
till you can indure to straine that liquor int a pann or lede thoroughe
a Sive of Heare. Then put to everie fowre Gallons of liquor a Gallon of
Honey or more if neede be, and stirr it well togither a longe tyme.
And if you will prove whether it be stronge ynoughe or noe: Take an
Egg and put therin, and if you may see it the breadth of a Peny
aboue the liquor then is it stronge inoughe of the honey. Then
make fyre vnder the liquor & cover it close and when it beginethe to
Seethe Skum it cleane & then drawe the fyre. and when it is a little
rested put in a cleane Mashinge Tubb. And lett it stand therin a daye
and a night till it be perfectlie colde. Then ther will rise a frothe
thervppon. wch Skum awaye cleane and lette it stand an other daye and night
and Skum it cleane agayne. Then put therto a paylefull of good newe
barme, and stirr it not but lett it stand a nother daye and a night,
and Skum it the third tyme, And let it stand an other daye and night & skum
it noe more. Then clense it as you doe good Ale. and tunn it vpp in
vessell fast Stopped wth fayre Claye, that noe ayre can come out ther-
of. And if it be thus made it is as good as wyne.





To make a Tisan to cleare the Pypes./

Take a Pynte of Cleane Barley, and eyght Pynt of good
stale Ale or runninge water, then boyle then a while togither
and put therto a quantitie of brused Liquoris and a quantitie
of Annise seedes boyle them a while togither, then take Roote
of Helenacampana cleane washed & mynced, and a handfull
of Organum & a handfull of Soape & put in the said liquor
& boyle them till the liquor be halfe wasted, and then straine
it, and drinke it cold: Probatum.

A Confortative is made in this wise.

Take a pound of Dates, pick out the Stones and the
filme & stampe them in a Morter. Then take a quantitie
of honey and melt it wth the dates, & with these Spices follow-
inge. Viz. Take Gallingal, Nuttmegg, Cloves, longe
Pepper, Maces of eache of them halfe anounce, and a qrte
of Suger. beate yo Spices to fyne poulder. Then meddle
all these matters togither, and boyle it on a Softe fyre till
it be thick. Then take it from the fyre and put therto an
ounce of Ginger & stirr it altogither till it be cold. Then
put it in Boxes or Glasses & vse to eate therof everye
morneinge & Eveninge: for it will restore man or
woman that are in feoblenes & weake of nature.

To preserve Apricott wthout water

Take your Apricott and pare them verie thin and put
them into some Deepe vessell that they may be reserved
and beate yo sugar verie fyne and put to everie pound
of Apricott a li of sugar. Then put your sugar vppo
yo Apricott and lett them stand till the sugar be melted
wth the iuyce of the Apricott. Then put them to the fyre
till they be boyled enough. Any kind of white
frute may be preserved after this manner, but in any
wise they must be boyled wth a soft fyre.





54
ffor the greate and necessarie vse that ther is of Sugar and of
Honey in Conserves Syrropes and many other thing for
healthe good and comfort of man and that beinge clarified
and purefied thay are farr better vse & more wholsome
for mans bodye. It is a pryncypall thinge to know how
to Clarifie them.

Sugar is thus Clarified

One Pound of Sugar must have fowre ounces of water, and when
they be dissolved, put two whites of Egg and wth a Short Stumped
brushe Swing the water. Then take the fome of the whites of the
Egg from the Sugar disolved over the fyre and lett it not seethe
but straine it and keepe it till you occupie it as you have neede.
Everye li of Sugar must have an ounce of honey to Conserves.
To Syrropes take of eyther lieke quantitie.

The opinion of many men is. That Conserves are made but pperlie of
Iuyce of leaves, seeinge that Syrrope should be added therto for
liquid. But your leaves to be beatensmall and medled wth Sugar
mixed wth honey. Neverthelesse it is to be considered that rawe Sugar
is flegmatique vnlesse it be clarified. As it appearethe by Sugar
Candye how it is altered by Clarifieinge. Therefore the purer
yo Conserves are made, the greater creditt it wilbe to the maker
& soonest healthe vnto the patiente.

But put noe Honey to Conserves vnlesse it be a Small quantitie
To a li. of Sugar an ounce of Clarified honey. But vnto
Syrropes put as much honey as Sugar as hath bene said, and soe
decoct everie conserve.

Honey is Clarified in this manner. Viz

If you will Clarifie a quarte of honey put therto halfe a Gill of
cleare water and seethe it over an easie fyre till the water be welled
agayne, and Skum it clean and then it may be put in potions
or other medycynes or Conserves wthout perill. or els not./

To Clarifie Honey with Sengrene.

Put yo Honey in a Pann & sett it over a softe fyre, and when it beginethe yo
boyle put thertp the Iuyce of Sengreene & lett them boyle togither and Skum
them cleane, and lett it boyle till it be as thick as it was in the beginninge
and if you have iiij. Gallons of honey put therto a pottle of the iuyce of
Sengreene, And soe you may make yo pportion after that rate.







How to make greene dryed Apples./

Take yo Apple while it is smale and greene about
the biggnes of a walnut, then skald them wth a
leysurelie fyre till the peele may be taken of
Then peele them and put them into the same
water agayne and let them stand vppon Embers
till they become greene. Then take them from
the Core saveinge the stalke and the Core whole
togither, Then take the pulpe and put it into a Dishe vppon a Chaffinge Dishe of ^Coles and drye it
ther the space of halfe an howre. You must take
a lieke quantetie in wayte of sugar and yo stuffe,
your sugar beinge made very fyne, boyle them togither
one howre wth contynuall stirringe. Then laye
your Coffins made fitt for the Core of the Apple, and
when yo stuffe is readie, fashon yo aplle to yo
owne liekeinge with the Core and the stalke put
in the Coffin made of a Card. To make it the
best waye put in Amber Greece. and drye them
vppon a plate.





55
Medicines for the head Ache.

Agaynst the payne pf the head cominge of an Ague: Take a handfull of sage
& choppe it smale wth salt, and put therto two or three spoonefull of vinegre, & set it
on the fyre, puttinge therto a few crummes of wheate breade and put it in a lyne
Clothe as hott as may be suffered, fro one eare to the other, and vse it for it is a good med^ycyne

A nother for the same.

Take Bittony, Alehoofe & Cammomell ana.Springle those over with a little vinegre
and warme them over a chafeinge dishe of coles, and them hott to the hinder part of yo
head, and behinde bothe your eares.

an other.

Take Hemlock and seethe them till they be soft as papp, then laye them to yo heade
wher the payne is, and let it lye all night, and in the morninge laye on an other
hott playster of the same, and soe doe two or three tymes, and yow shalbe sound.

an other

Heate Charvell and Rosmarye betweene two tyles, and laye them to the hinder pte
of the head betweene the Crowne and the Nape hott.

ffor the Head Ache.

Take Redd Rose leaves or oyle of Roses, Rosemary, Margerum,
Camamell, Bitteny and Lavender and stoppe the eares wth it.
And if you will purge the head put Pelletorie of Spayne vnto it
And this will helpe him. Alsoe still the herbes and laye the
water to the forehead and to the pulses, or seethe the herbes in
runninge water, and straine the water and laye it as aforesaid.

To Clense the head and to put awaye the filthe and
to fasten the teethe that be loosened.

Take the Roote of Pelletorie Spayne and chewe theron often
tymes iij. dayes and it will clense the head and doe awaye ake-
inge, and fasten the teethe that be loose./

ffor an Akeing head.

Take a Rose Cake and laye vppon a lynnen Clothe, and put
powlder of Nuttmegg vnto it and a little rose water
and soe laye it to the head and it will give ease.

ffor to take awaye the payne in the head or for deafnes

Take Margerum and bruse it, and stopp the eares wth it and
it will take awaye the payne.

ffor the Head Ache./

Take Cammamell flowres & Rosemarie flowres & Lavender
of eache a quarter of a handfull, and beate them in grosse
poulder, put Cummin in poulder an ounce & Margerum in poulder
and put all this poulder togither in a bagg of Cotten or
Sarsenett, and make the bagg as bigg as the Palme of yo
hand. And make two bagg and laye one to the Crowne
of the head at night & the other in the morneinge and
when you take awaye the one, warme the other & laye it to
the same place.






ffor e Head Ache.

The Seed of Ivie medled wth Vinegre & oyle of Roses
helpethe the headache of ot be annoynted therwth.

ffor the Megrom in the head.

Take iij.d weight of the Roote of Pelletorie one Penny
weight of Spikenard and grynd them small togither
& boyle them in good Vinegre. Then take a Sawserfull
of Honey three Sawsers full of Mustard, and when
the liquor is boyled doe the Mustard and honey togither
& medle them altogither; And of that let the Patient
vse halfe a Spoonfull at a tyme, and hold it in his
mouthe as longe as a man may saye two Creedes,
then Spett it out into a vessell and after take more
& doe soe tenn or twelue tymes, and wthin a little
while after thou shalt eate. And then afterward a
little before Eveninge vse this x. or xij. tymes, and
alwayes spett it out, And when thou goest to bedd washe
cleane the mouthe & drinke a draught, and vse this
iij. dayes and you shall see much glutt & fylthe come fro you.

An other for the Megrom in the head.

Take an ounce of Gallingale, an ounce of Ginger,
halfe an ounce of Nutmeg a quarter of an ounce
of Cloves, a penny weight of Camamell flowres
dryed, of Spiknell a penny weighte, of Anniseedes
a quarter of an ounce, of Helenacampana halfe
an ounce, of Licorishe, & Sugar of eache an ounce, And
temper all these togither in fyne poulder and lett the
Sicke vse this powder. One Spoonfull at once, and it will
helpe by God helpe.

ffor the Megrom or for the Ague.

Take Stonecrope a handfull of newe made Mustarde a
farthing worthe, and of Ale a pynte, and stampe the herbe
and put the iuyce into Ale and Mustard, and straine
it & drinke it leuke warme; And if you take it to purge
the head, put the iuyce of Pelletorie into it. And this will
take the filthe out of the head. Proved.





56
ffor the greatest and oldest payne of the head./

Take the Iuyce of ground Ivie put it in a Spoone and snuf
it vpp into the nose. I purifiethe the head Mervelouslie
and takethe awaye the payne. Very well proved.

Alsoe the Iuyce of Ivie put into the Nose helpethe the
Polupe in the Same.

ffor Impostemes in the head, or for Singinge in the head,
or for any deane in the head or deafenes.

Take ivie that frowethe in the Ashe tree, and pare of the
outermost of the Mosse, and the outermost barke and take e
myddle barke wth the stick and cleave the stickes and soe still
them take of the same water, and put a little in the Eare
on that side that is greived, and stopp the eare wth black
wooll, and lye with the Sore Side vpper ward. pbatum.

ffor one that hath bene deafe a longe tyme.

Stampe Antes Egg and put therto the Iuyce of Swynes
Cresses or Knott grasse, And take two or three dropps at
a tyme in to yo eare when you goe to bedd stopping e
eare with black wooll/

An other for deafnes.

Take the Iuyce of an Oyster that wch runnethe
forth when you open it. And put a good quantitie
therof wth a Spoone in the Eare when you goe ot bedd
and stopp yo eare wth black wooll, and it will cure the
deafnes. Proved in old men that have bene a verye
longe tyme deafe./






ffor the Pynne & the Webb & to cleare the sight./

Take Redd Rose leaves, wyld Thyme, Redd ffennell, Selendyne and
Rewe or Verven, and seethe these herbes in runninge water,
very well an straine the water & not the herbes. Some take
but Rose leaves, Rosemarye, Thyme & Isope, and seethe them &
straine the water, And put a little Allom to it. And these
two Medydynes have bene proved diverse tymes and dothe
alwayes helpe, And put a droppe in the Eye at a tyme,
If these herbes be sodden in white wyne it is pure good./

ffor the Pinn and the webb in the Eye.

Take three leaved grasse that hathe a shaddowe of white in it and
take the iuyce of it and droppe it in the Sore Eye, and it will
kill the pinn & the webb./

Agaynst the payne in the Eyes or the Webb./

Take Howseloeke, Violetts, Selendyne e white of an Egg
ffennell, Rosemarye, and the water of honey, and take for
Rosemarye Strawberrye if you will, All these waters be good.

ffor the Pynn and the webb in the Eye.

Take Daysie leaves and Rootes iij. handfull washe them
cleane and stampe them, then straine them and take the whites
of two Egg and beate them and put the Cleare of them into
the Iuyce of the daysies, and halfe a Spoonefull of honey & a
little Coperas white, and temper all these togither, And soe
droppe the iuyce of all into the Eye Eveninge and morninge
as the patient lyethe in his bedd, and it will helpe the pinn and
the webb. But he must forbeare Egg oynions, Mustard & all
stronge drinkes and Wynes, and keepe out of the wynd, and
vse this till it be whole.

To breake the Pearles beinge over growne on the Eye.

Take Rose water & put therto a little white Coperes. Then
take daysies that have a white flowre, wth a redd Ringe
about the edges, the leaves and the flowres, and of it be
in winter take the Rootes and put the iuyce therof to the
water, and it will break the Pearle beinge droppped in e Eye.

ffor Sore Eyes.

Take Howslieke t is white and still it, and take Seaven Spoonefull
of this water and drinke it fastinge, and vse this./





57
ffor Runninge Eyes./

Take black Snayles and seethe them in runninge water, and take the
grease that is on the water, and annoynte the Eyes. or still them./

ffor Wateringe Eyes./

Take a Redd Cole and annoynte it wth the white of an Egg,
and laye it to the wateringe Eyes when you goe to bed./

ffor to Clarifie the Eyes and for dymnes of them./

Take Rewe Howselieke and Selendyne and Stampe them wth
a little honey & put it in a lynnen Clothe, then put it between
two basons close and still it over a pott seetheinge over a fyre,
and take the water and put a droppe in the Eye at a tyme.

ffor dymnes of the Eyes./

Take Rewe and Red fennell and stamoe them togither and strai
it, and lett a litttle therof fall vppon the ball of the Eye, And
doubles it is a pretious water for the Eyes and diverse tymes
pved./

ffor the Sight of the Eye doctr Hawswell

Take womans mylke and Rose water, pf each of them a Sawser
full and put to it the whites of two Egg beaten soe that the
Skum arise then take linnet or fyne flax and wett it in these
three aforesaid thing beinge temperes togither and soe wett
laye it to the Eyes nightlye, And it will helpe it whether it
be a blood or other wise hurt. Probatum./

A pretious water for the sight of the Eye.

Take Howselieke, Redd fennell, Rewe, Bittenye, Egremonye
Pimpernell and Sellendyne of eache a lieke quantitie,
and washe them cleane, and stampe them and seethe them
in a quart of white wyne, till the liquor be almost sodden
awaye, then straine it and putto it three spoonefull of honey
and seethe the liquor & it fayre and Softlye & clarified
straine them agayne and put it into the Eye, a dropp at a
tyme, and soe doe often but lett it be cold when you vse it.

Oyle of Rosemarye flowres stilled in the Sonne
or in Sand comfortethe Synnewes, wasteth the webb,
and all other payne in the Eyes, alsoe Spott &
Pimples in the face.

Take Rosemarye flowres, and fill a glasse full, stop it close wth a lynnen
Clothe wth mastick and burye it in Sand a monethe or more till
the flowres be consumed to water, and straine it and purifie it
in the Sand./





To Clense the face or any other place.
Put dragagant a Night in Rose water, and in the
morninge, put therto Boras and a little Campher
and Annoynt the face therwith.

A Medycyne for Rednesse and Pimples on ye face.
Take a Spoonfull of the juyce of Sorrell, the white
of one Egg beaten into an Oyle, Two Spoonefulls
of Sallett oyle, halfe a Spoonefull of Vinegre, halfe
a Spoonefull of Rosewater. Beate all these well
togither and soe use therof with a fether./

For bleedinge at the Nose./
Take ye poulder of Mumme & put into the Nose, or make a plaster
of the poulder and the Glace of an Egge and laye it to the browes.

To stint bleedinge at the Nose.
Take a little redd Sage and burne it uppon a Cole that it may
smoke, and then rubb out the fyre and put it to your nose, and lett
it not longe carrye ther but take it out a while and put it
in agayne, and doe soe twise or thrice and it will stoppe
the bleedinge. Alsoe the bone of a Carpes head beaten to
poulder and given the bleeder to drinke is verie good./

For bleedinge at the Nose or any other place or wound,
or for Spottinge of blood.
Take Planten leaves small and greate, howselicke leaves & Bitteny
leaves and stampe them in a morter wh baye Salt and laye it
to the nose And if it be for a Sore take noe salt. And this hath
bene often tymes proved.





for the Same
For Spotting of blood.
Alsoe take Mynte, Rewe, and Bitteny and seethe them well in good
Mylke and give it to drinke it at morneing & at night.
Mallowe leaves woodbyne leaves & Adders tonge leave are specyall
good for the same. But Woodbyne leaves to clensethe gore blood.

A poulder to stoppe blood.
Take Sanguis dracoms Gumm of Arabie, Bolus Armamack and
make them in poulder. Alsoe the hott donge of a hogg is good alone by it self.

For Spottinge of blood.
Take ye fayre of Bitteny and temper it wth Gotes milke, And lett
the patient drinck therof two dayes./

A medycyne for the Toothe Ache./
Let ye ptie grieved bynd his head verye hard about wth a cloathe and soe laye himselfe
downe uppon his bedd uppon the same side that his teethe doe ake on
Then take a little Angelica, Fetherfewe Cressis seede, and some little
quantitie of the oyle of bitter Almondes pounded togither in a morter,
and afterwardes betweene your fingers force some three drops of the juyce therof
into the eare that lyes uppwardes of the ptie grieved, and soe let him dispose
himselfe to sleepe.
for ye Toothe
Ache.

For the Toothe Ache.
Take an ounce of the Roote of Pelletorie of Spayne in poulder, and a peny
worth of Ginger and as much Pepper beaten to poulder and
finelye sersed, And put all the poulders togither, and temper
them well togither, Then put a little of this poulder over the
Toothe and it will take awaye the payne, and make the mouthe to
water, and Spett out the water as it comethe. But looke you
take of the poulder of the Pepper and Ginger xxtie penny weight
for ye poulder of the pelletorie.

An other for the Toothe Ache.
Take ye second barke of white Hassell, a quantitie of Pepper
& Baye Salte and beate them altogither and then make of ym
small Balls of the bignes of peasen, and then put them
in Lynnen or Cypresse and knitt it fast wth thrid. Then take
Vinegre in a Swacer, and boyle the balls in it uppon a Chafinge
dishe of Coles. Then take eache of the balls one after another
and laye them hott to the sore toothe or in it, And it will helpe
it pved./
If the Toothe be hollowe take Henbane and beate it to poulder
and melt it wth waxe, and make a little pellet & put it into the
hollow toothe.





For the Ache in the Toothe or for
Swellinge in the Cheeke./
Take Freshe Butter and melt it wth a little Waxe and
Frankencense and put to it a little Pepper wth three dropps
of Aquavitae and melt them altogither, and if the toothe be
hollow stopp it wth this, and annoynte the gummes and
Cheeke wth it. And keepe out of the wynd three dayes./

26250

For the Mouthe that is full of Bladders
or in greate heate.
Take Honey and Wyne Vinegre of eache a Pynte, and sett
it over the fyre and Skum it as it rysethe, and put unto it
a Spoonefull of burnt Allom in poulder, and half a Spoonfull of
baye Salt, And lett them boyle altogither halfe an howre
and put it in a glasse and annoynte the sore place. pved.

An other for the Same./
Take Sauge, Cinquefol and Woodbyne leaves of eache a handfulland seethe them in a quarte of Runninge water till they be softe,
Then put in a peece of Allom as bigg as a wallnutt and iii
Spoonefull of Hunney, And straine it thoroughe a Clothe when
it is well sodden, And then washe the same mouthe therwith
softlye; And keepe a Spoonefull in your mouthe a greate
while, Then Spett it out and take an other. And use this
And put a spoonefull of Honey unto it. Proved./





A Soveraigne & approved remedie for the
Squmantia or Sore throate./
Take Sauge, Cammamell, Woodbynes and Cullombynes
of eache a handfull, Angellica, Dragones, Planten
Mouseare, Bayes the five leaved grasse, Rosemarie and
Violette of eache a quarte of a handfull, Three Jewes
Eares Boyle all these a Sufficient quantitie of run=
ninge water one howre, Straine it and put therto of
Roche Allom two ounces and of honey one Spoonefull,
Then let them boyle one quarter of an howre more. Take
of this water and all to washe and gargill the mouthe
and throate therwithe, and when it is well clensed let the
patient Swallowe downe some of the water at every dressing
And use it thus five or Sixe tymes in a daye till you be well.

An Oyntement good for the Same.
Take Sauge Woodbynes, Callumbynes, Cummamell Ange
lica, Dragones Planten, Mouseare & Rosemarie of eache
halfe a handfull, Poplar leaves, heaven Ladder Larks
Tonge, Orches that is the Cocke wth Spotts in the leaves
Serpents Tonge, Lovage, Smallage, Bonesett, Growndsell
dill, wormewoode and fetherfewe of eache on handfull
Boyle all these in the pound of May butter clarified three
quarters of an howre. Straine it and wth two ounces of waxe
make therwth an Oyntment. With this annoynte the place
outwardlye. And make a plaster soe broad as will cover
the griefe all over. Use it morninge and Evenine till
you Syne remedye. Proved by one Person an hundred
tymes and more when the Phisition could doe noe good
euther by blood lettinge or otherwise./





A Medycyne for ye Swelling in the
Throate, and for the Stomake.
Take Malmsey halfe a pynte & as much freshe
butter as an Egge and Clarifie bothe togither. Then
take Nutmege and a little Englishe Saffron and
make them to poulder & put bothe these poulders to=
gither to the Malmsey & butter, and boyle all these
togither a little, and put a little Treacle therto, and
give it the sicke to drinke as warme as he can
drinke it & as often as he will. And lett him be layd
in his bedd, and cover him wth many Clothes that he
may Sweate and after this sweate let him be keept
close in his chamber three dayes that he take not
the ayre. And this will helpe him wth Gods helpe./

To make one have a Stomake to eate./
Take ye water of Sentorie and make Conserve of
Burberies with it or els take Conserve of Barberies
and put water of Sentory unto it, and lett it stand
iii dayes in the Conserve And then eate of it
fastinge Conserve of Wyldinge is good for
the same.

For ye Glutt in the Stomake.
Take Sentorye and seethe it in good stale Ale &
when it is sodden, and when it is sodden stampe
it in a Morter and seethe it agayne wth the same liquade
Then straine it, and unto two Spoonefulls of the Juyce
put iii Spoonefulls of Clarified honey. And propor=
tion your juyce and honey after this manner, And
then boyle it well togither agayne, and take therof a
little in the morneinge. /





60
ffor ryseinge vnder the Stomake./

Take Pepper, longe Pepper, Graynes, Saffron, Ginger
Anise seedes lycorise & Sugar, and seethe them all in
good stronge Ale, till it be thick. and Skum it often.
And give it the Sicke to drincke. Et Sanabitur

To destroye the heate in the Stomake/

Take good Ale and put therin Shivers of of browne
breade and lett them lye therin an howre. and boyle
it well and skim it. And drinke therof two or three
dayes and it will helpe.

ffor all evilles in the Stomake./

Take Ashe seedes, lynse seed & Cummin seedes of eyther
in lieke quantitie And give it to drincke wth hott worte
or Ale.

To avoyde fullnes of Stomake.

Take Burrage, Marygold langue de beofe of each a hand-
full, Wormewoode Cinquefol, Liver worte Sentorye, Hart
Tonge, the Rynd of fennell Rootes, & the rynde of Parsley
rootes of eache of these halfe a handfull, and seethe all this
in fayre water till halfe be sodden awaye and then put as
much Sugar as will make it pleasant to drincke. And
soe drincke therof everie daye after dinner & Supper
three or fowre Spoonefulles at a tyme.

To make a Plaster for the Stomake.

Take yarrowe Redd fennell Redd mynce Burrage, Rewe
and fether fewe, Clote leaves Peretorie Egremony of every
one in lieke quantitie, and put therto the grease of a Barrowe
hogg and for a woman the grease of a yolke hogg. And
seethe them altogither in good Ale and make a plaster
therof and laye it to the Stomake, as hott as may be suffered
three tymes.





ffor the Stomake in greate heate and
for the Ague and Pestilence

Take Violett leaves or the flowres, Endiffe, Borage Straw-
berrye leaves, Tormentill and Scabious Stampe of eache of
these herbes a handfull and straine it, Then take the Iuyce and
a pynte of white wyne & mylke and make therof a Possett and
wth a little Sugar give it the patient to drinke warme, but
none of the Curde. Lett him drinke a little and often. And if
you seethe the herbes in the possett Ale, it is the best waye, after
the Curde is taken awaye.

ffor him that Can not keepe his meate.

Take Endiffe water and Mynt, and seethe the Mynt till the Iuyce
be in the water, and straine it, and seethe it wth Sugar, and
then give it the Patiente

ffor the Liver, and to destroye wicked
humors in the Stomake.

Take Scabious seethe it in wyne & drinke it, and it is good for
the Liver & to destroye evill humors in the Stomake.
And drinke it fastinge and you shall never gave Imposteme
breedinge vppon you. And ther is noe man lieveinge t
knowethe all the vertues of Scabious:
It purgethee the brest. It puttethe awaye old Agues and ever
keepeth a man in lustie liekeinge

Sentorye is hott and moyste in iij. degrees. It is good for Synnews
It is good for the Stomake makeinge an Electuarie therof
wth Clarified Sugar & Relented well thicked, and eateth therof
and it will make one have a good appetite to his meate
Alsoe it is good for the dropsey and many other Sicknesses,
And it hathe more then an hundred vertues.

ffor the Liver groweinge to the side.

Take wormewoode, Cammamell, Hayhove, Persley, fennell
Anis, Comen, and the bark of an Ashe. and boyle, all these
togither in two pottes of water and wyne and drinke therof.



Take a good quantitie of liver worte and brase it a little and then
seethe it wth good stronge Ale, with a quantitie of Rubarbe and
drinke e quantitie of a Spoonefull everie morneinge.

Paine in the

ffor Shortnes of wynd and brest

ffor the ryseinge of the hart and
streightnes of the Brest./

Take the Iuyce of Redd Cole and lett it boyle well in Wellwater
and put awaye the fome and Skum it cleare, and when it is
well sodden put in a penny worthr of Saffron, Then take of
Honey and Sugar one li. and soe make an Electuarie and
vse therof Morneinge and Eveninge nyne dayes and you
shalbe whole Deo volenter/

Or els this

Take horhound, Isope, Marche ffennell Rewe, Sunsleke, Puliall
Clary and Nepp by even ptions, and put therto the powder
of Pepper & Honey & seethe all these in possett Ale or
well Water, and drinke a quantitie therof Eveninge &
Morninge. And this helpeth The brest, the Sydes, and the
Melt and is good for the Coughe.

A remedie for the Coughe, Tissick and fleame

Take Lycorishe x. penny weight Isopedryed viij. penny
weight, Anise seed vjd waight, Rootes of Elenacampana dryed
a C. penny weight, Sugar Candie xijd weight; Make all these
in powlder and take therof everie morneinge and to bedward
iijd. weight, If you take small Raysons and bruse them small
and put them in the water and lett it stand still awhile; and vse
therof as you have neede, And drinke that powlder wth that
water it is verie good.

ffor the Coughe and him t takethe Cold.

Make a tost of bread and laye the best Treacle of Ieane you can gett
on bothe sides of the tost, wth a little aqua vitae vppon it and when you
goe to bedd eate it, & drinke not after it that night, and eate an
other in the morneinge and fast two howres after





A Good Medicyne to avoyde fleame

Take parsley Root and fennell Rootes, Isope and Bittenie
and seethe them in good Ale wth Liquorishe and a quantitie
of Clarified honey and vse to drinke it and you shalbe
well God willinge.

An other good Medicyne for fleame.

Take a Pynte of Endiffe water & a halfepenny worth
of Gentium, and of Ginger as muche, and shredd
them small & put them into the water, and lett it
stand still awhile and vse therof as you have neede,
drinke it and it will purge you cleane.

ffor the Stiche

Take dryed Acornes or Coles of an Ashe Tree and drinke
the powlder fastinge in the morneinge a Spoonefull at
a draught. Or els take the Budd of an Ashe Tree, the
blacke beinge taken awaye, & take but the greene of
the Budd when they be tender, and make poulder
of them and put a Spoonefulll of powder to three
Spoonefull of drinke and drinke it warme. This
Medycyne never faylethe.

ffor him that hath greate Thirst.

Take the Root of Lovage and Stampe it and temper it
with water and give the Sick to drinke three night

ffor the drye Horsenes

Take a jli of figg dates a li of Reysonnes of the Sonne,
and an ounce of Licorishe. Alsoe take Red fennell root
and rootes of Parsley and Seethe all these togither in
a gallon of runninge water to a quart drinke it

A good poulder for Moystnes & for
the wynd Collick

Take Cumin, Graynes, Lycoris stickes and Sugar Candie and
in any wise soe that they be all verye pure & good & cleane.
Then beate them in a Morter of Brasse. put therto fennell
Seede, and beate them well togither agayne, and then searse
it throughe a fyne Searse; and eate of it. And it will keepe you moiste
& from the wind Collick.





62
ffor the Spleane if it be diseased

Take Heart Tonge Camamell two lyllye Root, Malllowes
flowres of Broome, braye them small & frye them wth
butter and laye therof a plaster to the side greeved.

To make one Vomitt.

Take the inward pill of Elder that lookethe greene and Stampe it
in a Morter. Then take the iuyce therof wth Treacle and warme
it till the Treacle be melted And drincke it warme and it will
cause you to vomit.

ffor the measelles or yellow Iuandies or
any other disease wthin the bodie.

Take a pynte of Malmesey and make a possett of it. then take
sixe Spoonefull of the possett Ale, as much of water Imperial
and a Spoonefull of Treacle of Ieane, and lett the patient drinke
it as warme as he may suffer it. and soe keepe him fastinge three
howres after. And this will bringe out the disease. But looke
you keepe him warme after the disease dothe appeare.

ffor the Yellowe Iaundies.

Still Selendyne & Yarrowe and drinke therof in the morninge &
in the Eveninge iij. or iiij. dayes, And it will soone waste awaye

An other for the Same. pved.

Take Ceuell Soape and scrape it and drincke it and it will helpe

An other for the Same

Take Endiffe dryed and thinner barke of a Barbarie Tree
beaten in a Morter, and a little Ivory Scraped. And make
powlder of all these and drinke therof Eveninge & Morninge.

An other for the Same: Probatum.

Take the greene Tares or the wylde Tare that groweth in some
hedges and Stampe it & straine out the iuyce and drinke therof
wth white wyne and a little Turmerick ij. or iij. morning
fastinge & it will helpe.





ffor the Blacke Iaundies

Take longe wormes & slitt them, and skowre them wth Salte
as you doe gutt then washe them verye cleane and Stampe
them and Straine them with Clarett wyne. And soe drinke them
in the morneinge fastinge.

An other for the Same. pved diverse tymes.

Tale Licorishe stickes one penny worthe and iij. ffennell Root
alsoe take vj. ffigg and two handfull of Endyffe and stampe
them in severall. Then lett them all be sodden in a quarte
of Ale vnto a pynte. And vse to drinke it blood warme.





63
A verie good Medycine for the Ague.

Take a halfe penny worthe of Aqua vitae a halfe penny worthe of
Runney a halfe penny worth of Treacle of Ieane, and a halfe penny
worthe of Graines. pound small yo graynes and put them
togither with the foresaid thing. and heate it as hott as you can
suffer to drinke it And lye downe vppon a bedd and be
warme covered, till you have surelie Sweatt. And vse this twise
or thrise. Et Sanabitur Probatum.

ffor the Same.

Take Croppes of Nettles, the Burr Roote and dandelion of eyther
of them a handfull. Bruse them and drinke them in three
pyntes of possett Ale sodden to the halfe.

ffor the Ague a principall Medycyne.

Take Sage, Baye leaves and Cammamell of eache two hand-
full and Stampe them straine them and take the iuyce and put
to it the third part of a Spoonefull of pepper, and put it all
in a little drincke And lett the Sicke drincke it when the fitt
comethe vppon him, and lett it abstaine three howres after.
Water of Cammamell is for the Shakeinge. drincke it. pved

ffor the Ague pved. hath holpen many

Take e youngeest of Baye leaves three handfull & of Sorrell half a handfull,
of Bitteny two handfull, of Sauge one handfull: And beate them in a
morter, and take the Iuyce and put to it e poulder of Pepper halfe a
Spoonefull. And stirr them together wth a Spoone a good while. And
before the fitt comethe give it to the Patient fastinge. and lett him
keepe him selfe fastinge iiij. howres after. And lett him take
this Medecyne wth a little stronge Ale luke warme

for an Ague
that comethe
of cold.   An other for the Ague that hath bene diverse tyme Proved

Take Baye leaves iiij. handfull of Ribworte, Sauge & Langedeboefe of eache
two handfulles of Planten & Strawberrie leaves of eache one handfull, and
beate all these in a Morter. then take the iuyce & put therto a little poulder
of Pepper, and a little Ale or Beare as the patient best liekethe, and
drinke therof as soone as the cold comethe. & let it be well stirred & make it
luke warme. And lett him keepe him selfe warme and fastinge iiij howres
after. nor he must not drinke iiij. howres after. This hath holpen many.





For a fevor./

Take Sauge, Baye leaves or a little Stonecroppe or Bitteny, Stampe
them in a Morter and Straine them & take the Iuyce and put
therin the poulder of Pepper. ANd put all this in Ale or Beare
as the Patient may drinle it at once, And let him drinke
before his Ague come. And it will helpe.

ffor the ffevor.

Take Ribworte, Cinquefol. & Langedebeofe and Stampe them
and Straine them. And take e Iuyce and drinke it Luke warme
before the Ague come. Probatum.

For the Gripeinge in the Belley.

Take five leaved grasse and boyle it well in runninge water
and a fewe flowres of Rosemarie if you can gett them, or of the
leaves or small stalk, and boyle them togither; And as hott as
the patient can endure it, laye it to the belley. Et Sanabitr

A present Remedie for Gripeinge in the Belley

Take halfe an ounce of Osmund and boyle it in fayre water with a
little honey, then Straine it and drinke it luke warme. et

ffor the wynd Collick in the Belley./

Take a pynte of Malmesey an halfe penny worthe of Saffron
and two Nuttmeg and braye them togither small in a
Morter, And take the wyne and put it in a Cupp and

And then put to yo poulder & drinke it Mylke warme
when the fervent Sicknes comethe vppon you And
vse this & it will helpe.

A Poultes for the stone.

ffor the Stone  Take a good handfull of Pellitorie of the wall of mallowes & Cammomyle
the lyke quantitie chopp them altogither and boyle them in three
pyntes of water the space of one howre. then take houshold
breade made of wheate grates, and soe thicken it to be a
Poultes, when it is thicke ynoughe, put into it a qrter
of a pynte of oyle of Cammomyll or of dyll or hearbe Grace,
for want of these take Sallet oyle; This beoinge done put it into a
white Cotton bagg, the Cotton syde beinge inward: Aplye it as hott
as may be endured. Then take possett ale made wth Parscelie seede, the drincke beinge
made of ale and white wyne wch is exceedinge good, lykewise possett ale made of parscelie & dill is
Verie good.





64
for the Stone A pure Medecyne.

Take greene Cherries and stampe them; and put them into
Renishe wyne and let them stand in steepe three dayes
then still them. take a draught therof and put therto a
Spoonefull of Sallet oyle and a little Sugar & drinck ther-
of first and last. Alsoe when you cannot gett Cherries
then take of Renishe wyne halfe a pynte and of fyne
Sallet oyle a Spoonefull sett it to the fyre and put a good
peece of Sugar to it and greate Nutmegg & let it boyle
Then drinke it warme first & last.

An other medecyne for the Stone

Take an herbe which is called Ramset in Maij or Iune when they be
seeded leafes and all, and drye them in the Shadowe and
not in the Sonne. and make therof poulder, and vse therof
every daye in thie pottage or in thie drincke. And this
will breake and destroye the Stone.

ffor the Stone.

Scrape two Spoonefull of white Castle Soape into a pynte
of Ale and heate them well in a pott by e fyre, till the Soape
be consumed into the Ale. And soe drinke it fastinge in e
morneinge as warme as you can Suffer it. Abstaine
three howres after from meate & drincke, walkinge &
stirringe about in the same Space. And lieke wise at night
three howres before you raste. And vse this Nyne dayes
togither more or lesse as you fynd it worke.

An other for the Stone.

Take the Red barke of the yeugh tree dryed beaten to poul-
der, and Ieate & beaten alsoe to verie syne powder and
very fyne Searsed and beinge medled drinke them wth wyne
or Ale blood warme. And vse five or Sixe tymes






An other good Medecyne for the Stone often pved.

Take Thyme, Cammamell, Ramses, Beane Coddes, paretorie
of the Wall & Saxafrege, laye them in white wyne
a night & still them, and drinke of the water & it will helpe.

Alsoe parietorie. Thyme and Parseley, Seethe them
tender and laye to the privities warme. Then Seethe
Thyme Parseley and Parietorie in white wyne
tender and straine them. and to it a Spoonefull of
Aqua vitae and let the patient drincke it. probatum.

Alsoe drinke the wyne that a Reddishe roote fynely
Sliced and fennell hath bene layd in all night.
All these are good for the Stone.

ffor to breake the Stone in the bladder.

Take Allexanders Lovache and water Cresses of
eyther lieke quantitie, and boyle in a gallon & a halfe
of fayre Runninge water, and put therto a quantitie
Sugar of fayre Clarified Sugar or Sugar not clarified,
or Lycorishe. And drinke it Eveninge & Morninge
and Soe contynue it twentie dayes And it will
breake it without fayle

That the Stone shall never wax hard
in the bladder after it is broken.

Take of Grummell Seed and Saxafrege of eache a .li.
halfe a li. of Coleander Seede, a quarter of a .li. of
Sorras white and Redd. And gryne all these small in
a Morter. And everie daye eate a Spoonefull therof
in tie pottage. Et fiat.

To make an oyle for the Stone to serve e whole yeare.

Take Parsley, Suthernwoode & Parietorie of the wall and
seethe them wth Swynes grease till it be greene, but burne
it not, and Straine it, and annoynte the patient wth it warme,
at the Navell & small of the belley.





65
ffor the Collick and the Stone.

Take a handfull of dandelion, Thyme, Avence, Parselley
Stichworte, Camamell, or Pelletorie of the wall of eyther
lieke quantities. Washe them fayre, Stampe and Seethe them
in a quarte of Ale to a pynte. Then straine the herbes &
sett the liquor over the fyre and put therto a pynte of
white wyne and poulder of Lycoris, Ginger, Cloves, Mace,
Anneseed, Long Pepper, Small Reysons and Saffron of
everie one a liekr=e quantitie a halfe penny worthe and three
penny worthe of Sugar. and lett them seethe alltogither
a little while, and Straine it and give the patient five
Spoonefull in the morninge and five in the Eveninge
blood warme ix. dayes first & last.

ffor to breake the Stone and to make one make water.

Take three handfull of psley, ij. of Thyme, iij. of Sentarye,
iiij. of Sampier two of Allexander leaves and one hand-
full of Cammamell And seethe them togither in fayre
water from a pynte vnto a quarte. then put to them
two Spoonefull of Aqua vitae: Then Straine the liquor
and give the Sicke to drincke. And after laye the same
herbes warme to the Small of the Belley in a Clothe
And it will cause the Stone to breake and voyde it.
Lett him drincke it fowre dayes togither. If he be
Sore grieved seethe it in white wyne, for it is better
then water.

A drinke for the Stone to breake it wthin the bladder
and to put it out, and to amke one pisse. Probatu.

Take Sampiere and Perietorie of the wall and still them togither
And take fowre Spoonefulles of the water and drinke it wth

piere then take Cammamell, ALexander and Parsley

pved.





ffor the Collicke and the Stone a very pure medecyne.

Take Parsley seede, Broome seede, Gromell seede
Allexander seede ffennell seede, Planten seede
or Coleander seede of eache of these a quarter of an ounce
Nuttmeg beaten very fyne halfe an ounce, Sugar
beaten verie fyne & searsed thoroughe a fyne Searse
three ounces, and put all these togither and lett them
be well mingled; Then take of white wyne eyghte
Spoonefull, and as much of the powlder as will fill halfe
a wallnutt Shell, and put it into the wyne and lett
the patient drinke it three tymes, and it will helpe.

And if the Patient be very sore, take an Oyntmt
called Altea and Oyle of Roses of eache of these an ounce,
and annoynte the place wher the sore is agaynst the
fyre as hott as may be Suffered And it will cause the
Stone to a voyde in short tyme.

A pretious Remedie for the Stone and to make
one to make water wthin halfe an howre, and
that will breake the Stone wthin Tenn dayes. pved.

Take Virga Aurea, and make it into fyne powder, and put ]a Spoonefull therof into a newe layde Egge softe rosted, and
give it to the patiente to drincke in Ale or in White wyne
in the morninge to his breakfast, And let him not eate
three or fowre howres after at the leaste. And then he shall
make water in lesse then halfe an howre. And yf you vse
this Contynuallye the Space of Tenn or Twelue dayes,
It will cause himto make water, and voyde out the stone
wthout any payne or griefe.

ffor himt is stopt of his water and for e stone Collick

Take Radishe Rootes Scraped and layd in white wyne a night. and
straine it and give it the patient to drinke and it will make
him make water. pved.

ffor the Collicke

Take Mustard seede, ffigg and vinegre, Stampe them altogither. Then

ffor the Collick and to make one pisse.

Take one handfull of Thyme and three handfull of Parsley and

Then strayne the said liwuor and give it to the Sicke. And after
