Cepe.

Cepe called oynion. Some saye t it is contagious to the sighte and to the
head. Gallen saithe it is not good for them t have sicknes in e gall. But
it profitethe muche to fflegmatique men Plinie saythe that it is good
for e blood wthin a man. it makethe good Couler It neshethe the wombe
And Stampe it with honey or vinegre or seethe it in wyne and it will
heale e swellinge of the hand and all Apostemes wthin three dayes.
Alsoe Stampe oynions wth Salt and hearbe Grace and it will heale the
bytinge of Dogg that are madd, & of Adders. Alsoe the Iuyce of oynions
and womans mylke helpethe the payne in the eares. The Iuyce dronke
wth water helpethe them that have lost their speache. And the Iuyce
put into the nostrill puttethe evill humours out of the head. Eate them
or drinke the Iuyce of them & it purgethe womens flowers. Take e
Iuyce of an oynion and mingle it wth Capons grease and annoynte the
feete t be Chaffed wth labor & it helpethe them. Alsoe the iuyce garguled
in ones mouthe aswagethe the Toothe ache. Seethe oynions in oyle and
eate them with bread & it helpethe to heale the sores in the mouthe. and
healethe the bloodie flix. and for the head that lackethe haire annoynte
it wth the iuyce of an oynion and it will growe. And drinke it &
it makethe a good apetite and ceasethe the stinkeinge in the mouthe.
and is good to annoynte sore eyes. Stampe oynions and laye them
to the Emerodes and it healethe them. It is hott in the third degree
and moist in the fowrthe.

Comin./

Comin hath a stronge savour. The leaves are lieke Coriander. It hath
many branches comeinge out e stalke. Take it in meates and make
pouder of e Seedes & drinke it in wyne or old Ale and it will put evill
wynd out of the Stomacke and the Bowelles and makethe good diges-
tion & good vryne and makethe one freshe of Couler. It Stoppethe
the Fluxe and lettethe Lecherie It is good to heale the bytinge of venemous
beast. Alsoe seethe a handfull of the pouder in good wyne from a
pottle to a quarte, and drinke therof as hott as you can suffer it
Eveninge and morninge first and last and this is a Soveraigne
medecyne for a stinkinge breathe. It is hott and drye.





Cicuta

Cicuta is an hearbe called Humbock. The Roote therof eaten in
steade of Persenips causethe one to loose his witts. Albeit
ther is a Soveraigne medicyne therfore viz. Drinke stronge
wyne after it & it will helpe it. If you stampe the leaves &
laye it in plaister to a sore head it makethe it whole. Alsoe
annoynte the eye liddes wth the iuyce therof and it helpethe
bleared eyes. and helpethe the Sicknes called in Latin
Herpera in Englishe holye fyre. Alsoe put the Iuyce to weomens
breast and it swagethe the swellinge of them, and dryeth vpp
mylke. Make a plaister of the Seede ground wth Silver
Spume and grease medled therwth and it is a medicyne
to put awaye the heate of lecherie and polution of kynd.
And make a plaister of this hearbe and laye it hott to the
Podagra and it helpethe it, and all vnkynd heates.
It is hott and drye in the third degree

Camamille

Camamile is an hearbe wherof ther are three kynd and all of them
have one propertie. Drinke it wth wyne it makethe good vryne
& breakethe the stone in the bladder. and purgethe the flowers
in a woamn in hir Matrice if she be washed therwith. Seethe it
and drinke it wth wyne and it abatethe the hurt and swelling in
the Stomake. The decoction therof healethe the Iaundise and the
diseas in a mans side called Hypocondriu It goethe awaye
wthout oyntment if it be dronke wth wyne Plinie sayethe
Take five penny weight of Camamell and xl penny weight
of white wyne and drinke it Contynuallie xl. dayes and it
clensethe the Splen & helpethe all evill blaynes in the white
macule, Sebel, pam.  of the Eye that are called _________ Alsoe make a plaister
therof and laye it to a wound and it clensethe it cleane.
And sodden in water and washe the head therwth that
akethe and it aswagethe the akeinge.







Calaminte
Calamynte is an hearbe of a stronge savour it bearethe three Spyces
the one is stonye ye other is hairie & the third is waterie and all these
as Hipocrates saithe destroye mans Tallent, ableit it is hott and
drye in the third degree. Drinke the Juyce therof wth hott mylke
and it will make all the bodye sweate. Seethe it in oyle and annoynte
all the bodye of him that hathe the fevor and it will helpe him. Alsoe
it healethe the Leprosie in the beginninge. Braye it and laye it to
the stinginge of an Adder and it will heale it. Drinke ye juyce wth
wyne and that healethe all Venome and sleaethe wormes in the bellye,
it healethe the kyneys and all evills in the sides. Stampe it and laye
it to blacke wounds and it will bringe them to their perfect couler agayne,
and doe awaye the blacknes. and doe muche the better if it be sodden in wyne
& laye to as a plaister. Alsoe drinke it wth wyne and it comfortethe the Sto=
make, & lettethe neesinge and stoppethe the appetite of Lecherie. Stampe
it and laye it in a howse and their shall noe Adders nor any Venomous
beaste have power to abyde wthin the howse. Alsoe who soe drinke the the juyce
of this hearbe and the juyce of Mugworte by the space of three dayes togi=
ther fastinge it will heale him of the yeallow Jaundise if it be curable
on warrantise probatum. /

Cenapium.
Cenapium is Mustard Seede. It hathe vertue to drawe out humors of glutt. The strength
is in ye seede. It Sharpenethe the witts and bringethe the wombe to good temper.
It makethe good uryne; It provokethe the flowres. Temper it wth aysell &
it expellethe evill fleame out of the bodye. It Comfortethe the sight. the Stomake.
the brest. wherof the Tyssick comethe, the Longs an the old akeinge of the raynes
of ye backe. It drawethe all evills out of the Bladder. It helpethe the Swellinge
about the harte, the Splene, the mawe, and helpethe all old evill wynds in the
bodye Alsoe they make Plaisters therof for Stinginge of Venomous wormes
and many other things ther are wherin Mustard Seede is used. Take Mustard
Seede and beate it in a Morter wth athird parte of Cromes of white breade,
drye Figgs and honey and Aysell and make a plaister and laye it to everie
disease afore named and it will helpe. Take ye Seede and the Roots and Stampe ym
wth newe Must or ever it be wyne, Strayne it, and that drinke is comfortable
for the Splene, the Stomake and the heade. Oyle made of the Seede is a
pretious Oyntment for ache & for the Synnewes. Alsoe the Seede stamped
wth Ale and barrowes grease is good for the vaynes in the neck. The
Leaves & ye Stalke burnt & the Ashes dronke is good for the fallinge sicknes. The
Seede stamped wth Figgs is good to eate for the Lethurgie, the Sleepinge disease
The Seede brayed wth barrows grease helpethe the Leaper, & puttethe awaye
Skabbs & Skarfes if they be annoynted therwth. It is hott & drye./





Centimodum.
Centimodum is an hearbe called Pusser Sparrowe Tonge,
Strill or Swypies Cresses. It is hott and drye. The juyce therof
dronke wth good Ale or white wyne breakethe the Stone in the bladder.

Columbina
Columbina is a Columbyne, It is hott in the third degree. Ther are two
kynds of them, one that bearethe white flowres and the other blewe, and
bothe are lieke but for the Color & have one vertue. The juyce dronke wth
water castethe out abortives. It helpethe the Coughe and Bruses.
Drinke it wth Aysell and it dryethe the Splene. Drinke it wth wyne and it
purgethe the Flowres. It helpethe the Dropsey in the beginninge. It healethe
greene wounds. The Juyce meddled wth a little honey is good to annoynte
sore Eyes. Alsoe stampe it wth oyle and annoynte the bodye therwth and
it puttethe awaye the coldnes and bringethe it into temperate heate./

Colorago
Colerago or Colorage is and hearbe called in Lattin Perfricaria in
Englishe Arse Smarte it is commune and growethe in wett lands.
Ther are two kynds therof.

Coriander.
Coriander is cold of nature. Galen saythe, Take Coriander and
drinke it and it kill the wormes in the bodye. Make a plaister of Coriander
Rosen Aysell & honey & laye it to the swellinge of a mans Codds & it will helpe
it. And make a Plaister of the juyce therof wth white bread and laye it
to any place that akethe & it helpethe. Take the pouder of the Seede
and give it him to drinke that hathe the fevor quotidian before the
sicknes come uppon him, and it will helpe. Soe many Seedes
as a woman eatethe at once soe many dayes hir flowers shall
stand.

Consolida Major.
Consolida Major is Comferie of some Consolie it bearethe greate leaves.
broad and longe. The vertue is. If a man be brused wthin the bodye
Take the roote therof & rost it well in hott Embers and lett
the Patient eate it wth honey when he is fastinge, and
it will heale him in short tyme. Alsoe the juyce dronke
healethe and knitethe broken bones. And if a veyne
be broken in ones nose Stampe Compfarie & give him
the juyce to drinke in stale ale & the blood will stanche.





Consolida minor.
Consolida minor is an hearbe called Daysie Broseworte or
Boneworte. The vertue therof is. Stampe it & laye it to a botche
and it will breake it. Stampe it & drinke the juyce and it will
knitt broken bones togither. and that drinke will asswage
akeinge & swellinge. And if one be brused wthin the bodye,
make pottage of this herbe and Comforie wth porke if it
be in the season therof and lett him use to eate the pottage
& it will make him hole. It is a kynd of Comfarie./

Costmarie.
Costmarie hathe leaves that be hott in operation whosoe eatethe
them it makethe him to have ill digestion of his meate. It maketh
good uryne. It Comfortethe the sight. It helpethe the Coughe.
Eate wth honey or stampe it, & it clensethe the bodye of Moles,
Spotts and Freckles. The roote therof sodden, stamped and
layde in plaister to a wound it will drawe out broken bones.
The seede therof sodden in wyne and dronke healethe the
menson Costmarie, Bytteny and Oxe gall, mixed togither
healethe the Morsewe & the blacknes of Strokes if it be
annoynted therwth. Whosoe usethe to eate therof in butter
or in Sawse it comfortethe him to ye worke of kynd./

Cresses.
Cresses is an hearbe wth these vertues Stampe the seede and
mixe it wth Barlie meale & it helpethe the Uncomes in ye face
that rise wth Quitter and abatethe the akinge. The Juyce therof
abatethe the Toothe ache if it be put on that side that akethe. And
that juyce fastnethe the haire of the head. The seede therof
dronke rastethe the dead Child out of the mothers wombe.
The smell therof makethe Adders to flye the place wher it is.
The juyce therof dronke with Aysell helpethe the Splene. Make
a plaister therof viz. the Seede barrow grease and Aysell
& it helpethe ye payne in the Thyes. Cresses and Goose grease
myngled togither will kill Lyce and Mytes in the head
The leaves stamped & sodden in Goates mylke supplethe
the brest & abatethe Coughe./





Daucus Asimus

Daucus Asimus is an hearbe called Dauke or Birdesnest it hath leaves
lieke Hemlocke, it bearethe a white flowre. It is good to heale a
dropsey, and the byteinge of all venomus wormes and beast
It vnbyndethe the stoppinge of the liver & Mylt & wombe
of it be stamped & dronke. It is hott and drye.

Dens Leonis.

Dens Leonis is an hearbe called Dan de Lion or Lyons toothe
Stampe it & drinke it wth Ale and it helpethe the Quotidian
fevor. drinke it before it comethe.

Ditanie

Ditanie is an hearbe t healethe the byteinge of all venemous
beast. make pouder of the seede and drinke it wth wyne. And
stampe the hearbe & frye it wth freshe grease and laye it to the
sore and it will drawe out all the venome, Cease the Ache, &
doe awaye the swellinge. And it is good for Morbo ladueo
to take the pouder of dittanie & of Castor and to let the
sicke drinke it wth the iuyce of hearbe Grace, & put some
therof in the Nostrill. Alsoe if one be diseased wth
cold about the harte seethe figg in wyne till they be
broken, and then clene them thoroughe a clothe, and
put therto two dragmes of pouder of Dittanie
& let the sicke drinke therof first in the morninge cold,
and last in the Eveninge warme. And if a wooman be
wth dead Childe, take the iuyce therof temper it wth
Wyne and put it in a Horne and doe it in hir Secret
And if yron or thorne be in ones bodie let him drinke
that drinke and it will soake it out. It is hott and drye.

Ditanie Minor

Dittanie minor is drye of nature. Stampe it wth oyle & Rosen
& annoynte a sicke mans head therwth and it will abate the head ache.
The smell therof avoydethe corrupt humors from the eyes.
A plaister therof made wth Aysell is good for the Stomake.
It purgethe fleame from the harte and the Lung. The foresaid
plaister breakethe an Aposteme and is good for the Raynes of
the backe. This hearbe hathe leaves lieke e greater Dittanie
but they be whiter & lesser and hath a sweete savour.





89
Dragancia Masculina

Dragantia Masculina is an hearbe called Serpentyne or
Bassilike. It is good for Stinginge of Adders,  also make
pouder therof and take two part therof & the third part
of vnsliked Lyme and mixe them togither wth vinegre, as
a plaister laye it to a Canker & it will heale it. Alsoe take
the pouder therof and temper it wth old wyne & let the sicke
drinke therof in winter and if it be in Somer take the greene
hearbe of Bassilike for it hath more verue then the pouder.
The pouder blown int ones nose dryethe the runninge of
it. This hearbe destroyethe the Coughe and helpethe the
festringe of wound. It growethe in Woodes & on hilles & hath a
stalke lieke an Adder. and the leaves are somewhat sharpe at
the ende. It is hott and moyst in the third degree. and it is
to be gathered in Iune and Iulie.

Dragancia faminia

Dragantia faeminia Is an hearbe that hathe leaves lieke
Ivie but they have white blott vppon them. It bearethe a yeallowe
flower and a stalke of two Cubitt in lengthe in manner of
a croked staff, lieke a Snake, and it bearethe seede aboue lieke
a Closter of grapes. and when the seede is rype it is yeallowe:
Make powder of the seede and meddle it wth oyle and put it in
his eare that cannot heare & it will helpe him. The iuyce
therof put into the nostrill wth a little wooll purgethe the head
& the nose. Alsoe stampe the roote therof. & the roote of a white
Vyne & temper them with honey and it is good to heale a Canker
in Wound. He that annoyntethe his hand wth the iuyce of the
toote of this hearbe he may take Adders wth his bare hand &
they cannot hurt him. Take e iuyce of the hearbe and annoynte
the eyes therwth and it putteth awaye the darknes of them
And if a man drinke the iuyce wth wyne it stirreth him to
the desire of a woman. This hearbe growethe in moyste
& drye places.





Herba Cruciata

Erba Cruciata is an hearbe called Crosworte. It bearethe small
leaves much lieke Cockle & a small stalke and hath white flowres
lieke a Crosse on one branche. And it is good to heale all wound.

Herba Iohannis

Erba Iohannis bearethe a yeallow flower and hath many stalk
runninge out of one stumpe. Stampe it and drinke it wth stale
Ale it is goof for all fevors. Alsoe keepe this hearbe in a
howse and noe evill thinge shall have power to come therin,
X or doe any hurte If it be gathered on Mydsommer Eve before
the sonne rise and sett it in fowre Corners of the house &
make a Crosse in Nomine patris &c

Herba feri.

Herbe Christofer Masculyne hath leaves lieke Crowsope but they
be whiter & bearethe a yeallow flower. It is good to drinke
for the plague pestelence & for the frensie. And take the roote
therof & of Pugell & Stampe them togither and drinke the
iuyce wth stale Ale by a good fyer and it healeth the stitche.

Herba feri faeminia

Herbe fer feminyne is an hearbe that hath leaves lieke
herbe Peter but they be not fullie soe white It groweth
by water & hath a longe stalke.

Herba Roberti

Herbe Robert hathe leaves lieke hearbe Bennett. The stalke therof
be redd with a little redd flowre, and hath a stronge Savour
lieke the Chafinge of a foxe. It is good to heale wound
The powder therof killethe the Canker. It growethe in
shaddoed hedges.





90
Herba Petri

Herbe Peter is called Cowslopp or Pugle. The vertue. The
Roote is good for the Stomake that swellethe. It causethe good digestion
It helpethe all sicknesses in the bodye. If it be dronke it maketh
good vryne. It clensethe weomens monthlye flowers. drinke
it wth wyne, and it healethe all venome or bytinge if it be brayed
& layed therto. The seede therof is good in Electuaries. But it
is ^not good for Sicklie folke to smell nor drinke. It is hott & drye in the
third degree.

Herba Gualteri

Hearbe Walter hath leaves lieke to Persley but they be smaller
Fayre and greene they seeme as bright as Silver bothe aboue
& vnder the leaves. It Springethe in februarie and seedethe in
June. The vertue is. It is a verie good Clenser & healer
of wound.

Erba Ive.

Hearbe Ive hath a roote groweinge even downe into the grownde, wth small
leaves by the ground. The vertue therof is. If a man hath a swelling
in his Eares take this hearbe and seethe it in ....... of  h
in wyne, oyle and Commin, and make a plaister therof & laye it
to the Eares & it will heale them. Alsoe the knobb of the roote dryed
and made into powder & dronken maketh one Laxative. Alsoe
take the knobb & the rootes and laye them asteepe in rose water
& sett it in the sonne till the rose water be consumed, and doe soe
twice or thrice and rubb yo face wth the powder therof, & washe it
wth water of roses & it will make yo visage puerlye fayre
& cleare. This powder dronke putethe awaye the Superfluitie
of fleshe. It is hott and drye.





Herba Benedieta

Herba Benedieta is an hearbe called Bennett or fetherfewe,
The vertue therof. It will keepe maydens Pappes from waxeinge
greate. The iuyce therof mixed with the Spume of Silver & Swynes
grease aswagethe the hott Podagra or Gowt & all hot evell.
Alsoe the water of this hearbe is a Soveraigne thinge to
annoynte sore Ioynt that ake if they be well annoynted
against a fyre. It Comfortethe the Stomake & aswagethe
the fevor Quotidian. It is good for the Crampe that comethe
of cold. Stampe it and laye it to the bytinge of venemous
beast. Temper it with Aysell for the morfewe. It ioyneth
broken bones beinge stamped and layed to. The roote
stoppethe the monthelye Course being dronke.
It is cold and drye.

Ebolus

Ebolus is an hearbe is an hearbe called Walworte. Ther be
two kyndes therof: the one white and the other the other blacke,
The white is more violent in operation from the blacke. Yf
a woman put it into her secrett it purgeth and clenseth
an Abortive, The poulder therof Snuffed into the nose
Dothe drive awaye the payne in the head. and medle the
poulder with the iuyce of Sellendyne and hearbe Grace and
it clensethe the sight. Put none of this poulder into pottoge
But this put in mylke will kill flyes that lye theron. The
water that this is sodden in helpethe those that be madde,
yf they drinke therof in the beginninge of their madnesse,
It helpethe the dropseye, the Melancholie and Curethe morbu
casucum, It clensethe the Leper, It ceasethe the gnaw-
inge in the stomacke. Ipocrates and Plynie will that
you give not aboue x. pennie weight to the man that you
will clense, nor in any wise to give it to old men children, nor
weomen with Child, but to sutch as are stronge of nature./
Alsoe let one seethe Walleworte in water with willowe leaves
Ache






91
Ache, Bittonie, verbine and Loveache and let one bathe
him therein and it helpethe the bruses and acketh of everie
part of his bodye. Stampe it and drincke it wth wyne
and it helpethe the palsey, and drawethe togither the
humors and expellethe blacke Choler and fleame,
It comfortethe the sight The roote stamped and layde in
playster dryethe the dropsey and the swellinge therof. And
the hearinge alsoe yf it lye to the eare two dayes. It
clensethe and it helpethe the toothache you must give
but five penny weight of the roote. It is hott and drye
in the third degree.

Etropium Helitropium
Etropium is an hearbe called Sponsa Solia or Cuory it groweth
in fast groundes and in meadowes and is a divyne hearbe of
the bodye of the sonne, and hathe Croked branches the flowre
hathe the Color of the sonne, and is cold in the second
degree, The iuyce of this hearbe healethe the bytinge
of venemous wormes and beastes, and is good for
the stoppinge of the mylt that comethe of Cold, and
for the opilations of the raynes The iuyce droncke
wth powder puttethe venome out of the bodye in short
tyme Bruse this hearbe and laye it to a mannes
Coddes and it will quenche the heate of Leacherie

Eupatorium

Eupatorium is an hearbe called wyld Sauge Saliva agrostis
It is hott in the first and drye in the second degree.





for a tetter

Take ashes of ashwood Blacke soape and

x A Medycyne to take awaye the heate in the
face.

x Take the fatt of a pullett that never layed egge, one
ounce, a qrter of an ounce of Camphire, a qrter of
of an ounce of Lythurge of gold, as much of siluer &
leade Lythurge, beate yo Camphire & yo Lythurge
& mixe it wth the fatt of the pullett and putt it
into a pott or glasse and whensoever they fynd any
heate or ytchinge in the face annoynte it therwth.
especyall at night.

To make a bagg to comfort & strengthen
the Stomacke.

Take a handfull of wormewoode as muche
of Minnt dryed redd rose leaves that have
not bene stild, or in the sommer freshe leaves
of the redd rose. Crummes of rye bread, a
Nutmegg. Cloves, a little saffron Shred yo
hearbes smale and beate your Spice & mingle m
altogither and put them in a pewter dishe
wth a little redd rose water & wyne vineger.
then put them into a lynnen bagg as bigg
as will cover the stomacke. quilt the bagg
and applye it warme & hange it about the
necke renuinge it every daye with rose water
& vineger Sprinkled vppon it.





A Medycyne to take awaye the shaking
of the Ague.

x A little before the fitt come take Yarrowe two little
handfull and put vnder eache arme pitt one.

An other for the Ague

x Take the iuyce of Yarrowe and Planten and make
some cleare posset wth Ale or Beere, then to a
draught of the possett ale put seven spoonefull
of the iuyce. then drinke it blood warme an howre
before the fitt come. probat. vse it till you see the
fitt diminishe.

An other for the Ague

x Take Planten, Yarrowe, hearbe Grace, baye salt,
Olubanum. of eache a little quantitie, mingle
it wth a little wyne vineger spread it vppon
Lynnen clothes byndinge it as streight as they
can indure it about the wrist a little before
the fitt, letttinge it lye till the next fitt
come & then renue it.

A nother for the Ague

x Take nyne rootes of Sheperd  purse and Scrape them
put them into a lawne Clothe and laye them one by one
that they come rounde about the necke sowinge it
fast as they can endure it and soe weare about the
necke till they be drye and then yf the ague
contynnue renue it.

x A medycyn for a Scalding or burninge

x f Howslieke, Marrigold leaves, redd Campanie of Ale hoof Planten of eache of these a good
handfull, and a li. of freshe hogg grease, stampe
the hearbes verye smale, and put them in to a possnett
and boyle them halfe an howre, then strayne
them and keepe the oyntmt in a pott and yf
the burninge ^or scalding be in the face melt some of the oyntmt
To keep e face
from a skarr  & with a feather ^annoynt e face and lett it not be drye, night or
daye. yf it be vppon any part of the bodye or
handes then laye it vppon a primrose leafe
or Campaine.

x A Poultise to aswage swellinge or rednesse.
x Take a Pynte of newe mylke and soe on the fyre
take Mallowes & Chickweede and redd Sage
yf the swellinge come of heate
leave out e wormewood &
fetherwoode & wormewoode and Sothernwoode, violettleaves
of eache of these a little handfull, a handfull
of dryed redd rose leaves and take these herbes
and mince them smale wth a knife and put
m in to the milke and lett them boyle a while
then take a penny loafe of the fynest bread put
& grate it verye smale and put it to the herbes
& milke & lett it boyle till it be verye thicke
when it is a most boyled take a good peece of
deere suett the quantitie of a greate walnutt
Then take it of the fire & put it into a dishe and
spread it vppon cloathe and applie it to the swelling
as hott as they are able to endure it and soe dresse
it twice a daye and it will eyther swage it or
breake it. Probatum.

An excellent Medycyne for a Cold and to make
one recover his fleshe that is growen Leane.

Doct Mathias Take soe much Coltsfoote as will make a quart of
iuyce beinge stamped and strained, then sett it on
the fyre in a pipkin; let it boyle, then clarified wth
the whites of two Egg, beinge well clarified, let
it boyle with a good quantitie of raysons of the sonne,
aniseedes and liquoris brused a good quantitie of eache
and halfe a pynte of Englishe honey, All boyled till
it be clere, of this take two Spoonefull in the mornige
two Spoonefull at fowre a clocke in the afternoone
and two at night when you goe to bedd, Contynue
the vse of this and you shall fynd greate good in it.

An Excellent medycyne to staye a fluxe.

D Mathias  x Take halfe a handfull of Balme to a pynte of
milke and boyle it well in the milke and then
make a possett, take of the hearbes and the Cird
and then sweeten it wth Sugar, and drinle of it evening
and Morninge

Doct Mat  An Excellent Medycyne to helpe coole a greate
heate in the backe.

Take Coltsfoote and Mylke and still them togither, and drinke of
the water with sugar and rose water.

Doct Ma Alsoe to Coole e backe

Bathe yo backe wth the white of an Egg vinegre and rose water
beaten togither.
Alsoe yf neede soe require annoynte the backe wth vnguentu Rosatu
& infrigidans Gallem.





95
To pcure Sleepe and settle the brayne.

D Ma Take a good handfull of Cowslipd picked the flowers,
boyle them in a pynte of mylke, strayne out the flowres
wth this mylke and ale mke a possett, drincke of this
often wth Syrope of Cowslipp flowres the oftener &
more the better and this will pcure sleepe, and staye the
brayne that is distempered.

An Excellent Cordiall.

Take two sheepes hartes of wethers cleane washed,
putt them smale cutt in a pewter pott, and put to the a
D Ma pynte if redd roswater, a pynte of Muscadell, a branche
of rosmarie, a stick of Cynamome, strewe these in a
pott of boylinge water for iiijor howres, straine owt the
brothe, give of this wth powder of Manus Christi.
made wth pearle, the oftener the better. Season it wth
oiyce of Lemons.

A Playster for to kill wormes in a Childes bellye.

Take Aloes and Myrrha and beate them to powlder
D Mathias verie smale then tske a peece of thinn leathet and laye it
Muskedyne and make a hole in the Leather for the
Navill. then strewe the poulder vppon the leather and
with a hott knife make it to cleave and when the vertue
of that is out make an other.

A remedye agaynst the ffallinge Sicknes  .....
96

Take live ffrogges by rhe hynder legges and strieke them vppon
the heade and amaze them, and before they be dead slitt
the back and take out the liver and the mawe and wype
them verye Cleane and drye. Then take a wyld Cole-
worte leafe wrappe them one by one in pleat in the leafes
and put them in an earthen pott that hath never bene
vsed, and close vpp the lid very Close that no ayre may
goe out and bake them in an oven with braed, then when
they are very drye beate them to verye fyne powder
and searse it. Then take an ounce of a deadmans scull
that dyes with a sound Harte, and an ounce of the poulder
of Pyonye rootes. To everye ounce of Pyonye rootes &
the dead mans scull you take two ounces of the
frogges and give them to the patient at one tyme as much
as will lye vppon a frenche Crowne, assoone as he feeles
the disease cominge vppon him. p probatu

To clense the Stomacke of ffleame.

x Take a quarte of runninge water, put into it two ounces of ffrenche Barley
two Stickes of Licoris cut in thinne slyces, one ounce of Anniseedes
a quarter of a pound of reasons of the Sonne, stoned let these
boyle to a Pynte, then straine the Liquor out, and put into it an
ounce of sugar Candye. Then everye Morninge heate iij or iiijor
Sponefulles and drinke it.

ffor one that is troubled wth ffleame.

x Take a quarte of Ysope water and a quarte of Coltfoote water put with it licoris
anniseedes and reasons of the sonne stond, of each a lieke quantitie let them
boyle almost halfe awaye, then straine it, and set the liquor over a soft
fyre and put some sugar therin, and anon it will be a syrope, then vse to
eate a little wth a licorise sticke when yow are troubled wth ffleame.

A Drinke to Comfort the Stomacke

x Take a quarte of Aquacomposita, put into it fowre penny worthe of
Cynnampme slyced thinn, two penny worthe of Dates slyced, a quarter
of a pound of reasons of the sonne ston'd, three flakes of mace
one Nutmegg slyced a quarter of a pound of sugar. When all these
have stood a fortnight, then cleare out the drinke into a bottle and
fill vpp the forenamed thing with Aquacomposita for they must not
stand longe drye, nowe when yow feele yo stomack ill, drinke a little
now and then.

An approved Medycyne agaynst the newe burninge sicknes

Take halfe a pound of frenche Barley, boyle it in two waters a
quart at a tyme, then change it into three quartes and put in halfe a
pound of reasons ston'd, one penny worthe of Anniseedes, one penny
worth of licoris, boyle all these togither from three quartes to
three pyntes, then clense it and drincke of it often in yo burninge fitt

Doctur Willowbyes water very good for all manner
of diseases.

Take Gallingale, Cloves, Quibebes, Ginger, Melelott, Cardimom
Mace, Nuttmeggs of each of these two drams, and of the iuyce
of Salyndyne a pynte, mingle all those made into poulder
wth the iuyce, and put therto a quarte of the best Aqua vitae
and three quartes of white wyne, Then distill all togither
in a glasse still, Yow myst drincke therof in Sommer everye
weeke one spoonefull, and in winter two.

An excellent medycyne for the Coughe of the Lunges.

Take ffennell seedes, Anniseedes, Helicampane rootes of eache
two ounces, Hysope and Horhownde dryed of eache one ounce,
of Ginger an ounce of Liquoris two ounces; Beate all
these into a fyne poulder and searce them. Then take a pynte
of fyne honey and Clarifie it verye well, and put therto halfe
a pound of Sweete butter, then putt all these spices by little & little
into the butter and honey and then put to it of the Syrropes of
Hysope, horhownde and of Helicampane of eache of them two
ounces, And then keepe it all well stirred till it be colde. And
eate the quantitie of a Walnutt at any tyme when yow are troubld
wth the Coughe but Chiefelye first and last morninge and evening

A Cordiall brothe.

Take mother worte, Liver Worte, and longe worte, of Egrymony
Hartes tonge, ffennell Rootes Parsley rootes Marche Mallow
Rootes of eache a hand full, of Succorye liekewise a hand full
Reasons of the sonne on li. stoned, Currence one pound, Sixe
*a manchet dates, a *loafe of bread cutt in halfe, a legge of veale, two
Gallons of water, Mayden heire a smale quantitie boyle
all these wth a softe fyre till halfe be consumed. then take
yo veale and stampe it in a Morter, and put it in agayne
into the liquor & lett it boyle a little while, Then take it of
& strayne it. yow keepe it and drincke it morninge &
eveninge






ffor an Humor in the Eyes

Take Tuttye of Alexander one ounce beate it

wyne, put therto one ounce of Redd Rose leaves

It helpes all humors in the eyes.

ffor to staye a Reughme

Take of Rubarbe the wayte of vjf. oyle of Amyseedes
ijd wayte of lycorishe three ounces, Pennidice
three ounces, orras three ounces, Gumdragon three ounces
fyne sugar halfe a pounde, put the Gum verye fyne

A water for the Eyes to recover sight
and to clarifie it

Take three dragmes of Tuttye made into fyne poulder
and as much Aloe Epaticum in poulder, two drames of
fyne sugar, sixe ounces of Rose water, sixe ounces of
good white wyne mixe all these together, and putt
them into a vessell of glasse, and beinge close stopped
sett it in the sonne for the space of a Monethe togither,
often stirringe and mixinge all the sd thinge togither
at the least once in a day, for the space of a monethe
that they incorporate well togither. Then vse it for
the mendinge of yo sight, by instillinge certayne drops
into yo eyes morninge and eveninge. Probatu - Dr Haima

A medycyne for one that is broken.

Take two heades of the fattest sheepe yow cann gett, dresse the
and seethe them thoroughely as if they should be eaten.
Strayne the brothe and take 2. handfull of Mallowes two
handfull of Smalage and put them into the brothe, then
seethe them vntill the hearbes be consumed to peeces,
Then take the hearbes out ofnthe brothe and laye them
to the sore, wth the fatt of the brothe as hott as the patient
can suffer it, vse this 3. or 4 tymes. Dr Haiman.

An other for the same

Annoynte the sore with oyle of Eggs.

An Excellent powder to pserue and restore sight
uerye good against all stoppinge of the spleene

x Take wywbright when it is most flowred & clippe of the leaues
and flowers of the topps of the stalkes, dry them ether in the
sune, or by the fire tyll they will goe to powder, then beat them
in a morter vntyll it be smale, then take to euery ounce of that
powder, one ounce of sweete fennell seede bejing put into powder
then take a pritye quantitye of mace, or nutmegges to mixe wth it,
and when you take the powder, put to it as much fine sugar
as the quantitye of powder you take.

A speciall remedy for a cough consumption
& for seuerall other diseases

+ Take 2 spoonfulls of e sirrop of domaske roses, 2 spoonfulls of e

A Medicine for keeping e small pox from
pitting and to take away e rednes
of the face.

Take a pint of white wine & a qr of a pound of figs,
slice e figs very small boyle them in white wine, till
halfe bee consumed. And when the small pocks begin to
dye, bath the face very well with this liquor warme;
then take the leafe of a hog, & put it on a Spitt &
roast it & let it drop into red rosewater, & beat
the rose water, & that together, & anoynt e face
aft it is bathed with this. You must use this often, till
they shall bee of and aftward, to take away e rednes.
And after to take away the rednes
Anoynt e eyes round about wth saffron & milke to keep the
pockes out of the eyes.
Eat conserue of red roses to keep them out of e throat.

Dr Burges's medicine for e plague.

Take 3 pintes of Muskadine, & boyle therein a handfull
of Sage, & a handfull of rue, until a pint be wasted,
then strayne it, & set it over e fire againe, then put
thereunto a pennyworth of long pepper, halfe an ounce of ginger,
& a qr of an ounc^e of nutmegs all beaten together, let it then boyle a little,
then put thereunto halfe an ounce of Mithridate, & halfe
an ounce of treacle, a quartr of a pint of hot a.....d an-
gelica water. Keepe this as the life above all treasure.
take it alwayes warme, both euening, & morning a Spoonfull
or 2, if you beee already diseased, then sweat therepon:
if not, once a day a spoonfull is sufficient in the
plague time. Trust this for a certaine ^vnder God there is
neither man, woman, or child by this deceiued, if the head
be not possessed wth the disease. This is likewise preua-
lent against e small pox, ague or feauer

A Receipt of the blacke Salve.

An approved receipt for the Stone
either in the Bladder or Kidneyes
to bee made in May

Sr George Hastings
gave 100li for
this receipt.

Take Saxifrage, parsly, Time, winter savory, Iermander, Lavender, ffennle, these being
shred small, put them into a mortar, & slice 2 or 3 nutmegs: put of each of
those herbes 2 good handfulls into 3 gallons of ale; put the hearbes & ale
into a Limbecke: then take Venice turpentine (Searce to the quantity of a
walnut & beat it with two Sponfulls of English hony, till you have quite
broken e body of the Turpentine; then let yo hearbes & ale begin to
boyle, & at the first beginning to boyle put in the Turpentine &
Hony mixt together, continually stirring it till all be well incorporated
which will quickly bee; then close up your Limbecke & sett under yo
Receiver. Keepe the first quart by itselfe. which is the strongest, then
draw 3 quarts more in severall glasses. The last is much e weakest.

The Vse of it.

Take halfe a pint of white wine & put in 2 or 3 sponnefulls of e water or
more, if it offend not yo stomacke, & soe drinke it being sweetned
with sugar for yo tast, & use some moderate exercise after it
if you can.

For want of wine you may drinke it in beere; or in any water distilled
of any of the hearbes aforesayde.

To Cure the Spleene, Obstructions, heat
in the backe & reynes, gravel, stone,
melancholy & hemrods.

Take fifty pints of running waterof ffrench measure, that is, neere fourescore
English; put it into a stone pott, & add thereto halfe an ounce of Roman
vitriol, & as much sal prunella. Let them dissolve 3 dayes in e water,
& then with a pipe such as Vintners use to racke their wine with
draw out the water quietly from between the scumme & the bottoe,
soe as the grounds may remayne below. Let the body be a little
prepared with physick before you take it. Then take of e water
Sixe pints in a mornin for fourteene or 15 dayes together. You may
Sweeten it if you please with Aceta Monatan, or some other Syrrop
that hinders not the operation. You must drinke e Sixe pints in halfe or
3 qrs

It is better effect (the proportion of the Mineralls being as aforsyd
equally mixed) then the waters usually drunke either at the Spaur
or elsewhere receiving from thence either too much or too little
of either.

A singular Medicine against e stone
either in the bladder or reynes as
hath binne often pvd to the great
comfort of those that have binne
grievously tormented with that Malady.

Take nerlor grosly pounded, & drinke them in white wine. It expells
gravel & pvokes urine. The leaves of any kind of nettles, or
the seeds worke e like effect; but not wth that good speed & so
assurefly as the Roman nettle.

An infallible Cure of an Ague.

Make mustard wth vineg & put 2 sponfulls of it into a pint of milke
ready to boyle. And it will turne it to a posset. Then
drinke a good draught of it. This will breake the fitt;
& taken e next time drive it quite away.





A notable expimt of Iohn
Bennet a surgeon at Maidston
in Kent for an Ague

See Gerrards Herbal

I. Bennt being desired to cure a bu.... boy of an ague which grievously
tormented him promised to give him a medicine & for the
psent for a shift as hee confired to Gerard tooke out
of his garden 3 or 4 leaves of the plant of rubarb, which
he stampd & straind wth a draught of Ale: & gave him to
drinke. It wrought extreamly upward & downward wth
him wthin an houre. In the end his strength overcame the
force of the physicke, & the boy lost his ague. But a
care may be had of the quantity, the former quantity beinge
too much.

lay it upo the pulse of the left wrist an hour bifore the
fitt, & the like upon the pitt of the stomacke, This
will infallibly cure an Ague.

For the Toothach.

mush as will cover a plaist of the bignes of halfe a crowne: Spread it upo a piece
of leath and apply it to the hollow of the necke.

An exellent Ale for the Scuruye

Senna Alexandriae .6. ounces, Polyrodium .4. ounces, bay berryes .3. ounces, Ashen-key
kernells, 4. ounces, sweet fennell seeds 2. ounces, roots of Saxifrage 2 ounces, Rubarb
one ounce, scurvie grasse a peck, beate all except the scurvie grasses and the
senna, the scurvie grasse you must stamp and straine out the Iuce and e Senna
you must rub to peices between your hands, stamp the rest and put the Senna to
them and put them in a course linnen^bag cloath  with a flint stone, put these into
3 gallons of stronge ale

this ale must be used in the spring from mid-march to mid may three weekes togh
together & in Autume from e beginning of September to the 15. october .15.
or 20. dayes, drinke euery morning halfe a pinte 2 houre after take a messe of
broath euery night 6 ounces if you use exercise after it it will worke the better
if it giue 6. stooles in a day and a night it will tis well if fewer not so good if
it giue more take lesse





To Make a good plum Cake

Take 2 li of e finest flower well dryed over coales sturing it so that it
do not lumper then beat 2 nug nutmegs a little cloues & mace most mace
& strow into your flower with a little salt and a qt of a pound of loaf
suger beaten & searced then worke in 2li of pure butter with your fingers so
small with your flower that it may be all broke then beat 4 yolks of eggs & butt 2 whits
and a full porrenger of pure ale yest together and strain into it then
strew in 2li of currants purely washed and picked and worke it little and
sett it in a boule couered to rise while e ouen is prepareing then put
in half a pint of pure thick creame scalding hott ^and 2 spoonfuls of rose water and sett it to rise by e
fire then pour it into e hoope with white paper and browne tyed about
e bottome well buttered and e sides of e hoope that you slip it out ise it
before you take it out halfe an hour will bake it

To make a Carraway Cake

take a li of flower a pound of butter a pound of carraway comfitts a qr
of a pint of rose water a qr of an ounce of mace one great nutmeg some 6 cloues
a little ginger salt beat and sift your spices into your flower sturring it well then beat 4 eggs
but 2 whites and a porringer or less of ale yest and straine it into the flower then melt a pound
of pure butter and dreane it from e dregs and pour it in and worke it into a little paste like a light
pudding, sett it bifore e fire to rise a quarter of an houre then pour it to a hoope with a white paper and
a browne tyed about e sides and bake it in a quicke ouen well cooled on e top with a wett broom
and lett it stand in halfe an hour y then ise it if you please

To her DP

The Sumeres sunn is is set
and it will rise no more
because it was his happles lucke
to marrie wth a whore

The Sumeres sunn is set
and winter draweth onn
Now little Roben Chearpes no more
alas his voice is gone

A Page a Knight a vicounte & an Earle
ffrom Scotland Came and married with an ... Girle
such foure to found weare neu seeene before
A wife a witch a murtherer & a whore

At Katherne Docke there launched a Burke
wch soare did leake but did not sinke
sumtymes she lay at Essex shore
Expecting Rigging yard good store

But sume dusasters to prevent
with wind in poops she saild to Kent
At Rochester she Anchor cast
wch Canterbury did distast
But winche: wth Elies helpe
did hold to .... this Lyones whelpe
he was weakesides and did neele
on Somm: to mend her keele

to ballance her and mend her sorte
and make her fit for any porte

A Medicyne for the Stone Colicke.

Take egge shelles a greate quantitie and a greate quantitie
of Gromell seedes and beate them to a smale poulder
and beinge beaten to a verye smale poulder put it into
a glasse and drinke of this powlder in white wyne
in the morninge fastinge for sixe weeke space for
a woman, and a man three monethes and this will
breake the stones.

ffor the Collicke

X A pynte of stilld parslye water, greate store of ginger
beaten, and a good quantitie of hard Sugar.

ffor runinge of the Raynes.

Take a pottle of Muskedyne greate store of honey
suckles, washe them and shred them smale shaking
out the water, and boyle them in the Muskedyne
till they become soe thicke that a Spoone may stand
vprighte. and soe eate it in the morninge in his bed
and sweate yf he Can.

ffor the whites.

The redd is + Take the blossomes of Archangell a peck everye
good for a morninge for three morning togither fryed wth two
man & e  or three eggs fryed wth sweete butter lieke a tanseye
white for a  and soe eate it vpp all with sugar yf yow please.
woman.

ffor a stiche.

+ Take a good quantitie of Acornes, a good quantitie of
Annisseed and fennell seedes a great quantitie
and drinke it in white wyne verye thicke wth the
powder bloudd warme overnight in bed and sweate
after it yf they can.

ffor the Ague.
x

Take two penny worthe of Garlicke and stampe it
and drincke it wth white wyne after it hath bene strayned
into the wyne. it will helpe an Ague that hath long
continnued in twice takinge.







ffor a Sore mouthe or Throate

x Take a good quantitie of Alume and stampe it to poulder
then take a good quantitie of redd rose water and a good
quantitie of Englishe honey and mingle these togither
over nighte and then in the mornine a good quantie
of these soe mixed and hold it a good wyle in yo mouthe
and afterward Swalloe it downe.

ffor deafnes.

x Take honey and put of it twice a daye to witt morninge &
x and eveninge and put of it into bothe the eares and lett
it runn into yo eares & head. then take a clove of garlicke
and pil of the Skin and pricke it and wrape in the honey
and laye it in yo eare but in nowe sort let it goe into
yo head. twice a daye doe this viz moringe and eveninge

ffor a Coughe that is newe.

Take a pynte of Aquavitae and a li. of Sugare
and burne the aquavitae with the Sugar till it come
to a Syrrope & then eate therof.

ffor the Mother that risethe vpward

x Take a pottle of Muskedyne and a li. of the best blewe
Currence and seethe it from a pottle to a quarte, and
take a spoone and eate it morninge or when the mother
comethe but before yow take this take iiijor pennyworthe
of Ieane Trekle, and as much Dragon water and
lett this serve foure morninge.

ffor the Mother downeward.

x Take balme water or a handfull of balme boyled in white wyne
and drinke it.

To clear the skin & take away freckles.

Take 3 pints of white wine vinegar, rosemary flowers, elder flowers, bean flowers of
each a good handfull, pumpecen seeds a good quantity, a qr of an ounce of
Camphire, bitter almonds a handful, an ounce of flower of brimstone,
the weighte of ten pence in Mercury: putt all these together into an earthen
pan, & lay 3 new laid eggs in it, till the shells be soft: then take them out
& pricke them, & put them in again. Adde a white lilly root beaten, &
straind ^in with some of the liquor.

An approved good water cald e Snayle wat
good for the Iaundise, collicke in e stomake
the consumption, & the passions of e heart,

Take a pecke of harden Snayles & wash them in beare, until they leave frothinge,
then put them in a great earthen pott, or Crusible, & cover it with a tile;
make a Soft charcoale fire about it, & lay a few coales upon the tile-stone,
tile stone,
then them let them stew till they leave hissing, or making a noyse: take
a quart of earth wormes, slitt them & scoure them with water & salt
untill they bee very cleane, then beat them wth yo Snayles in a Stone
moratr to peeces, then take a pott t is fitt for your still or limbecke, &
put therin 2 handfulls of Angelica, 2 handfulls of Sallendine, then
lay therein yo beaten wormes & Snailes, upon which lay 2 quarts of
rosemary flows, a sprig of AEgrimony, of red docke root, of the inner
barke of barbery trees, of wood Sorrell, of betony of eacg of them
a handfull of rew halfe a handfull, of Fennigricke, & of turmericke
of each an ounce, halfe a handfull of beare foot, of saffron beaten
well to poud e wayt of 6d: then poure 3 gallons of e strongest
ale you can gett, & Soe cover yo pott, & lett it stand all night in the
place where you are to Still it the next day, in the morning uncov yo
pott, & put thereunto 3 ounces of e powder of harts horne, 3 ounces
of Ivory, then take it off stirring it, but Sett on yo limbecke, & make
it fast wth rye dough & put und yo fire, & Soe receive yo water;
the first is e strongest, & therefore must be kept by it Selfe.





A Dyet deinke
for
Mrs Harris.

Take water cresses, brooklime & AEgremony of each
one handfull, Scurvigrasse 2 handfulls, boyle these
in a gallon of spring water, until halfe bee consumed,
then strayne it & put to the strained liqud Senna 2 ounces
polypody of the oake thre ounces, Rubarb one dram,
Anniseeds, ffennil seed, & Caroway seeds bruized of
each two drams; Let these stand all night on hott
embers, In the morning boyle them gently until
a pint be consumed, then stampe it & put a pound
of sugar to it & boyle it sixe or seven walmes.

Take of this drinke first & last a quarter of a
pint at a time, increase or diminish the
quantity as you will have it worke.

A Sear Cloth for the Backe or a strayne
in any other parts.

Take a pint of e best oyle olive & put into it a pound of red lead, &
boyle them until it be blacke, then let it stand, still till it be almost cold,
then put in halfe a pintound of rozen, & 2 pennyworth of Soft red wax, & boyle
them altogeth over a soft fire, until it be well mingled: stir it very well
all e while it boyles, then take some oyle, & oyle a board well, & make
it up in roules thereupon.

How to make Pills of Amber wch can preserve e stomacke from
all diseases & suffereth nothing to putrify therein because it expells
all humidity & uncleanes fro e stomacke & cleanseth e same:
They wch use it often get never more any payne in e stomack:
they cause mirth, they strengthen e head & stomacke, they cleanse
e reynes & the wombs esceedingly, wch are made as follow

Take e white or other Amber & Masticke, of each one quarter of an
ounce, aloes five drachms, agaricke one drachme and a halfe,
right Aristotochis halfe a drachme: this being beaten Smal &
tempered with the sirrup of ^Roman Wormewood make therof a
mass of pill, & forme seven pills of one drachme, take alwayes
2 or 3 of them, when you goe to bed.

You were best take halfe these quantityes & so make halfe the
masse at once.

An especiall medicine for a Consumption

Take new milke & white wine of each a pottle, put into it red sage, balme liverwort, &
Speeremint of each a good handfull, of raysons of the Sun stoned 2 pound, of figgs
one pound, of aniseeds an ounce & of licoras halfe an ounce both bruized: among
al these put a pottle of garden snailes first throughly purified clensed & taken out
of their Shells, then putt all these into a Still & Still them with a moderate fire
till they be all throughly Stilled, as long as the Still will goe, which will be about
24 houres, by no means letting the fire goe out, till the Still cease going. Into
the glasse into which the water runneth at first setting it under the Still
put halfe a pound of white Sugar Candy or very fine loafe Sugar of the finest
Sort & So let it Stand to receive the water that comes from the Still.

Before the Snailes be put into the Still, whereof there must be wth their Shells 3 pecks at the
least put them into a boule & Sprinckle them well wth Salt wch will cause them to purge;
whe they be well purged wash them in 2 or 3 Several waters to fetch of the Slime
cleare from them, when they be So done then cracke their Shells, & take them out of their
Shells, & Soe if there be by measure a pottle of them : if there be So many then put
them into the Still with the white wine milke & other things & So Still them altogether
Of this Stilled water let the patient take every morning fasting 8 Spoonfulls & So
much at night going to bed, or in the day time if he find himselfe faint & ill

Let him refraine from old cheese, Stale beere, Salt meat, drinke his drinke very warme:
Calves feet & all poultry are very good, & So be crayfish: boild Snayles in milke
are very good: to keep licoras in his mouth at all times will helpe his coughing
& at night ^eat Some Conserves of red Roses, & be Sure he keep himselfe warme
especially his necke & feet & through gods blessing next under god this will
cure him.





For the Piles

the quantity of a walnut
does it

Make a posset of Allum, that is turne the posset with a piece of Allum
putt into it, then dip two pecies of scarlet bages in them one after another,
& wrong them well; & then apply them to the piles some ten times
together as hott as you can well endure them, till they be coole
and then apply another. This is said to be a present remedy, & that
the next morning they will be gone.

Another
Take Orpine alias Pilewort, & mutton sewet; shred them small, & boyle
them together, till it lookes greene: then straine it, & anoint the
piles with it 3 or 4 times a day.

for a laske take
Take three a pills of Lint, made of holland, and whett them in beare or
sirope or anithing they can best Swallowe it, in this
is an approved medicine;

To Stope the Bleeding of a veine yt is Broken
or for any Inward bleeding
Take in the month of March the Spawne of frogges & put it into a
tub & dia a peice of new linnen cloth therein, untill it be shrow
wett with it then drie it & dip it againe doe this Nine times.
take small peices of this cloth & rowle it up like a pill & swallow it
& it will stopp the bleeding.

To make Jumballs uery good with almonds or without

Take a pound of very fine flower dryed halfe a pound of loafe suger beaten fine halfe spoonful a of carraway
seeds mingle them well together with e flower then haue in readyness e yolkes of 5 eggs newly
laid & one white well beaten ^for any houre together  .2. ounces ^of pure butter a qr of a pint of thick creame bare measure
2 Spoonfulls of rose water put e creame eggs rose water & butter into a pott & put that pot into
a skillet of boyling water and stir it well till all the butter be melted & a litte thickned then take it out & continue
stirring it till it be quite cold then mingle them all wth your flower into a very lithe paste then role
them into small roles wth as little flower to keep them from sticking as you can & make them up in
like e figure of 8 or in what fashion you please upon a pye plate finly buttered wth butter in a
col Cloth you may stir in a qr of a pound of blanched almonds beaten with a litte rose water
if you please to make them e better before you make then up upon the plate take some double
refined sugar mingled thick with rose water & ice them all ouer with a feather & sett them
quick in an ouen well heat t they may rise quick & when e edges are coulered take them out and
loosen them and if they be soft at e bottom set them in againe,

A surrop to purg melancholy
Of the Juce of pipens a pint, of the Juice of Burrage and balme
of each a pint, clarify the Juices of the hearbs, then mingle them and
add 4 ounces of Senna picked cleane and thread & halfe an ounce of
Epithymum, and lett it stand upon embers above 12 houres then
straine it and to every pint take a pound of Suger and boyle it to a
sirrope of this take 3 or 4 spoonfulls in possett drink./

An aproved medsen for the Collick and ye stone
take of a horse redish a inch in length and steepe it in half a pinte
of renish or white wine a hole night then take of ye pouder of ye pissill
of age horse as much as will cover a groate and of ye pouder of amber
as much as will cover 2 pence ye like of gett and also the same
quantity of the gall of an ox and having bruised all these together
having so done then of the wine take a spoonfull and put your
pouder into it forthwith sup it up and after it drink ye rest of your wine
thus doing for nine mornings together fasting under god it will prove well

A sirruppe for a Consumption
Take 4 greate handsfulls of janicle sable & of Avence speedwell
comfory agrimony woodbugle Betteny dasy roots & leaves bramble
toppsies venset unfitt hysoppe Colts foote ribwortes scabous of each of them

basen Take too every pinte of the juyce 1lb of fine suger set it over
ye fier when the suger disolved have redy too beaten to every pin
te pound of suger the white of one egg you must beate white of the eggs
with som of the Cold juce or with fare water wrought when you have
Rought and beaten ye eggs and suger well togather let it stand over a soft fire
stiring it Continualy with A ladle til it be scalding hott that the curd may
rise by degrees then Claryfi it as you doe suger take of the Scum
straine strayne the liquer into a basen then put it into a clean skillet
still take of ye scumme as it rieseth when it is boyled to a surops hith
shall know when it is anogh by blowing to ye spoon sirop in the spoon
for then it will Candy let it stand tell it be throughly Cold in an Earthen
basen put it up into glases keep it for your use
